# All V3 configuration keys

This reference lists 289 canonical property entries across 301 schema declarations. The same spelling can behave differently in different object variants. Aliases appear with their canonical property.

These are **resolved-config** constraints: templates are resolved before Specmatic applies this schema. Required means required inside the indicated object variant, not that the object must appear in every file. Named component/map entries are shown as `<name>`; array entries as `[]`. Runtime explanations below are separate from schema facts.

[Start with a task](/references/configuration) · [Configuration basics](/references/configuration/getting-started)

## Adapter Configuration

Adapter entries are literal hook names mapped to commands/scripts.

Schema camelCase and snake_case names are not necessarily runtime aliases. The traced contract/codec hook readers use snake_case names; no automatic name conversion was confirmed.

## Async Api Common Run Options

AsyncAPI options configure server connections, client/admin properties and schema registry access.

A nonempty per-spec override generally replaces the global option map; Async server extraction additionally falls back to global servers when the override omits them. Test reply timeout defaults to 10000 ms and readiness wait to 0.

<details id="asyncapicommonrunoptions-schemaregistry">
<summary><code>schemaRegistry</code> — Schema registry</summary>

Schema registry settings used by asynchronous tests and mocks.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.schemaRegistry`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.schemaRegistry`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi.schemaRegistry`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.schemaRegistry`

</details>

<details id="asyncapicommonrunoptions-servers">
<summary><code>servers</code> — AsyncAPI servers</summary>

Server connection settings used by asynchronous tests and mocks.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI runtime server resolution; runnable evidence is Kafka mocking in Enterprise 1.28.3. Other bindings and application testing are not established by this recipe.
- **Effect:** Selects runtime broker connections shared by the service specifications.
- **When omitted:** The DTO has an empty server list. Client resolution can fall back to a matching server in the AsyncAPI document; omission does not request an embedded broker.
- **Precedence:** A matching spec entry with servers selects that list instead. Source tracing also shows that a nonempty spec override without servers inherits this service server list: servers are a special exception to whole-map replacement. That inheritance case remains execution-pending.
- **Watch for:** Use wrapped server entries with explicit management type. Matching is by protocol, not merely list position. Do not infer that any configured protocol overrides every contract server. Enterprise 1.28.3 CLI Kafka testing also performs an initial availability check at the document endpoint before messaging. A config pointing to a healthy different endpoint does not replace that check; keep both aligned. See the separate Kafka application-test recipe.
- **Example:** A Kafka server at localhost:19092 selects that endpoint even when the contract declares localhost:19095.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/async-kafka).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.servers`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.servers`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi.servers`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.servers`

</details>

## Async Api Mock Run Options

AsyncAPI options configure server connections, client/admin properties and schema registry access.

A nonempty per-spec override generally replaces the global option map; Async server extraction additionally falls back to global servers when the override omits them. Test reply timeout defaults to 10000 ms and readiness wait to 0.

<details id="asyncapimockrunoptions-inmemorybroker">
<summary><code>inMemoryBroker</code> — In-memory broker</summary>

Deprecated optional in-memory broker settings used by asynchronous mocks.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI runtime server resolution; runnable evidence is Kafka mocking in Enterprise 1.28.3. Other bindings and application testing are not established by this recipe.
- **Effect:** Legacy embedded-broker configuration retained in models, but rejected as deprecated by the released configuration validator.
- **When omitted:** Omit the legacy block and describe the managed broker in the typed servers list.
- **Precedence:** The validator rejects a configured legacy block even when an equivalent typed server already exists; remove the block rather than supplying both forms.
- **Watch for:** Runtime legacy fallback code still exists. Schema acceptance or model presence is not proof that config validate accepts it. CLI-only legacy and log-directory precedence require separate guidance.
- **Example:** Replace inMemoryBroker with a server containing host: localhost:19092, protocol: kafka and type: in-memory.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/async-kafka).
- **deprecated:** true.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.inMemoryBroker`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.inMemoryBroker`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi.inMemoryBroker`

</details>

<details id="asyncapimockrunoptions-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specification overrides</summary>

Optional per-specification AsyncAPI overrides.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI run-option selection in the reviewed core models and Enterprise 1.28.3 consumers. Runnable mock/test recipes establish selected explicit forms; unexecuted omission/duplicate cases remain source-backed.
- **Effect:** Provides per-spec run-option overrides selected by IDs in definitions; it does not add contracts to the service.
- **When omitted:** With no list or no matching ID, service-level async options are selected. An empty override config retains the service config.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching override generally replaces the option map; servers are a special inheritance exception when the override omits the servers key. An explicit servers key selects its list instead.
- **Watch for:** Reply timing and schema registry settings are not universally field-by-field inherited. Supply required selected values explicitly. Unknown/duplicate IDs and empty lists need separately scoped execution evidence.
- **Example:** Keep definitions.specs containing spec id ping/path asyncapi.yaml; use asyncapi.specs to supply options for ping.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.specs`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs`

</details>

<details id="asyncapimockrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Mock mode</summary>

Mock branch discriminator.

- **Value:** exactly "mock".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI run-option selection in the reviewed core models and Enterprise 1.28.3 consumers. Runnable mock/test recipes establish selected explicit forms; unexecuted omission/duplicate cases remain source-backed.
- **Effect:** Selects the mock branch of AsyncAPI options in its service role. This is different from a server type selecting external versus in-memory broker management.
- **When omitted:** Inline mock service options can omit type: the model has null and uses its service role during protocol validation. Named component options require an explicit test or mock discriminator. These omission details are source/schema-backed.
- **Precedence:** The service role restricts this discriminator to mock; a reference does not make an incompatible type valid at its use site.
- **Watch for:** Do not use this field as a broker lifecycle selector. The typed server has its own type. Explicit mock/test examples are checked in separate recipes.
- **Example:** Use type: mock inside runOptions.asyncapi.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.type`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi.type`

</details>

## Async Api Mock Run Option Specification Entry

<details id="asyncapimockrunoptionspecificationentry-spec">
<summary><code>spec</code> — AsyncAPI mock run-option override</summary>

Per-specification AsyncAPI mock run-option values.

- **Value:** object + see variant constraints.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI run-option selection in the reviewed core models and Enterprise 1.28.3 consumers. Runnable mock/test recipes establish selected explicit forms; unexecuted omission/duplicate cases remain source-backed.
- **Effect:** Wraps one per-spec override object.
- **When omitted:** The wrapper is required by this schema variant. Omitting the whole specs list leaves service options in effect.
- **Precedence:** ID matching selects the wrapped object; the wrapper itself introduces no additional merge rule.
- **Watch for:** Place id and option values inside spec, not beside it. Mock overrides forbid replyTimeout and subscriberReadinessWaitTime; test overrides forbid legacy inMemoryBroker.
- **Example:** Use specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: ping, servers: [...]&#125;&#125;] as a placement fragment, not a complete config.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs[].spec`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec`

</details>

## Async Api Run Option Specification Common

AsyncAPI options configure server connections, client/admin properties and schema registry access.

A nonempty per-spec override generally replaces the global option map; Async server extraction additionally falls back to global servers when the override omits them. Test reply timeout defaults to 10000 ms and readiness wait to 0.

<details id="asyncapirunoptionspecificationcommon-id">
<summary><code>id</code> — Specification ID</summary>

Specification identifier this override targets.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI run-option selection in the reviewed core models and Enterprise 1.28.3 consumers. Runnable mock/test recipes establish selected explicit forms; unexecuted omission/duplicate cases remain source-backed.
- **Effect:** Matches an override to the spec ID declared in service definitions. The ID is user-chosen identity, not a path or operation name.
- **When omitted:** The runtime matcher receives no override when the definition has no ID. A missing override ID does not match a non-null declared ID; give both explicit matching strings.
- **Precedence:** The accessor selects the first exact matching ID. Do not rely on that as a valid duplicate-ID convention; configuration validation has separate checks.
- **Watch for:** A source path does not automatically become an ID. Missing/mismatched/duplicate-ID execution remains unverified for these AsyncAPI recipes.
- **Example:** Declare spec id ping next to path asyncapi.yaml; use id ping inside the run-option spec override.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs[].spec.id`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.id`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.id`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.id`

</details>

<summary><code>inMemoryBroker</code> — In-memory broker</summary>

Deprecated optional in-memory broker settings used by asynchronous mocks.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.
- **deprecated:** true.

**Placement**

</details>

<details id="asyncapirunoptionspecificationcommon-replytimeout">
<summary><code>replyTimeout</code> — Reply timeout</summary>

Maximum time in milliseconds to wait for an asynchronous reply.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Matching AsyncAPI per-spec test options in Enterprise 1.28.3; source-traced selection, with the CLI runner limitation established by the service-level recipe.
- **Effect:** Represents the requested reply wait in milliseconds in the async test config DTO. In the checked released CLI runner, it does not replace the message-wait timeout passed to the scenario.
- **When omitted:** The DTO reads JVM property SPECMATIC_ASYNC_REPLY_TIMEOUT using toLongOrNull, then 10000 ms. The CLI initializes that property from --reply-timeout, default 10000. However, executeTest supplies TimeoutConfig(messageWaitTimeout = 10000) explicitly for the checked CLI scenario.
- **Precedence:** Config values can overwrite the JVM property before scenario execution, but the already-created timeout argument retains its fixed 10000 ms message wait. Neither replyTimeout: 500 nor CLI --reply-timeout 500 enforced a 500 ms deadline in the independent 1500 ms reply tests.
- **Watch for:** Do not describe DTO selection as an effective CLI deadline. The limitation is confirmed for this local request/reply entry point; retry/dead-letter and direct APIs need separate checks. A matching per-spec value is resolved into the DTO but is not an execution-tested exception to this fixed argument. The common schema declaration is reused by both variants, but the per-spec mock variant explicitly forbids replyTimeout and subscriberReadinessWaitTime. AsyncMockConfigDTO does not read replyTimeout either: it is not a mock response-delay selector.
- **Example:** The demo waits 1500 ms and still passes with replyTimeout: 500. Use the recipe to understand this released boundary rather than relying on the option for a shorter CLI reply deadline.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs[].spec.replyTimeout`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.replyTimeout`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.replyTimeout`

</details>

<details id="asyncapirunoptionspecificationcommon-schemaregistry">
<summary><code>schemaRegistry</code> — Schema registry</summary>

Schema registry settings used by asynchronous tests and mocks.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs[].spec.schemaRegistry`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.schemaRegistry`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.schemaRegistry`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.schemaRegistry`

</details>

<details id="asyncapirunoptionspecificationcommon-servers">
<summary><code>servers</code> — AsyncAPI servers</summary>

Server connection settings used by asynchronous tests and mocks.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI runtime server resolution; runnable evidence is Kafka mocking in Enterprise 1.28.3. Other bindings and application testing are not established by this recipe.
- **Effect:** Selects the broker server list for the specification whose ID matches this entry.
- **When omitted:** Without a servers key, the resolver can retain the service-level server list even when other spec options are supplied. An explicit empty list selects no configured servers, allowing client fallback; those omission/empty cases are source-backed, not executed here.
- **Precedence:** An explicitly present servers list replaces the service-level list for this matching spec. Contract references resolve after selecting that list.
- **Watch for:** A $serverRef requires AsyncAPI 3 and this spec-level placement. Conflicting types for the same reference fail resolution.
- **Example:** Spec ID ping selects localhost:19093, replacing the shared localhost:19092 list.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/async-kafka).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs[].spec.servers`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.servers`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.servers`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.servers`

</details>

<details id="asyncapirunoptionspecificationcommon-subscriberreadinesswaittime">
<summary><code>subscriberReadinessWaitTime</code> — Subscriber readiness wait time</summary>

Time in milliseconds to wait for an asynchronous subscriber to become ready.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Matching AsyncAPI per-spec test options in Enterprise 1.28.3, source-traced. The single-scenario released recipe checks the service-level field, not a matching per-spec override.
- **Effect:** Requests an additional wait in milliseconds after the Kafka subscription readiness latch. It does not replace that latch or start the application subscriber.
- **When omitted:** AsyncTestConfigDTO reads JVM property SPECMATIC_ASYNC_TIME_TO_WAIT_FOR_SUBSCRIBER_TO_BE_READY with toLongOrNull, then 0 ms. The CLI sets the property from --subscriber-readiness-wait-time, default 0. TimeoutConfig also reads the property when constructed.
- **Precedence:** In the CLI runner, executeTest constructs its default TimeoutConfig before applying this scenario's config to JVM properties. Consequently the first scenario uses the CLI-initialized readiness wait, not the newly read config value. JVM properties are then overwritten from the config and may influence later scenarios; multi-scenario effects remain execution-pending.
- **Watch for:** Do not present config selection as an effective first-test wait. Negative values can reach Thread.sleep and fail; choose nonnegative milliseconds. The fixed CLI reply-timeout issue is separate. No general guarantee across protocols or programmatic runners is established here. The shared schema declaration appears in the common model, but the per-spec mock variant forbids this key and replyTimeout. AsyncMockConfigDTO does not consume it.
- **Example:** The single-scenario recipe sets subscriberReadinessWaitTime: 60000. Its first test completes before that wait. With CLI --subscriber-readiness-wait-time 3000, the test duration includes at least 3000 ms while remaining below the configured 60000 ms.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs[].spec.subscriberReadinessWaitTime`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.subscriberReadinessWaitTime`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec.subscriberReadinessWaitTime`

</details>

## Async Api Test Run Options

AsyncAPI options configure server connections, client/admin properties and schema registry access.

A nonempty per-spec override generally replaces the global option map; Async server extraction additionally falls back to global servers when the override omits them. Test reply timeout defaults to 10000 ms and readiness wait to 0.

<details id="asyncapitestrunoptions-replytimeout">
<summary><code>replyTimeout</code> — Reply timeout</summary>

Maximum time in milliseconds to wait for an asynchronous reply.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** AsyncAPI DTO and the Enterprise 1.28.3 CLI request/reply contract-test runner. Direct scenario API and other workflows are separate entry points.
- **Effect:** Represents the requested reply wait in milliseconds in the async test config DTO. In the checked released CLI runner, it does not replace the message-wait timeout passed to the scenario.
- **When omitted:** The DTO reads JVM property SPECMATIC_ASYNC_REPLY_TIMEOUT using toLongOrNull, then 10000 ms. The CLI initializes that property from --reply-timeout, default 10000. However, executeTest supplies TimeoutConfig(messageWaitTimeout = 10000) explicitly for the checked CLI scenario.
- **Precedence:** Config values can overwrite the JVM property before scenario execution, but the already-created timeout argument retains its fixed 10000 ms message wait. Neither replyTimeout: 500 nor CLI --reply-timeout 500 enforced a 500 ms deadline in the independent 1500 ms reply tests.
- **Watch for:** Do not describe DTO selection as an effective CLI deadline. The limitation is confirmed for this local request/reply entry point; retry/dead-letter and direct APIs need separate checks. A matching per-spec value is resolved into the DTO but is not an execution-tested exception to this fixed argument.
- **Example:** The demo waits 1500 ms and still passes with replyTimeout: 500. Use the recipe to understand this released boundary rather than relying on the option for a shorter CLI reply deadline.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/async-kafka-test).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.replyTimeout`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.replyTimeout`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.replyTimeout`

</details>

<details id="asyncapitestrunoptions-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specification overrides</summary>

Optional per-specification AsyncAPI overrides.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI run-option selection in the reviewed core models and Enterprise 1.28.3 consumers. Runnable mock/test recipes establish selected explicit forms; unexecuted omission/duplicate cases remain source-backed.
- **Effect:** Provides per-spec run-option overrides selected by IDs in definitions; it does not add contracts to the service.
- **When omitted:** With no list or no matching ID, service-level async options are selected. An empty override config retains the service config.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching override generally replaces the option map; servers are a special inheritance exception when the override omits the servers key. An explicit servers key selects its list instead.
- **Watch for:** Reply timing and schema registry settings are not universally field-by-field inherited. Supply required selected values explicitly. Unknown/duplicate IDs and empty lists need separately scoped execution evidence.
- **Example:** Keep definitions.specs containing spec id ping/path asyncapi.yaml; use asyncapi.specs to supply options for ping.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.specs`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs`

</details>

<details id="asyncapitestrunoptions-subscriberreadinesswaittime">
<summary><code>subscriberReadinessWaitTime</code> — Subscriber readiness wait time</summary>

Time in milliseconds to wait for an asynchronous subscriber to become ready.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 CLI AsyncAPI request/reply testing and Kafka subscriber implementation. Other integrations/bindings need their own effective-timing checks.
- **Effect:** Requests an additional wait in milliseconds after the Kafka subscription readiness latch. It does not replace that latch or start the application subscriber.
- **When omitted:** AsyncTestConfigDTO reads JVM property SPECMATIC_ASYNC_TIME_TO_WAIT_FOR_SUBSCRIBER_TO_BE_READY with toLongOrNull, then 0 ms. The CLI sets the property from --subscriber-readiness-wait-time, default 0. TimeoutConfig also reads the property when constructed.
- **Precedence:** In the CLI runner, executeTest constructs its default TimeoutConfig before applying this scenario's config to JVM properties. Consequently the first scenario uses the CLI-initialized readiness wait, not the newly read config value. JVM properties are then overwritten from the config and may influence later scenarios; multi-scenario effects remain execution-pending.
- **Watch for:** Do not present config selection as an effective first-test wait. Negative values can reach Thread.sleep and fail; choose nonnegative milliseconds. The fixed CLI reply-timeout issue is separate. No general guarantee across protocols or programmatic runners is established here.
- **Example:** The single-scenario recipe sets subscriberReadinessWaitTime: 60000. Its first test completes before that wait. With CLI --subscriber-readiness-wait-time 3000, the test duration includes at least 3000 ms while remaining below the configured 60000 ms.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/async-kafka-test).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.subscriberReadinessWaitTime`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.subscriberReadinessWaitTime`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.subscriberReadinessWaitTime`

</details>

<details id="asyncapitestrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Run mode</summary>

Test branch discriminator.

- **Value:** exactly "test".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI run-option selection in the reviewed core models and Enterprise 1.28.3 consumers. Runnable mock/test recipes establish selected explicit forms; unexecuted omission/duplicate cases remain source-backed.
- **Effect:** Selects the test branch of AsyncAPI options in its service role. This is different from a server type selecting external versus in-memory broker management.
- **When omitted:** Inline test service options can omit type: the model has null and uses its service role during protocol validation. Named component options require an explicit test or mock discriminator. These omission details are source/schema-backed.
- **Precedence:** The service role restricts this discriminator to test; a reference does not make an incompatible type valid at its use site.
- **Watch for:** Do not use this field as a broker lifecycle selector. The typed server has its own type. Explicit mock/test examples are checked in separate recipes.
- **Example:** Use type: test inside runOptions.asyncapi.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.type`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.type`

</details>

## Async Api Test Run Option Specification Entry

<details id="asyncapitestrunoptionspecificationentry-spec">
<summary><code>spec</code> — AsyncAPI test run-option override</summary>

Per-specification AsyncAPI test run-option values.

- **Value:** object + see variant constraints.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI run-option selection in the reviewed core models and Enterprise 1.28.3 consumers. Runnable mock/test recipes establish selected explicit forms; unexecuted omission/duplicate cases remain source-backed.
- **Effect:** Wraps one per-spec override object.
- **When omitted:** The wrapper is required by this schema variant. Omitting the whole specs list leaves service options in effect.
- **Precedence:** ID matching selects the wrapped object; the wrapper itself introduces no additional merge rule.
- **Watch for:** Place id and option values inside spec, not beside it. Mock overrides forbid replyTimeout and subscriberReadinessWaitTime; test overrides forbid legacy inMemoryBroker.
- **Example:** Use specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: ping, servers: [...]&#125;&#125;] as a placement fragment, not a complete config.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs[].spec`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi.specs[].spec`

</details>

<details id="asyncclientserverconfig-host">
<summary><code>host</code> — Server host</summary>

Host or connection address of the asynchronous server.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI runtime server resolution; runnable evidence is Kafka mocking in Enterprise 1.28.3. Other bindings and application testing are not established by this recipe.
- **Effect:** Sets the broker connection endpoint; for an in-memory Kafka server it also supplies the advertised endpoint and selected port.
- **When omitted:** Required for an inline server. A reference obtains the host from the named contract server instead.
- **Precedence:** A selected matching runtime server host takes precedence over the contract server host. A typed in-memory server endpoint also takes precedence over the legacy mock CLI --port, as observed with 19092 versus 19094.
- **Watch for:** For in-memory brokers, the resolver requires a parseable host and port in 1..65535. Host is not a guarantee of loopback-only binding: the checked Kafka listener binds 0.0.0.0. Log-directory behavior differs between release source and the checkout; it is not covered by this note. Enterprise 1.28.3 CLI Kafka testing also performs an initial availability check at the document endpoint before messaging. A config pointing to a healthy different endpoint does not replace that check; keep both aligned. See the separate Kafka application-test recipe.
- **Example:** Use localhost:19092 for this local Kafka mock.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/async-kafka).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.servers[].host`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.servers[].server.host`

<details id="asyncclientserverconfig-protocol">
<summary><code>protocol</code> — Server protocol</summary>

Protocol identifier used to select this asynchronous server.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI runtime server resolution; runnable evidence is Kafka mocking in Enterprise 1.28.3. Other bindings and application testing are not established by this recipe.
- **Effect:** Chooses the binding used to connect to the configured server.
- **When omitted:** Required on an inline server; a contract server reference obtains it from the contract.
- **Precedence:** Client resolution chooses the first server whose protocol matches the requested binding case-insensitively; without a match it can fall back to the contract.
- **Watch for:** The in-memory broker factory supports Kafka, JMS and AMQP. Other bindings must not be presented as embedded brokers based on this field alone; only Kafka was executed in this recipe.
- **Example:** Use kafka for the Ping/Pong contract.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/async-kafka).

<details id="asyncclientserverconfig-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Server type</summary>

Specifies whether the asynchronous server is external or in-memory.

- **Value:** "external", "in-memory".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI runtime server resolution; runnable evidence is Kafka mocking in Enterprise 1.28.3. Other bindings and application testing are not established by this recipe.
- **Effect:** Selects broker management: in-memory requests a broker managed by Specmatic; external describes an existing broker.
- **When omitted:** Configuration validation requires an explicit type. The internal DTO fallback to external is not a valid omission convention for these checked V3 configs.
- **Precedence:** For a reference, host and protocol come from the contract, while management type comes from this config. A typed in-memory endpoint wins over legacy mock CLI host/port selection.
- **Watch for:** Allowed strings are external and in-memory. External broker lifecycle, credentials and reachability require their own verification; no external broker was exercised here.
- **Example:** Use type: in-memory for the local Kafka mock.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/async-kafka).
- **default:** "external".

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.servers[].server.type`

## Async Server Reference Config

A $serverRef selects a named server in the AsyncAPI specification, not a Specmatic component.

Supported for AsyncAPI 3 per-spec server entries. Unresolved server variables and conflicting types fail validation.

<details id="asyncserverreferenceconfig-serverref">
<summary><code>$serverRef</code> — AsyncAPI server reference</summary>

Local reference to a named server in the associated AsyncAPI document.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI runtime server resolution; runnable evidence is Kafka mocking in Enterprise 1.28.3. Other bindings and application testing are not established by this recipe.
- **Effect:** Reuses a named server from the AsyncAPI document, including its host and protocol.
- **When omitted:** Choose an inline host/protocol server instead when no reference is supplied. A reference variant requires this field.
- **Precedence:** Resolves after the matching spec-level server list is selected. The config can supply type, adminCredentials and client properties alongside the reference.
- **Watch for:** Use #/servers/&lt;name&gt;, inside a wrapped server at spec level, with AsyncAPI 3. Do not combine with host/protocol. Missing destinations or unresolved host variables fail resolution. Those last two failures are source-backed, not executed by this recipe.
- **Example:** Use #/servers/localKafka to obtain localhost:19095 from asyncapi.yaml.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/async-kafka).
- **pattern:** "^#/servers/[^/]+$".

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.servers[].server.$serverRef`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.specs[].spec.servers[].server.$serverRef`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi.servers[].server.$serverRef`

## Backward Compatibility Settings

Backward-compatibility settings select the baseline branch/path, repository and strictness.

These are consumed by compatibility-check commands, not ordinary test target routing. Paths and branch availability still require runtime checks.

<details id="backwardcompatibilitysettings-basebranch">
<summary><code>baseBranch</code> — Base branch</summary>

Branch used as the baseline for compatibility comparison.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Resolved global V3 backward-compatibility settings, including a selected reusable settings component. Local OpenAPI CLI effects are checked in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; other protocols/integrations require their own applicability checks.
- **Effect:** Selects the Git baseline compared with changed OpenAPI contracts. It does not select a Git specification source branch for test/mock runs.
- **When omitted:** CLI --base-branch, then the configured value, then SystemGit.currentRemoteBranch: the checked-in consumer uses the current branch upstream if one is configured, otherwise the current branch. In the checked demo with no upstream, omission compares change with itself and skips all changed specs.
- **Precedence:** A non-null CLI --base-branch wins over the resolved global backwardCompatibility.baseBranch. An unreferenced component settings entry has no effect.
- **Watch for:** Use an existing explicit baseline. A missing branch is an error; an empty string is not the same as omission and is passed to Git. A successful skipped check is not a compatibility verdict. Tracking-upstream and remote/fetch layouts remain separate from the checked local no-upstream demo.
- **Example:** Set baseBranch: main in specmatic.settings.backwardCompatibility to compare your current contract change with the committed main version.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/compatibility).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.backwardCompatibility.baseBranch`
- `specmatic.settings.backwardCompatibility.baseBranch`

</details>

<details id="backwardcompatibilitysettings-repodirectory">
<summary><code>repoDirectory</code> — Repository directory</summary>

Local repository directory used for compatibility comparison.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Resolved global V3 backward-compatibility settings, including a selected reusable settings component. Local OpenAPI CLI effects are checked in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; other protocols/integrations require their own applicability checks.
- **Effect:** Selects the local Git repository inspected and temporarily checked out for compatibility comparison. It does not clone or fetch a configured contract source.
- **When omitted:** CLI --repo-dir, then configured repoDirectory, then the command working directory (.). The checked relative contracts-demo value is process-relative; omission while reading a config from the parent still uses the current demo directory.
- **Precedence:** A non-null CLI --repo-dir wins over the resolved global repoDirectory. The checked CLI value overrides a configured nonexistent directory.
- **Watch for:** The path must lead to a usable local Git repository. ConfigPathMapper can transform paths for consumers that invoke it; that helper does not establish config-relative resolution for this CLI. Comparisons can switch branches and stash/pop changes. Checked scenarios restore the current branch and clean tracked files; interrupted/conflicting restoration needs separate checks.
- **Example:** With specmatic.yaml beside contracts-demo, set repoDirectory: contracts-demo and run from that parent directory.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/compatibility).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.backwardCompatibility.repoDirectory`
- `specmatic.settings.backwardCompatibility.repoDirectory`

</details>

<details id="backwardcompatibilitysettings-strictmode">
<summary><code>strictMode</code> — Strict compatibility mode</summary>

Apply strict compatibility rules when comparing repository states.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Resolved global V3 backward-compatibility settings, including a selected reusable settings component. Local OpenAPI CLI effects are checked in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; other protocols/integrations require their own applicability checks.
- **Effect:** Controls the verdict from a usage-aware backward-compatibility hook. In the Enterprise Insights hook, true keeps a breaking contract failed even if Insights reports no consumers; false can accept that no-consumer result.
- **When omitted:** A non-null CLI --strict value wins, then configured strictMode, then false. Explicit false remains a configured boolean; it is not omission.
- **Precedence:** The shared command passes the selected CLI/config/default boolean to a registered hook. With no hook, the normal compatibility result is returned independently of strictness. The Enterprise hook also fails unknown/error consumer-impact results rather than treating them as confirmed absence of usage.
- **Watch for:** This is not OpenAPI strict parsing, a schema-validation toggle or a way to permit breaking changes in the basic OSS check. The local demo has no origin and cannot initiate an Insights consumer request; strict-versus-lenient verdict effects with a real usage service remain execution-pending. Schema type is boolean: strings, numbers and explicit null are not its declared values.
- **Example:** Use strictMode: true when breaking contracts must fail even after Insights confirms no consumers.

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.backwardCompatibility.strictMode`
- `specmatic.settings.backwardCompatibility.strictMode`

</details>

<details id="backwardcompatibilitysettings-targetpath">
<summary><code>targetPath</code> — Compatibility target path</summary>

Path whose compatibility is evaluated.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Resolved global V3 backward-compatibility settings, including a selected reusable settings component. Local OpenAPI CLI effects are checked in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; other protocols/integrations require their own applicability checks.
- **Effect:** Filters changed Git file paths before the compatibility check. It is not the service-definition specs list and does not load an unchanged file solely because its path is named.
- **When omitted:** CLI --target-path, then configured targetPath, then an empty string: no narrower path filter. The demo then checks both changed contracts and fails because one is breaking.
- **Precedence:** A non-null CLI --target-path wins over the resolved global targetPath. CLI other.yaml selects the independently breaking contract instead of configured contract.yaml.
- **Watch for:** The reviewed consumer uses path-string contains after converting the target to a Path string. Treat this as a substring filter, not a glob or an exact-file guarantee; repeated names can match multiple paths. Empty string is the unfiltered fallback. Deleted/renamed files, external references and platform-specific path matching need separate checks.
- **Example:** Set targetPath: contract.yaml to narrow the demo to its compatible contract; use a distinguishing path in a larger repository.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/compatibility).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.backwardCompatibility.targetPath`
- `specmatic.settings.backwardCompatibility.targetPath`

</details>

## Cert Configuration

Certificates configure TLS key material and, for mock listeners, optional incoming mutual TLS.

Select a keystore file or directory. Schema validation rejects both together, although plain binding prefers file. Store/key passwords default to forgotten and aliases default to specmatic plus a caller-specific suffix.

<details id="certconfiguration-keystore">
<summary><code>keyStore</code> — Key store</summary>

Identity store containing the certificate and private key.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI mock CLI and contract-test client in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. SOAP/proxy and other consumers need separate execution checks.
- **Effect:** Selects an existing store or directory mode. Core mock converts it to server key material; tests convert it to outgoing client key material.
- **When omitted:** No store means no configured key material. An otherwise present cert object does not alone enable HTTPS. CLI wildcard mock credentials can be used if no configured registry key material exists.
- **Precedence:** Source-traced certificate merge is field-dependent: explicit config keyStorePassword and mtlsEnabled are retained; nested file/directory, alias and private-key password accept non-null CLI overrides. Do not generalize a CLI-wins rule. The recipe demonstrates that a correct CLI store password does not replace an explicit wrong config store password.
- **Watch for:** Getters canonicalize file/directory paths; entry-point path mapping can precede that. The recipe keeps config and stores in one working directory. Relocated config/source-path layouts remain unexecuted. Directory-mode behaviors differ between mock generation and test loading.
- **Example:** Use file certificates/server.pfx for the mock and certificates/client.pfx for tests.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/https).

**Placement**

- `components.certificates.<name>.keyStore`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.cert.keyStore`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.cert.keyStore`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore`
- `proxies[].proxy.cert.keyStore`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore`

</details>

<details id="certconfiguration-keystorepassword">
<summary><code>keyStorePassword</code> — Key-store password</summary>

Password opening the identity store; separate from the private-key password.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI mock CLI and contract-test client in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. SOAP/proxy and other consumers need separate execution checks.
- **Effect:** Passed to KeyStore.load. An incorrect resolved password prevents startup/client setup.
- **When omitted:** Defaults to the literal forgotten in HttpsConfiguration; an existing store must actually use that password or loading fails.
- **Precedence:** Source-traced certificate merge is field-dependent: explicit config keyStorePassword and mtlsEnabled are retained; nested file/directory, alias and private-key password accept non-null CLI overrides. Do not generalize a CLI-wins rule. The recipe demonstrates that a correct CLI store password does not replace an explicit wrong config store password.
- **Watch for:** Empty strings are supplied as authored; this is not a password discovery mechanism. Use supported config templates and avoid secret defaults in shareable files.
- **Example:** The demo uses &#123;TLS_PASSWORD:docs-demo-pass&#125; for the same public PKCS12 store/key password.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/https).

**Placement**

- `components.certificates.<name>.keyStorePassword`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.cert.keyStorePassword`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.cert.keyStorePassword`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStorePassword`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStorePassword`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStorePassword`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStorePassword`
- `proxies[].proxy.cert.keyStorePassword`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStorePassword`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStorePassword`

</details>

<details id="certconfiguration-mtlsenabled">
<summary><code>mtlsEnabled</code> — Enable mutual TLS</summary>

Require a client certificate on incoming core mock TLS connections.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI mock CLI and contract-test client in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. SOAP/proxy and other consumers need separate execution checks.
- **Effect:** True selects the incoming mTLS listener path. Core test client key selection is based on key material, not this flag.
- **When omitted:** Omitted/null/false means false for incoming mTLS. True without server key material fails mock startup in reviewed source.
- **Precedence:** Source-traced certificate merge is field-dependent: explicit config keyStorePassword and mtlsEnabled are retained; nested file/directory, alias and private-key password accept non-null CLI overrides. Do not generalize a CLI-wins rule. The recipe demonstrates that a correct CLI store password does not replace an explicit wrong config store password.
- **Watch for:** Mock incoming mTLS requires a client certificate but uses an insecure trust manager; the independent self-signed client succeeds. Core test HTTPS uses TrustAllStrategy and NoopHostnameVerifier in the reviewed source. These settings do not establish production certificate trust or hostname validation. HTTP/2 negotiation failed in the checked curl/mTLS setup; the working published request explicitly uses HTTP/1.1.
- **Example:** Add mtlsEnabled true to mock cert; tests need client key material without this flag.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/https).

**Placement**

- `components.certificates.<name>.mtlsEnabled`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.cert.mtlsEnabled`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.cert.mtlsEnabled`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.cert.mtlsEnabled`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.cert.mtlsEnabled`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.cert.mtlsEnabled`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.mtlsEnabled`
- `proxies[].proxy.cert.mtlsEnabled`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.cert.mtlsEnabled`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.mtlsEnabled`

</details>

## Component Async Api Run Options

<details id="componentasyncapirunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Run mode</summary>

Selects the concrete AsyncAPI test or mock option branch.

- **Value:** "test", "mock".
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 AsyncAPI run-option selection in the reviewed core models and Enterprise 1.28.3 consumers. Runnable mock/test recipes establish selected explicit forms; unexecuted omission/duplicate cases remain source-backed.
- **Effect:** Selects test versus mock concrete AsyncAPI options in a reusable named component.
- **When omitted:** Required in this component schema; unlike a role-specific inline service, the component has no enclosing service role to choose its branch.
- **Precedence:** When referenced, the selected component must fit the test/mock use site. Broker management still comes from each server.type.
- **Watch for:** A component source/service reference is separate from an AsyncAPI document $serverRef. Keep these two reference namespaces distinct.
- **Example:** The checked Kafka mock component uses asyncapi.type: mock, then dependencies references its service.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi.type`

</details>

## Component Graphql Sdl Run Options

<details id="componentgraphqlsdlrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Run mode</summary>

Selects the concrete GraphQL SDL test or mock option branch.

- **Value:** "test", "mock".
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 GraphQL HTTP mock/test consumers in Enterprise 1.28.3. Shared schema acceptance does not establish OSS GraphQL runtime support. A reusable component affects execution only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Chooses test or mock for a reusable GraphQL run-options component.
- **When omitted:** The component union requires a type discriminator; the typed inline selected-service models can omit it.
- **Precedence:** A referenced component must agree with the service role. Declaring it does not start an API or a mock.
- **Watch for:** Component mode selection is source/schema-backed; the linked local recipe exercises inline modes, not component conversion.
- **Example:** components.runOptions.catalogMock.graphqlsdl.type: mock

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.graphqlsdl.type`

</details>

## Component Open Api Run Options

<details id="componentopenapirunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Run mode</summary>

Selects the concrete OpenAPI test or mock option branch.

- **Value:** "test", "mock", "stateful-mock".
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Chooses the concrete test/mock shape when an OpenAPI run-option component is not already typed by a service role.
- **When omitted:** Required in this component schema; directly typed test/mock service options have different omission rules.
- **Precedence:** test, mock and stateful-mock choose their corresponding branches. The consuming service still has to accept the chosen shape.
- **Watch for:** This is a discriminator, not a command. Do not mix test-only and mock-only properties because both appear in the catalog.
- **Example:** Give a reusable OpenAPI component type: mock before selecting it from a dependency service.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/component-references).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.type`

</details>

## Component Protobuf Run Options

<details id="componentprotobufrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Run mode</summary>

Selects the concrete Protobuf test or mock option branch.

- **Value:** "test", "mock".
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Selects the test or mock variant for reusable protobuf run options. Use a matching role when referring to it from a service.
- **When omitted:** A reusable component requires an explicit type: test or mock in the V3 schema. Inline role-specific run options have a different omission rule.
- **Precedence:** The selected component and its use site must have compatible roles; reference siblings are a separate replacement step.
- **Watch for:** A schema-defined component is not an executed service until selected. The local gRPC recipe uses inline run options, so it does not establish every reusable-component conversion.
- **Example:** Set components.runOptions.greeterTest.protobuf.type to test and select it from the tested service.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.type`

</details>

## Component Run Options

<details id="componentrunoptions-asyncapi">
<summary><code>asyncapi</code> — AsyncAPI run options</summary>

Directly typed AsyncAPI component options.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Supplies AsyncAPI execution options for the referencing test/mock service context.
- **When omitted:** No explicit options for this protocol are supplied by this field. Validation can construct default option objects, but that does not establish that the runner can execute without its required endpoint/broker inputs.
- **Precedence:** The service definition selects specs; the consumer selects this protocol's option branch. A service runOptions reference is converted for the consuming role. Matching per-spec overrides follow that protocol's rules.
- **Watch for:** This does not select a contract file or grant edition support. Different protocol branches may coexist for appropriate specs; do not combine test-only and mock-only fields in one branch. Follow the protocol guide for executable release/edition support.
- **Example:** Nest asyncapi under runOptions for the selected service, and supply the appropriate variant's fields.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/async-kafka).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.asyncapi`

</details>

## Components

Components hold reusable definitions. Select a service through systemUnderTest or dependencies to use its specs for testing or mocking.

Component services and run options are interpreted in their use-site context. A component reused in incompatible test/mock contexts can fail typed conversion.

<details id="components-adapters">
<summary><code>adapters</code> — Adapter components</summary>

Named adapter hook configurations. Only the supported hook names are valid.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Stores named adapter definitions for supported hooks and processors.
- **When omitted:** No reusable adapters are declared; selected data.adapters may still be inline.
- **Precedence:** Reference the adapter from the applicable data.adapters field. Selection and execution are separate; the invoking consumer determines supported hooks.
- **Watch for:** Do not infer hook execution, order or process behavior merely from a schema-valid component. Follow the adapter guide and the specific command.
- **Example:** Use data.adapters: &#123;$ref: "#/components/adapters/booksHooks"&#125; where the data field is supported.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/component-references).

**Placement**

- `components.adapters`

</details>

<details id="components-certificates">
<summary><code>certificates</code> — Certificate components</summary>

Named reusable TLS identity configurations.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI mock CLI and contract-test client in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. SOAP/proxy and other consumers need separate execution checks.
- **Effect:** Stores certificate configurations resolved by cert references at consuming use sites; declaring a component alone starts no listener.
- **When omitted:** Optional; inline cert configuration is supported.
- **Precedence:** Reference resolution and override behavior follow the consuming cert resolver; merely naming a component does not attach it to a service.
- **Watch for:** The recipe executes one named mock certificate reference. Test references, sibling overrides, multiple identities and protocol-specific consumers need separate checks.
- **Example:** Define components.certificates.demoTls and use cert $ref #/components/certificates/demoTls.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/https).

**Placement**

- `components.certificates`

</details>

<details id="components-dictionaries">
<summary><code>dictionaries</code> — Dictionary components</summary>

Named dictionaries available to services and specifications.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Stores named dictionaries whose path can be selected for request/response value generation.
- **When omitted:** No reusable dictionaries are declared; a selected data.dictionary may still be inline.
- **Precedence:** Reference the chosen dictionary from a service or dependency data.dictionary. Dictionary selection then follows that consumer's precedence.
- **Watch for:** The name itself is not a file path, and declaring the dictionary does not make every service use it. A dictionary supplies values, not a proof of application correctness.
- **Example:** Select #/components/dictionaries/books from data.dictionary; the dictionary object supplies path.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `components.dictionaries`

</details>

<details id="components-examples">
<summary><code>examples</code> — Example components</summary>

Named example-directory collections.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Holds reusable common directory groups and test/mock lists.
- **When omitted:** No reusable example collections are declared. This does not disable inline examples or other selected directories.
- **Precedence:** Select these groups/lists explicitly from data.examples using the supported refs. Declaring commonExamples alone does not make it global input.
- **Watch for:** This collection has the defined commonExamples/testExamples/mockExamples fields; it is not an arbitrary map of named example groups.
- **Example:** Reference #/components/examples/mockExamples as a whole list or #/components/examples/commonExamples as a list item.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `components.examples`

</details>

<details id="components-runoptions">
<summary><code>runOptions</code> — Run-option components</summary>

Named directly typed protocol run-option components. These values require their protocol branch discriminator.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Stores named, protocol-keyed execution options for reuse.
- **When omitted:** No reusable option groups are declared; service options can still be inline.
- **Precedence:** Reference the group at a service runOptions field. The consumer selects test/mock context; matching per-spec IDs are handled inside its protocol options.
- **Watch for:** A runOptions component alone does not select a contract. Supply the appropriate type and required fields for the consuming protocol/role.
- **Example:** Select #/components/runOptions/localMock from a mock service runOptions field.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions`

</details>

<details id="components-services">
<summary><code>services</code> — Service components</summary>

Named reusable services whose test/mock context is selected by the consumer.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Stores named reusable services. Each declaration supplies definitions plus optional description, data, settings and run options.
- **When omitted:** No reusable services exist; selected services can still be written inline.
- **Precedence:** Reference a named service from systemUnderTest.service or dependencies.services[].service. The use site determines test/mock conversion.
- **Watch for:** A declaration does not start a service or select it for a command. Names are your map keys, not spec IDs.
- **Example:** Declare booksBackend here, then select #/components/services/booksBackend from a dependency.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/component-references).

**Placement**

- `components.services`

</details>

<details id="components-settings">
<summary><code>settings</code> — Settings components</summary>

Named settings. The concrete shape is selected from global, test, mock, proxy, or contextual settings.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Stores named settings objects for a supported global or role-specific use site.
- **When omitted:** No named settings are declared; global and service settings can still be inline.
- **Precedence:** Use a concrete object with general/test/mock/proxy/backwardCompatibility for global settings; use the expected direct fields or the appropriate nested target for a service.
- **Watch for:** A name does not create an environment profile or activate settings. Existing refs must still convert to the expected type; global wrappers are not interchangeable with direct service fields.
- **Example:** A global reference can target #/components/settings/common, while a test-only reference can target its /test child.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/component-references).

**Placement**

- `components.settings`

</details>

<details id="components-sources">
<summary><code>sources</code> — Source components</summary>

Named source components. A source is selected by exactly one of git, filesystem, or web.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Declares named reusable source objects. Definitions select one with a component reference; a declaration is not a list of specs or an instruction to start a service.
- **When omitted:** No reusable source map is required when definitions use inline sources. With no selection, a declaration does not contribute specification paths to an operation.
- **Precedence:** A definition selects its source independently of other definitions. The source name identifies a component; it does not set repository name, cache directory or runtime service ID.
- **Watch for:** Each map value still needs exactly one valid provider. Source credentials are selected from operation sources, not automatically from every declared component. Literal component traversal constrains usable names.
- **Example:** Declare components.sources.orderContracts with a git object, then reference #/components/sources/orderContracts inside a definition.

**Placement**

- `components.sources`

</details>

## Component Wsdl Run Options

<details id="componentwsdlrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Run mode</summary>

Selects the concrete WSDL test or mock option branch.

- **Value:** "test", "mock".
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Selects the test or mock subtype for a reusable WSDL run-options component.
- **When omitted:** Unlike an inline selected-service variant, the component WSDL union requires type to choose its subtype.
- **Precedence:** The service role must agree with the referenced run-options type; a component does not start a service by itself.
- **Watch for:** Use test for a system under test and mock for dependencies. The component discriminator requirement is schema/model-backed; the released recipe exercises inline modes, not component conversion.
- **Example:** components.runOptions.soapTest.wsdl.type: test

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.type`

</details>

## Concrete Settings

<details id="concretesettings-backwardcompatibility">
<summary><code>backwardCompatibility</code> — Backward compatibility settings</summary>

Settings for compatibility comparisons.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Supplies the configured Git baseline and specification scope for applicable compatibility checks.
- **When omitted:** The config accessor returns no configured compatibility object. The compatibility command's own inputs/defaults still apply.
- **Precedence:** This object supplies compatibility settings separately from test/mock run options; explicit command inputs follow the compatibility command's rules.
- **Watch for:** A command banner or successful skip is not proof that a comparison ran. Follow the guide's selected-spec, baseline and outcome checks.
- **Example:** Use specmatic.settings.backwardCompatibility with the checked Git-baseline recipe; it does not configure a test server.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/compatibility).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.backwardCompatibility`
- `specmatic.settings.backwardCompatibility`

</details>

<details id="concretesettings-general">
<summary><code>general</code> — General settings</summary>

Settings shared across Specmatic operations.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Groups general behavior such as logging, inline-example handling, telemetry and example-directory templates.
- **When omitted:** No general object is selected; each accessor decides its default and environment/property fallback.
- **Precedence:** These are global controls. Service test/mock settings do not create a second general settings scope.
- **Watch for:** Settings can affect discovery or formatting rather than the API under test. Consult the individual field for its actual consumer and precedence.
- **Example:** Use general.logging.text for text-log output under specmatic.settings.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/how-config-is-used).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general`
- `specmatic.settings.general`

</details>

<details id="concretesettings-mock">
<summary><code>mock</code> — Mock settings</summary>

Settings for running mocked dependencies.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Provides shared defaults for configured dependency mocks.
- **When omitted:** Core accessors use default MockSettings values, with dependency/service and non-file inputs still applicable.
- **Precedence:** Dependency mock settings merge over global mock settings; service fields can override the result. Explicit false remains an override.
- **Watch for:** A configured default does not start a mock. Some mock lifecycle settings are consumed globally rather than per service.
- **Example:** Use specmatic.settings.mock.strictMode: true for the shared default.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/mock-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.mock`
- `specmatic.settings.mock`

</details>

<details id="concretesettings-proxy">
<summary><code>proxy</code> — Proxy settings</summary>

Settings controlling proxy recording and header handling.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Groups recording controls used by the core HTTP proxy consumer.
- **When omitted:** No proxy settings are supplied; the proxy command applies its own defaults.
- **Precedence:** The proxy command can apply explicit command options. These controls do not replace the listener/target fields in proxies[].proxy.
- **Watch for:** ignoreHeaders has a documented released redaction defect; recordRequests disables saved artifacts, not console traffic logs. Use the tested proxy guide.
- **Example:** Use specmatic.settings.proxy.recordRequests: false to disable saved recordings.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/proxy).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.proxy`
- `specmatic.settings.proxy`

</details>

<details id="concretesettings-test">
<summary><code>test</code> — Test settings</summary>

Settings for executing tests against the system under test.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Provides shared test defaults such as timeout, strictness and resiliency settings.
- **When omitted:** Core test settings use a default TestSettings object unless a selected service contributes settings.
- **Precedence:** Selected service test fields merge over global test fields. Command-staged values and environment/property fallbacks remain field-specific.
- **Watch for:** This does not select specs or a test target. A service settings object uses direct fields without this global test wrapper.
- **Example:** Use specmatic.settings.test.timeoutInMilliseconds: 2000 and override that field on a selected service when needed.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/test-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.test`
- `specmatic.settings.test`

</details>

## Context Service

Components hold reusable definitions. Select a service through systemUnderTest or dependencies to use its specs for testing or mocking.

Component services and run options are interpreted in their use-site context. A component reused in incompatible test/mock contexts can fail typed conversion.

<details id="contextservice-data">
<summary><code>data</code> — Service data</summary>

Optional examples, dictionary, and adapter data for the service.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Stores example, dictionary and adapter selections in a reusable service. Its test or mock use site determines which consumers read them.
- **When omitted:** No data contribution is declared by this component. Other data sources can still apply at its selected use site.
- **Precedence:** A data field beside a service $ref replaces the whole referenced data field. After conversion, test and mock selection rules differ: core mocks can combine dependency/service example directories; core test data is selected from the tested service.
- **Watch for:** A reusable declaration does not apply its data to every service. Adapter execution and protocol-specific data behavior need their own consumer evidence.
- **Example:** Use data.examples for selected directory groups or refs, and data.dictionary for a dictionary object or ref.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.data`

</details>

<details id="contextservice-definitions">
<summary><code>definitions</code> — Service definitions</summary>

Specifications and their sources for this service.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Lists the sources and specs for this reusable service. Each item has a definition wrapper.
- **When omitted:** Required in the resolved service shape. An empty list contributes no specification entries; it does not discover arbitrary files.
- **Precedence:** Inline definitions beside a service reference replace the referenced definitions list. Multiple definitions otherwise contribute their explicitly selected specs.
- **Watch for:** Source directory/repository and spec paths work together. Follow the path-resolution guide; a path-mapping helper alone does not prove a particular command uses config-relative paths.
- **Example:** Use definitions: [&#123;definition: &#123;source: &#123;filesystem: &#123;directory: .&#125;&#125;, specs: [books.yaml]&#125;&#125;].

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/component-references).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions`

</details>

<details id="contextservice-description">
<summary><code>description</code> — Service description</summary>
