Optional human-readable description of the service.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Adds a human-readable description to this reusable service.
- **When omitted:** Optional; no description is supplied. This field does not select a target, spec or mock.
- **Precedence:** An inline description beside a service reference replaces the component description.
- **Watch for:** Treat this as descriptive metadata. It is not a service ID, a spec ID or a guarantee that a runner will display the text.
- **Example:** Use description: Books backend; choose spec IDs separately in the definition spec entries.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/component-references).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.description`

</details>

<details id="contextservice-runoptions">
<summary><code>runOptions</code> — Contextual run options</summary>

Optional test-or-mock run options selected by the consuming context.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Selects protocol-specific execution options for this reusable service, such as an OpenAPI test target or mock listener.
- **When omitted:** No service-level options are supplied. Core HTTP consumers can use their defaults or CLI inputs; other protocols may require explicit options.
- **Precedence:** A referenced options object is converted for the consuming test/mock role. A matching per-spec ID can select overrides with protocol-specific fallback rules.
- **Watch for:** These options do not select specs: definitions does that. Inline runOptions beside a service reference replaces the whole referenced runOptions field; do not assume recursive merging.
- **Example:** For a test service use runOptions.openapi.type: test and baseUrl; for a dependency use type: mock and port.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.runOptions`

</details>

<details id="contextservice-settings">
<summary><code>settings</code> — Contextual settings</summary>

Optional test-or-mock settings selected by the consuming context.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Applies test or mock behavior settings to this reusable service; reusable component settings acquire their role at the use site.
- **When omitted:** No service override is supplied. Test accessors fall back to global test defaults; mock accessors can also inherit dependencies.settings.
- **Precedence:** Resolve any service reference and its shallow overrides first. Then the selected service settings merge field by field with the applicable wider settings.
- **Watch for:** The service object takes role-specific settings directly, such as timeoutInMilliseconds for a test; it does not use the global test/mock wrapper. Runtime precedence still depends on the command and individual field.
- **Example:** Put settings: &#123;timeoutInMilliseconds: 2000&#125; on a tested service, or settings: &#123;strictMode: true&#125; on a mock dependency.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/component-references).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings`

</details>

## Data Definition

Data supplies external examples, dictionaries and adapters at applicable service/dependency use sites.

Test examples combine service directories with environment/property additions. Mock examples also include dependency-wide directories. Conventional example-directory templates provide discovery beyond explicit lists.

<details id="datadefinition-adapters">
<summary><code>adapters</code> — Service adapters</summary>

Adapter hooks used by this service, inline or through a named reference.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.data.adapters`
- `dependencies.data.adapters`
- `dependencies.services[].service.data.adapters`
- `systemUnderTest.service.data.adapters`

</details>

<details id="datadefinition-dictionary">
<summary><code>dictionary</code> — Service dictionary</summary>

Dictionary used by this service, inline or through a named reference.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core V3 data resolution and core OpenAPI consumers in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners require their own review.
- **Effect:** Selects a dictionary used by core OpenAPI request/response generation. The value is an inline &#123;path: ...&#125; object or a reference to a named dictionary.
- **When omitted:** Tests fall back to SPECMATIC_STUB_DICTIONARY. Mocks fall back first to dependency-wide data.dictionary and then that variable. With no selected path, core OpenAPI parsing can discover a dictionary beside the spec.
- **Precedence:** Tests use the tested-service dictionary before SPECMATIC_STUB_DICTIONARY. Mocks use the matching-service dictionary, then dependency-wide dictionary, then the variable. The variable uses environment before JVM property. Dictionary paths select one file; dictionaries are not merged by these accessors.
- **Watch for:** The variable has STUB in its name but also supplies the core test fallback. The older generic getDictionary accessor reads only the variable; core test/mock execution subsequently applies their selected path through Feature.useDictionary. Do not assume every caller uses the same accessor.
- **Example:** Put a shared mock dictionary under dependencies.data.dictionary, then give one dependency service its own data.dictionary to select a different file.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/data-recipes).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.data.dictionary`
- `dependencies.data.dictionary`
- `dependencies.services[].service.data.dictionary`
- `systemUnderTest.service.data.dictionary`

</details>

<details id="datadefinition-examples">
<summary><code>examples</code> — Service examples</summary>

Examples used by this service, inline or through a named reference.

- **Value:** object,array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core V3 data resolution and core OpenAPI consumers in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, with the separate Enterprise 1.28.3 GraphQL mock/test consumer exception stated below. Other protocol runners require their own review.
- **Effect:** Selects external example-directory groups for this service or for all dependencies. Each group can be inline or referenced; the whole list can also be referenced.
- **When omitted:** No configured directories are contributed by an omitted examples value. Core consumers can still discover the conventional spec-name_examples directory and read EXAMPLE_DIRECTORIES; omission does not disable external examples. Whether an additional directory is actually loaded depends on the consumer branch.
- **Precedence:** The per-spec data accessors behave as follows: for a matching test spec, service directories are followed by EXAMPLE_DIRECTORIES. For a matching mock spec, service directories are followed by dependency-wide directories, then EXAMPLE_DIRECTORIES. The fallback variable is read from the environment before the JVM property, split on commas and trimmed. These lists combine; a service list does not replace the dependency list.
- **Watch for:** An empty service list does not clear dependency or environment directories. A component declaration alone does not select examples. List order is not a promise that the first example wins an ambiguous mock request. In released CLI mock loading, configured/implicit examples can bypass the fallback to additional --examples or EXAMPLE_DIRECTORIES directories. The per-spec accessor list is not proof of loaded mock examples. Include additional directories in data.examples when combining them with configured examples. For Enterprise 1.28.3 GraphQL, configured service directories supply mock examples, but the test runner still reads the colocated SDL-name_examples directory. The Configure GraphQL with SDL recipe verifies that custom mock data affects responses while the test keeps its colocated example request; do not transfer the core accessor combination rules to that test consumer. Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC validate --spec-file combines configured directories with the colocated examples instead of replacing them: the checked setup validates both hello.json and extra-examples/bad.json, detects its numeric name and succeeds after correction. This does not establish config-wide discovery, every path base or shared/global validation.
- **Example:** Reference the whole test list with examples: &#123;$ref: "#/components/examples/testExamples"&#125;, or use examples: [&#123;directories: [./examples]&#125;].

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/data-recipes).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.data.examples`
- `dependencies.data.examples`
- `dependencies.services[].service.data.examples`
- `systemUnderTest.service.data.examples`

</details>

## Definition Entry

<details id="definitionentry-definition">
<summary><code>definition</code> — Definition value</summary>

Named definition value containing its source and specification list.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Wraps one source and the specification paths selected from that source. A service can contain several such definitions.
- **When omitted:** Required for each definitions list item. Its value needs the required source/specs fields.
- **Precedence:** Each definition resolves its own source, then its specs. Spec IDs belong to individual spec entries and are matched by per-spec run options.
- **Watch for:** Do not place source and specs directly beside the list dash, or use this wrapper as an operation-level definition inside an API document.
- **Example:** Under definitions, use - definition: with source and specs nested below it.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition`

</details>

## Definition Value

A definition binds a source to specification paths. Named spec IDs connect declarations to per-spec run-option overrides.

Specs can be strings or wrapped spec objects. IDs are matching identifiers, not a global CLI alias registry. The first matching ID override is selected.

<details id="definitionvalue-source">
<summary><code>source</code> — Definition source</summary>

Inline source provider or reference to a named source component.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Selects the provider for the specs in this definition: filesystem, Git or web. A local component reference can reuse the same provider configuration.
- **When omitted:** The definition requires source; there is no implicit filesystem source inserted for a missing field. An empty source object satisfies the baseline schema but fails model binding.
- **Precedence:** Choose exactly one non-null provider. A source ref is converted to SourceV3; sibling replacement follows the reference rules rather than combining providers.
- **Watch for:** Spec paths depend on the selected provider. A found component is insufficient if it has the wrong shape. Browser/source checks are separate from fetching files and serving responses.
- **Example:** Use source: &#123;filesystem: &#123;directory: contracts&#125;&#125; and specs: [books.yaml], or reference a named source from components.sources.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/contract-management).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source`

</details>

<details id="definitionvalue-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specifications</summary>

Specifications loaded from the selected source.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 specification declaration and core OpenAPI endpoint selection. Released execution evidence covers mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; test targets and separate protocols retain their own consumer rules.
- **Effect:** Selects specification files from this definition’s source. Entries can be paths or wrapped spec objects with identity and additional supported metadata.
- **When omitted:** The resolved object variant requires specs; an empty list selects no files in this definition. Another definition can still supply files.
- **Precedence:** Declarations select files; runOptions.&lt;protocol&gt;.specs supplies matching overrides and does not append files. Each declared spec is converted to a source entry with its selected endpoint.
- **Watch for:** String paths have no ID. Path interpretation depends on the source and consumer; this recipe checks local paths while running beside the config. Other path bases and protocol-specific fields need their own guidance.
- **Example:** Declare books.yaml and authors.yaml in one definition; a matching authors override gives it a separate port.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.specs`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.specs`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.specs`

</details>

## Dependencies Definition

Dependency services provide specifications and settings for mocks.

Mock settings use service, then dependency-wide, then global values. Startup/hot-reload/restart accessors use dependency/global settings rather than a single service’s value.

<details id="dependenciesdefinition-data">
<summary><code>data</code> — Dependency data</summary>

Optional examples, dictionary, and adapter data for dependencies.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Selects shared mock examples, a dictionary and adapters for dependency services.
- **When omitted:** No shared dependency data is selected. A service can still supply data, and other discovery inputs can apply.
- **Precedence:** Core mock directories combine service examples before shared dependency examples. A service dictionary takes precedence over a dependency dictionary. Adapter hook selection has its own consumers.
- **Watch for:** This is not global test data. Do not infer that every test/protocol consumes the dependency data or that common examples in components are automatically selected.
- **Example:** Use dependencies.data.examples with directory groups and dependencies.data.dictionary with a selected dictionary ref.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `dependencies.data`

</details>

<details id="dependenciesdefinition-services">
<summary><code>services</code> — Mock dependency services</summary>

Mock dependency entries. The array may be empty; each item must contain a service wrapper.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Lists the wrapped services selected for mocking; different services can select different specs and run options.
- **When omitted:** Required within dependencies, but an empty list is structurally allowed and selects no services from that list.
- **Precedence:** Each list item must contain service. Core matching finds the first configured service containing a spec file; avoid duplicate ownership when the settings should differ.
- **Watch for:** This is a list, not a map of service names. Multiple listeners and ports depend on the selected protocol runner.
- **Example:** Use dependencies.services: [&#123;service: &#123;$ref: "#/components/services/booksBackend"&#125;&#125;].

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `dependencies.services`

</details>

<details id="dependenciesdefinition-settings">
<summary><code>settings</code> — Dependency settings</summary>

Optional mock settings or a reference to them.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Sets shared mock defaults for dependency services, inline or through a settings reference.
- **When omitted:** Falls back to specmatic.settings.mock and then each mock field's accessor default.
- **Precedence:** For core mock settings, the service setting wins over the dependency setting, which wins over the global mock setting when a non-null value is supplied.
- **Watch for:** These are direct mock fields, not a nested mock object. The actual command can stage CLI settings and individual fields can have additional fallbacks.
- **Example:** Use dependencies.settings.strictMode: true; a service can explicitly choose false.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/mock-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `dependencies.settings`

</details>

## Dictionary Configuration

A dictionary supplies values used by generated requests or mock responses.

Tests prefer service dictionaries then the dictionary flag. Mocks prefer service, then dependency, then flag. Shared parser accessors do not all consume scoped YAML dictionaries.

<details id="dictionaryconfiguration-path">
<summary><code>path</code> — Dictionary file path</summary>

Filesystem path to the dictionary file.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core V3 data resolution and core OpenAPI consumers in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners require their own review.
- **Effect:** Names a JSON/YAML object file supplying generation values. This config contains the file path, not the dictionary entries.
- **When omitted:** The path member is required when a dictionary object is supplied. If no nonempty path is selected, core OpenAPI parsing checks spec-name_dictionary.yml, then .yaml, then .json beside the specification; without any of them it uses an empty dictionary.
- **Precedence:** Service/dependency/environment selection happens before file loading. A nonempty selected path prevents implicit sibling-file selection; a missing selected file fails rather than falling back to another dictionary.
- **Watch for:** Relative explicit paths use the process working directory. An empty string is retained by config accessors and suppresses their later fallback, but the OpenAPI file selector treats it as no explicit path and searches beside the spec. Missing files, directories in place of files and non-object/invalid contents fail dictionary loading. Omit the dictionary to request normal fallback; do not use an empty path as a disable switch.
- **Example:** dictionary: &#123;path: ./data/orders.yaml&#125; selects that file from the process directory.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/data-recipes).

**Placement**

- `components.dictionaries.<name>.path`
- `components.services.<name>.data.dictionary.path`
- `dependencies.data.dictionary.path`
- `dependencies.services[].service.data.dictionary.path`
- `systemUnderTest.service.data.dictionary.path`

</details>

## Example Directories

Data supplies external examples, dictionaries and adapters at applicable service/dependency use sites.

Test examples combine service directories with environment/property additions. Mock examples also include dependency-wide directories. Conventional example-directory templates provide discovery beyond explicit lists.

<details id="exampledirectories-directories">
<summary><code>directories</code> — Example directories</summary>

Directories searched for example payloads.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core V3 data resolution and core OpenAPI consumers in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners require their own review.
- **Effect:** Lists filesystem directories containing external JSON request/response examples. Core test loading walks these directories recursively.
- **When omitted:** This member is required in a directory-group object. Use an empty list when the group contributes no directories; other selected groups and implicit discovery still apply.
- **Precedence:** Data.toExampleDirs flattens selected groups in authored order. Core DirectoryExampleSource removes duplicate canonical directory paths before loading; that does not make distinct files or competing requests identical.
- **Watch for:** Core DirectoryExampleSource and ExampleModule construct File(path): relative paths use the process working directory. Missing directories can contribute no examples rather than fail. An explicit path-mapping tool can rebase values, but ordinary loading does not automatically run mapPaths. In released CLI mock loading, configured/implicit examples can bypass the fallback to additional --examples or EXAMPLE_DIRECTORIES directories. The per-spec accessor list is not proof of loaded mock examples. Include additional directories in data.examples when combining them with configured examples.
- **Example:** directories: [./examples/common, ./examples/orders] searches both locations from the process directory.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/data-recipes).

**Placement**

- `components.examples.commonExamples.directories`
- `components.examples.mockExamples[].directories`
- `components.examples.testExamples[].directories`
- `components.services.<name>.data.examples[].directories`
- `dependencies.data.examples[].directories`
- `dependencies.services[].service.data.examples[].directories`
- `systemUnderTest.service.data.examples[].directories`

</details>

## Examples Configuration

Data supplies external examples, dictionaries and adapters at applicable service/dependency use sites.

Test examples combine service directories with environment/property additions. Mock examples also include dependency-wide directories. Conventional example-directory templates provide discovery beyond explicit lists.

<details id="examplesconfiguration-commonexamples">
<summary><code>commonExamples</code> — Common example directories</summary>

Example directories shared by test and mock execution.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core V3 data resolution and core OpenAPI consumers in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners require their own review.
- **Effect:** Stores one reusable directory-group object. Use it in a selected data.examples list or in the reusable test/mock lists.
- **When omitted:** No common group is declared; there is no automatic global common-directory contribution.
- **Precedence:** A list item with $ref: "#/components/examples/commonExamples" resolves this group. Other selected groups continue to contribute their directories.
- **Watch for:** commonExamples is an object with directories; testExamples and mockExamples are lists. Referencing the common object as the whole data.examples list has the wrong target shape.
- **Example:** data: &#123;examples: [&#123;$ref: "#/components/examples/commonExamples"&#125;]&#125; selects the common group.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/data-recipes).

**Placement**

- `components.examples.commonExamples`

</details>

<details id="examplesconfiguration-mockexamples">
<summary><code>mockExamples</code> — Mock example directories</summary>

Example-directory entries used by mocks.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core V3 data resolution and core OpenAPI consumers in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners require their own review.
- **Effect:** Stores a reusable list of example-directory groups in components.examples. It is not a named map of arbitrary groups.
- **When omitted:** No reusable mock list is declared. This does not disable examples selected inline or implicit discovery.
- **Precedence:** A data.examples reference to #/components/examples/mockExamples selects this list. The consuming test/mock data path determines its use; its name alone does not activate it.
- **Watch for:** Do not put arbitrary names directly under components.examples. Its declared members are testExamples, mockExamples and commonExamples. Directory-group items can reference commonExamples.
- **Example:** components.examples.mockExamples: [&#123;directories: [./examples/mock]&#125;] can be selected by a data.examples reference.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/data-recipes).

**Placement**

- `components.examples.mockExamples`

</details>

<details id="examplesconfiguration-testexamples">
<summary><code>testExamples</code> — Test example directories</summary>

Example-directory entries used during tests.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core V3 data resolution and core OpenAPI consumers in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners require their own review.
- **Effect:** Stores a reusable list of example-directory groups in components.examples. It is not a named map of arbitrary groups.
- **When omitted:** No reusable test list is declared. This does not disable examples selected inline or implicit discovery.
- **Precedence:** A data.examples reference to #/components/examples/testExamples selects this list. The consuming test/mock data path determines its use; its name alone does not activate it.
- **Watch for:** Do not put arbitrary names directly under components.examples. Its declared members are testExamples, mockExamples and commonExamples. Directory-group items can reference commonExamples.
- **Example:** components.examples.testExamples: [&#123;directories: [./examples/test]&#125;] can be selected by a data.examples reference.

**Placement**

- `components.examples.testExamples`

</details>

## Feature Flags

General settings are used by shared parsers, matching, logging and lifecycle code.

They can affect tests, mocks and validation. Telemetry uses environment/property precedence and disabling is retained for the lifetime of the process. Pretty printing defaults to enabled.

<details id="featureflags-escapesoapaction">
<summary><code>escapeSoapAction</code> — Escape SOAP actions</summary>

Apply SOAP action escaping behavior.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Prefers a quoted SOAPAction header for generated SOAP 1.1 requests when no example provides the header.
- **When omitted:** Absent configuration falls back to SPECMATIC_ESCAPE_SOAP_ACTION, environment before JVM property; if absent there too, false selects unquoted generated actions.
- **Precedence:** An explicit config true/false wins over the environment/property fallback. An example-supplied SOAPAction selects its matching header pattern ahead of this quoting preference.
- **Watch for:** This controls SOAPAction quoting, not XML escaping or all SOAP header validation. A supplied unquoted example remains unquoted even with the flag true.
- **Example:** Set escapeSoapAction: true under specmatic.settings.general.featureFlags to generate quoted actions; review external example headers first.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.featureFlags.escapeSoapAction`
- `specmatic.settings.general.featureFlags.escapeSoapAction`

</details>

<details id="featureflags-fuzzymatcherforpayloads">
<summary><code>fuzzyMatcherForPayloads</code> — Fuzzy payload matching</summary>

Enable fuzzy matching of payload keys.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Enables the core fuzzy-matching strategy flag for payload matching.
- **When omitted:** With no configured value, the accessor reads SPECMATIC_FUZZY from the environment, then the Java property; without either it returns false.
- **Precedence:** An explicit configured boolean wins over SPECMATIC_FUZZY, including false. The environment is checked before the Java property.
- **Watch for:** It does not mean that every mismatching request becomes valid; matching details and protocol applicability need their own checks.
- **Example:** Use featureFlags.fuzzyMatcherForPayloads: true only when you want the fuzzy strategy in the applicable consumer.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/how-config-is-used).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.featureFlags.fuzzyMatcherForPayloads`
- `specmatic.settings.general.featureFlags.fuzzyMatcherForPayloads`

</details>

<details id="featureflags-schemaexampledefault">
<summary><code>schemaExampleDefault</code> — Use schema examples as defaults</summary>

Use schema example values as defaults where supported.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Enables the core strategy that uses schema examples as default generated values.
- **When omitted:** With no configured value, the accessor reads SCHEMA_EXAMPLE_DEFAULT from the environment, then the Java property; without either it returns false.
- **Precedence:** An explicit configured boolean wins over SCHEMA_EXAMPLE_DEFAULT, including false. The environment is checked before the Java property.
- **Watch for:** This is separate from named request/response examples, dictionary values and ignoreInlineExamples. It does not validate application behavior.
- **Example:** Use featureFlags.schemaExampleDefault: true when applicable generation should prefer schema-provided examples.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.featureFlags.schemaExampleDefault`
- `specmatic.settings.general.featureFlags.schemaExampleDefault`

</details>

## File System Source Options

A filesystem source supplies local specification files.

Path resolution depends on the consumer: typed V3 helpers use the discovered config directory; the released CLI local loader resolves a relative source directory from the process working directory. Run beside the selected config, or use an absolute source directory to align those bases.

<details id="filesystemsourceoptions-directory">
<summary><code>directory</code> — Source directory</summary>

Local source directory. The core CLI loader resolves it from the process directory; typed V3 matching can use the discovered config directory.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Local filesystem sources in core CLI test/mock runs and the separate typed V3 source resolver in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3.
- **Effect:** Sets the local source directory, with definition.specs paths selected inside it. Use an absolute directory when the process working directory and config location differ, or run from the directory containing the selected config.
- **When omitted:** The core LocalFileSystemSource loader defaults to . and resolves it from the process working directory. The typed V3 source helper instead starts at the globally discovered config directory. Omission is reliable when those directories agree; it is not a universal config-relative default.
- **Precedence:** The selected definition source supplies this directory, inline or through components.sources. Templates are resolved before binding. Tools that explicitly canonicalize or rebase source paths can change the value; ordinary loading does not automatically perform that transformation.
- **Watch for:** Relative paths can be loaded from the process directory while typed spec lookup/validation uses the config directory. For a nested config, merely setting CONFIG_FILE_PATH does not align those bases. A missing spec can produce a warning and no runnable configuration. Specmatic templates require the NAME:default form; &#123;NAME&#125; alone is not that substitution syntax.
- **Example:** From project/config, directory: ../specs selects project/specs. From project itself, select config/test.yaml and supply an absolute directory through CONTRACT_DIRECTORY="$PWD/specs" and directory: "&#123;CONTRACT_DIRECTORY:../specs&#125;".

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/files-and-paths).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.filesystem.directory`
- `components.sources.<name>.filesystem.directory`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.filesystem.directory`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.filesystem.directory`

</details>

## File System Source Schema

A filesystem source supplies local specification files.

Path resolution depends on the consumer: typed V3 helpers use the discovered config directory; the released CLI local loader resolves a relative source directory from the process working directory. Run beside the selected config, or use an absolute source directory to align those bases.

<details id="filesystemsourceschema-filesystem">
<summary><code>filesystem</code> — Filesystem source options</summary>

Local directory settings for this filesystem source.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Chooses local file loading. Its directory and each spec path locate existing specifications; it does not download them.
- **When omitted:** Omitting this provider means choose another provider; an otherwise empty source is not equivalent to filesystem: &#123;&#125;. The empty filesystem options object itself is supported.
- **Precedence:** Mutually exclusive with git and web within one source. Use separate definitions to load from different providers.
- **Watch for:** Typed V3 matching can use the discovered config directory while the CLI local loader uses the process directory. Do not infer one universal path base from the source wrapper.
- **Example:** Use filesystem: &#123;&#125; with specs: [contracts/books.yaml] when running beside the config.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/contract-management).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.filesystem`
- `components.sources.<name>.filesystem`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.filesystem`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.filesystem`

</details>

## General Settings

General settings are used by shared parsers, matching, logging and lifecycle code.

They can affect tests, mocks and validation. Telemetry uses environment/property precedence and disabling is retained for the lifetime of the process. Pretty printing defaults to enabled.

<details id="generalsettings-disabletelemetry">
<summary><code>disableTelemetry</code> — Disable telemetry</summary>

Disable telemetry reporting.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Controls the core telemetry-disabled setting.
- **When omitted:** Without configuration or SPECMATIC_DISABLE_TELEMETRY, the accessor returns false. Other license/lifecycle policy can independently prevent shipping telemetry.
- **Precedence:** SPECMATIC_DISABLE_TELEMETRY from environment or Java property is read before the configured boolean; this differs from the ordinary general boolean settings.
- **Watch for:** Setting false does not prove that telemetry is shipped. This is not a general network-off switch.
- **Example:** Use disableTelemetry: true under specmatic.settings.general.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/how-config-is-used).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.disableTelemetry`
- `specmatic.settings.general.disableTelemetry`

</details>

<details id="generalsettings-featureflags">
<summary><code>featureFlags</code> — Feature flags</summary>

Optional feature switches controlling matching and output behavior.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Groups the fuzzy payload, schema-example default and SOAPAction compatibility flags.
- **When omitted:** Each omitted child uses its own accessor fallback; omitting this object does not force every feature off.
- **Precedence:** Child flags have independent environment/property and configured-value rules. A general object is not a service-level settings scope.
- **Watch for:** These flags do not select a protocol, service or specification. Check each child before enabling a compatibility behavior.
- **Example:** Use featureFlags.schemaExampleDefault: true; keep unrelated flags omitted.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/how-config-is-used).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.featureFlags`
- `specmatic.settings.general.featureFlags`

</details>

<details id="generalsettings-ignoreinlineexamples">
<summary><code>ignoreInlineExamples</code> — Ignore inline examples</summary>

Ignore examples embedded in specifications.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Controls the core ignore-inline-examples setting used when loading specification examples.
- **When omitted:** With no configured value, the accessor reads IGNORE_INLINE_EXAMPLES from the environment, then the Java property; without either it returns false.
- **Precedence:** An explicit configured boolean wins over IGNORE_INLINE_EXAMPLES, including false. The environment is checked before the Java property.
- **Watch for:** This does not disable external example directories or dictionary generation. Loading behavior differs from merely suppressing example warnings.
- **Example:** Use ignoreInlineExamples: true when selected external examples should be used without inline examples.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.ignoreInlineExamples`
- `specmatic.settings.general.ignoreInlineExamples`

</details>

<details id="generalsettings-ignoreinlineexamplewarnings">
<summary><code>ignoreInlineExampleWarnings</code> — Ignore inline-example warnings</summary>

Suppress warnings about ignored inline examples.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Suppresses the core inline-example warning messages controlled by this setting.
- **When omitted:** With no configured value, the accessor reads IGNORE_INLINE_EXAMPLE_WARNINGS from the environment, then the Java property; without either it returns false.
- **Precedence:** An explicit configured boolean wins over IGNORE_INLINE_EXAMPLE_WARNINGS, including false. The environment is checked before the Java property.
- **Watch for:** Warning suppression does not repair an invalid example or prove that an example was selected.
- **Example:** Use ignoreInlineExampleWarnings: true to suppress these warnings while retaining the separate inline-example selection setting.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.ignoreInlineExampleWarnings`
- `specmatic.settings.general.ignoreInlineExampleWarnings`

</details>

<details id="generalsettings-logging">
<summary><code>logging</code> — Logging configuration</summary>

Log level and JSON/text output configuration.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core diagnostic logging in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol commands may supply different logging inputs; use their command-specific guidance.
- **Effect:** Controls core diagnostic verbosity and text/JSON output destinations. Use it to retain logs or choose machine-readable output.
- **When omitted:** Without general.logging, the accessor supplies INFO with ordinary text console output and no JSON output or log files.
- **Precedence:** Core logging setup combines explicit command inputs with the file field by field; supplied command values win. Which inputs are applied depends on the command. Service test/mock settings do not create separate general logging scopes.
- **Watch for:** A named components.settings entry has no effect merely because it exists; select it through specmatic.settings. Configuring logging does not establish that sensitive values are redacted. Ordinary logger setup does not automatically invoke the model path-rebasing helper.
- **Example:** Set specmatic.settings.general.logging.json to &#123;directory: ./logs, console: false&#125; to select JSON files while retaining the default text console.

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging`

</details>

<details id="generalsettings-prettyprint">
<summary><code>prettyPrint</code> — Pretty-print output</summary>

Enable pretty-printed output where supported.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Supplies the core preference for readable JSON formatting in consumers that honor this setting.
- **When omitted:** With no configured value, the accessor reads SPECMATIC_PRETTY_PRINT from the environment, then the Java property; without either it returns true.
- **Precedence:** An explicit configured boolean wins over SPECMATIC_PRETTY_PRINT, including false. The environment is checked before the Java property.
- **Watch for:** This is not an API response-schema change or a universal formatter for every report or separate protocol command.
- **Example:** Use prettyPrint: false for compact diagnostic JSON formatting.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/how-config-is-used).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.prettyPrint`
- `specmatic.settings.general.prettyPrint`

</details>

<details id="generalsettings-sharedexamplesdirectorytemplate">
<summary><code>sharedExamplesDirectoryTemplate</code> — Shared examples directory templates</summary>

Templates for shared example directories searched across specifications.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Sets the list of naming templates used for shared-example directory discovery.
- **When omitted:** Defaults to ["&lt;SPEC_EACH_PARENT&gt;_common", "common"] in the global-settings accessor.
- **Precedence:** An explicit list replaces the default list, rather than appending. An empty list is retained as an explicit choice.
- **Watch for:** These are discovery templates, not automatically selected component examples. Supported token validation applies to every list item; exact discovery traversal needs its own consumer evidence.
- **Example:** Use sharedExamplesDirectoryTemplate: ["common"].

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.sharedExamplesDirectoryTemplate`
- `specmatic.settings.general.sharedExamplesDirectoryTemplate`

</details>

<details id="generalsettings-specexamplesdirectorytemplate">
<summary><code>specExamplesDirectoryTemplate</code> — Specification examples directory template</summary>

Template for the directory containing examples associated with a specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Sets the naming template used by consumers discovering per-specification example directories.
- **When omitted:** The global-settings accessor defaults to &lt;SPEC_FILE_NAME&gt;_examples.
- **Precedence:** An explicit template replaces that default. This naming template is separate from explicitly selected data.examples directories.
- **Watch for:** Only &lt;SPEC_FILE_NAME&gt; and &lt;SPEC_EACH_PARENT&gt; are accepted angle-bracket variables by this deserializer. The parser also has legacy colon/brace extraction; do not infer ordinary environment-template semantics from a lookalike string. Discovery and path bases depend on the consumer.
- **Example:** Use specExamplesDirectoryTemplate: "&lt;SPEC_FILE_NAME&gt;_examples".

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.specExamplesDirectoryTemplate`
- `specmatic.settings.general.specExamplesDirectoryTemplate`

</details>

## Git Bearer Env Authentication

Choose one Git authentication form: bearer token file, environment variable containing a bearer token, or personal access token.

This authenticates contract-source access, not API requests. Bearer environment lookup reads the environment directly; token files are trimmed.

<details id="gitbearerenvauthentication-bearerenvironmentvariable">
<summary><code>bearerEnvironmentVariable</code> — Bearer-token environment variable</summary>

Name of the environment variable containing the Git bearer token.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Names the process environment variable containing the Git bearer token. The value in YAML is the variable name, not the token.
- **When omitted:** Required when choosing this auth variant. If the named environment variable is absent, the traced loader logs that it is unset and returns no bearer token.
- **Precedence:** Token lookup uses System.getenv for the named variable; it does not fall back to a same-named JVM property. Configuration templates can separately resolve the authored variable name. Credential selection follows the first matching source URL.
- **Watch for:** An empty environment value is returned as a supplied token value; this helper does not validate it against the host. Do not infer that schema validity or an existing local checkout proves successful authentication.
- **Example:** Set bearerEnvironmentVariable: CONTRACTS_TOKEN, and supply the token in the process environment for the actual Git host.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth.bearerEnvironmentVariable`
- `components.sources.<name>.git.auth.bearerEnvironmentVariable`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth.bearerEnvironmentVariable`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth.bearerEnvironmentVariable`

</details>

## Git Bearer File Authentication

Choose one Git authentication form: bearer token file, environment variable containing a bearer token, or personal access token.

This authenticates contract-source access, not API requests. Bearer environment lookup reads the environment directly; token files are trimmed.

<details id="gitbearerfileauthentication-bearerfile">
<summary><code>bearerFile</code> — Bearer-token file</summary>

Path to a file containing the Git bearer token.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Selects a text file containing the Git bearer token; the traced reader trims its contents before forming a token.
- **When omitted:** Required within this auth variant. A missing selected file logs that it cannot be found and returns no token; it does not select an arbitrary alternative credential file.
- **Precedence:** The reader constructs File(path).absoluteFile from the process working directory. A separate path-mapping entry point can rebase a path, but the mapping helper alone does not prove normal CLI loading invokes it.
- **Watch for:** Existing-but-unreadable files can throw during reading. Empty trimmed content is still a supplied value. Private-host authorization and all integration path bases are execution-pending; use an absolute file path when removing ambiguity.
- **Example:** Use bearerFile: /absolute/path/to/contracts-token.txt for an existing token file; keep that file outside committed config examples.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth.bearerFile`
- `components.sources.<name>.git.auth.bearerFile`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth.bearerFile`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth.bearerFile`

</details>

## Git Personal Access Token Authentication

Choose one Git authentication form: bearer token file, environment variable containing a bearer token, or personal access token.

This authenticates contract-source access, not API requests. Bearer environment lookup reads the environment directly; token files are trimmed.

<details id="gitpersonalaccesstokenauthentication-personalaccesstoken">
<summary><code>personalAccessToken</code> — Personal access token</summary>

Resolved personal access token used for Git retrieval.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Supplies the Git personal-access token value. The traced CLI Git credential adapter formats it as a Basic Authorization header using the token followed by a colon.
- **When omitted:** Required within this auth variant. A blank selected token is filtered out by getPersonalAccessToken, yielding no PAT header from this adapter.
- **Precedence:** Mutually exclusive with bearer auth variants in one object. Accessors take the first selected source with the same repository URL; values are not combined across source declarations.
- **Watch for:** This is a value, not a filename or environment-variable-name field. Use an authored template to obtain a value from external inputs and avoid credentials in defaults. Host-specific Basic/PAT requirements and fallback transport differences are not established by the local clone recipe.
- **Example:** Set personalAccessToken using a config template whose value is supplied outside the checked-in file; choose the auth form supported by your Git host.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth.personalAccessToken`
- `components.sources.<name>.git.auth.personalAccessToken`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth.personalAccessToken`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth.personalAccessToken`

</details>

## Git Source Options

A Git source loads contracts from a repository and can select a branch or authentication method.

matchBranch attempts the current checkout branch, with a declared-branch fallback. Repository caches and bundled contracts can affect where files are loaded; config validation may fetch repositories.

<details id="gitsourceoptions-auth">
<summary><code>auth</code> — Git authentication</summary>

Optional authentication method for the Git source.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Selects one Git authentication form: bearer token file, bearer-token environment variable name, or personal-access token value. This is separate from API request authentication.
- **When omitted:** Optional for repository access that does not need a supplied credential. An authored empty auth object is not an authentication-disable switch and fails binding; omit auth instead.
- **Precedence:** The model requires exactly one authentication property. V3 accessors consider tested-service sources before mock sources and take the first matching repository URL; repeated definitions of the same URL do not merge credentials.
- **Watch for:** The clone/access transport consumes this selection; a local file-URL clone does not verify authentication. Existing checkout/cache branches can avoid a clone. Private-host compatibility and SSH transport behavior still require execution checks.
- **Example:** Use auth: &#123;bearerEnvironmentVariable: CONTRACTS_TOKEN&#125; to select a token from the process environment without putting its value in the config.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth`
- `components.sources.<name>.git.auth`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.git.auth`

</details>

<details id="gitsourceoptions-branch">
<summary><code>branch</code> — Git branch or ref</summary>

Branch or ref to retrieve.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Requests a branch/ref for a separate contract checkout. Use it to select the contract revision needed by the run.
- **When omitted:** With no configured branch and branch matching disabled, a new clone uses the repository default. The main default in the demonstration template is authored, not a runtime default.
- **Precedence:** matchBranch or the applicable CLI branch-matching input takes the current branch selection ahead of configured branch. If current-branch determination throws, the configured branch is the fallback. Reused bundled/home checkouts can bypass local checkout checks.
- **Watch for:** Checkout exceptions are caught and debug-logged by the traced checkout helper; startup alone does not prove the requested branch exists or was selected. Validate the loaded contract or branch. Detached heads, absent refs and all refresh cases are not established by the successful branch recipes.
- **Example:** The demonstration selects main to return Specmatic Handbook, then alternate to return Alternate Handbook.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/contract-management).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.git.branch`
- `components.sources.<name>.git.branch`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.git.branch`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.git.branch`

</details>

<details id="gitsourceoptions-matchbranch">
<summary><code>matchBranch</code> — Follow current repository branch</summary>

Follow the current repository branch when resolving this source.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Asks a separate contract source to follow the current working/CI branch instead of its configured branch when determining the requested checkout.
- **When omitted:** Defaults to false in V3 source loading unless the caller enables current-branch matching. Omission does not automatically follow the application branch.
- **Precedence:** V3 combines the caller’s current-branch flag with the source boolean using OR; a caller enabling it is not disabled by source false. GitHub Actions lookup checks nonblank GITHUB_HEAD_REF, then GITHUB_REF_NAME, then local currentBranch. Lookup failure falls back to configured branch.
- **Watch for:** The checkout helper can create a missing matching branch in the cloned repository; that does not prove a corresponding remote contract branch exists. Checkout errors can be debug-logged. Existing bundle/home checkout paths can bypass these local checks. CI matching and missing-branch behavior remain execution-pending.
- **Example:** Set matchBranch: true when contract and application branch naming intentionally align; inspect the selected branch and loaded spec rather than relying on the mock startup banner.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.git.matchBranch`
- `components.sources.<name>.git.matchBranch`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.git.matchBranch`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.git.matchBranch`

</details>

<details id="gitsourceoptions-url">
<summary><code>url</code> — Git repository URL</summary>

Git repository URL. Omit intentionally for monorepo/current-repository mode.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Supplies the clone URL for a separate contract repository. Spec paths are inside its selected checkout.
- **When omitted:** Omitting url intentionally selects current-checkout Git mode. This differs from an empty string, which is not null and selects the separate-repository branch.
- **Precedence:** The traced GitRepo loader first considers a bundled same-named checkout, then one in the home .specmatic/repos, then its local repos cache. Local refresh/branch checks do not run identically in every branch.
- **Watch for:** Repository names are derived from the URL basename with .git removed. A valid URL does not establish permissions or freshness; the local-URL recipe verifies cloning but not private-host transport, all cache branches or every URL scheme.
- **Example:** Supply CONTRACTS_REPOSITORY as the file URL of the demonstration source-repository; for your host, supply its actual clone URL and matching spec paths.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/contract-management).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.git.url`
- `components.sources.<name>.git.url`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.git.url`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.git.url`

</details>

## Git Source Schema

A Git source loads contracts from a repository and can select a branch or authentication method.

matchBranch attempts the current checkout branch, with a declared-branch fallback. Repository caches and bundled contracts can affect where files are loaded; config validation may fetch repositories.

<details id="gitsourceschema-git">
<summary><code>git</code> — Git source options</summary>

Repository and branch settings for this Git source.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Chooses a Git repository source, either a separate checkout selected by url or current-checkout mode when url is omitted.
- **When omitted:** Choose another provider if Git is not needed. git: &#123;&#125; is a valid intentional current-checkout source; source: &#123;&#125; is invalid.
- **Precedence:** Mutually exclusive with filesystem and web. A non-null url selects GitRepo; an omitted url selects GitMonoRepo in the traced loader.
- **Watch for:** An empty url string is supplied and does not select the monorepo branch. Current-checkout loading requires a Git context and has different path/cache behavior from cloning.
- **Example:** Use git: &#123;&#125; for the current checkout, or git with a supplied clone URL and branch for a separate repository.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/contract-management).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.git`

## Graphql Sdl Common Run Options

GraphQL run options are converted into Enterprise test/mock configuration.

Nonempty per-spec option maps replace global maps, rather than deep merging. Test config host/port precede staged values; fallback is localhost:8080. DTOs ignore unknown properties.

<details id="graphqlsdlcommonrunoptions-host">
<summary><code>host</code> — GraphQL service host</summary>

Host used by the GraphQL service.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 GraphQL HTTP mock/test consumers in Enterprise 1.28.3. Shared schema acceptance does not establish OSS GraphQL runtime support. A reusable component affects execution only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Sets the GraphQL mock bind address or the tested implementation hostname.
- **When omitted:** Without a resolved config host, the mock uses the CLI host then 0.0.0.0. Tests use a staged CLI/programmatic host then localhost; the GraphQL CLI stages 0.0.0.0 by default. The programmatic fallback is not the CLI default.
- **Precedence:** Resolved protocol host wins over staged CLI values. The first matching spec ID selects an override. A nonempty override configuration map replaces the service protocol map; an empty override map retains it. This is not field-by-field inheritance.
- **Watch for:** The GraphQL mock selects one listener configuration from the first convertible specification config, not a listener per SDL. Tests resolve endpoints per specification. The recipe verifies one local SDL only. The test consumer constructs an http URL at /graphql; this field is not an HTTPS/baseUrl setting.
- **Example:** host: 127.0.0.1 with mock port 9101 or test port 9100.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/graphql).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.graphqlsdl.host`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.graphqlsdl.host`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.graphqlsdl.host`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.graphqlsdl.host`

</details>

<details id="graphqlsdlcommonrunoptions-port">
<summary><code>port</code> — GraphQL service port</summary>

Port used by the GraphQL service.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 GraphQL HTTP mock/test consumers in Enterprise 1.28.3. Shared schema acceptance does not establish OSS GraphQL runtime support. A reusable component affects execution only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Sets the GraphQL listener port for mocking or the implementation port for testing.
- **When omitted:** Without a config port, mock uses its CLI/default port 9000 and chooses a free port if that default is occupied. Tests use the staged port then programmatic fallback 8080; the GraphQL CLI stages 9000 by default.
- **Precedence:** Configured port wins over CLI --port. The first matching spec ID selects an override. A nonempty override configuration map replaces the service protocol map; an empty override map retains it. This is not field-by-field inheritance.
- **Watch for:** The GraphQL mock selects one listener configuration from the first convertible specification config, not a listener per SDL. Tests resolve endpoints per specification. The recipe verifies one local SDL only. A configured occupied port is not the same as the free-port fallback for the unconfigured default. Choose an available usable TCP port.
- **Example:** Mock port: 9101; test port: 9100.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/graphql).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.graphqlsdl.port`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.graphqlsdl.port`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.graphqlsdl.port`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.graphqlsdl.port`

</details>

## Graphql Sdl Mock Run Options

GraphQL run options are converted into Enterprise test/mock configuration.

Nonempty per-spec option maps replace global maps, rather than deep merging. Test config host/port precede staged values; fallback is localhost:8080. DTOs ignore unknown properties.

<details id="graphqlsdlmockrunoptions-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specification overrides</summary>

Optional per-specification GraphQL SDL overrides.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 GraphQL HTTP mock/test consumers in Enterprise 1.28.3. Shared schema acceptance does not establish OSS GraphQL runtime support. A reusable component affects execution only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Lists protocol overrides selected by specification ID.
- **When omitted:** An omitted or empty list has no matching overrides. An id-only entry has an empty config map and retains the service protocol map.
- **Precedence:** The first matching spec ID selects an override. A nonempty override configuration map replaces the service protocol map; an empty override map retains it. This is not field-by-field inheritance.
- **Watch for:** The GraphQL mock selects one listener configuration from the first convertible specification config, not a listener per SDL. Tests resolve endpoints per specification. The recipe verifies one local SDL only. This list does not select or load the SDL; definitions does that.
- **Example:** specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: catalog, host: 127.0.0.1, port: 9102&#125;&#125;]

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/graphql).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.graphqlsdl.specs`

- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.graphqlsdl.specs`

</details>

<details id="graphqlsdlmockrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Mock mode</summary>

Mock branch discriminator.

- **Value:** exactly "mock".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 GraphQL HTTP mock/test consumers in Enterprise 1.28.3. Shared schema acceptance does not establish OSS GraphQL runtime support. A reusable component affects execution only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Declares the inline selected GraphQL run option as mock.
- **When omitted:** The selected-service typed model accepts an omitted type because the enclosing service supplies its role. A reusable run-options component needs type.
- **Precedence:** An explicit mode must agree with its selected service role; it does not change the service section.
- **Watch for:** Choose mock under dependencies and test under systemUnderTest. Omission is not an instruction to run both modes.
- **Example:** type: mock

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.graphqlsdl.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.graphqlsdl.type`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.graphqlsdl.type`

</details>

Nonempty per-spec option maps replace global maps, rather than deep merging. Test config host/port precede staged values; fallback is localhost:8080. DTOs ignore unknown properties.

Specification identifier this override targets.

## Graphql Sdl Test Run Options

GraphQL run options are converted into Enterprise test/mock configuration.

Nonempty per-spec option maps replace global maps, rather than deep merging. Test config host/port precede staged values; fallback is localhost:8080. DTOs ignore unknown properties.

<details id="graphqlsdltestrunoptions-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specification overrides</summary>

Optional per-specification GraphQL SDL overrides.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 GraphQL HTTP mock/test consumers in Enterprise 1.28.3. Shared schema acceptance does not establish OSS GraphQL runtime support. A reusable component affects execution only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Lists protocol overrides selected by specification ID.
- **When omitted:** An omitted or empty list has no matching overrides. An id-only entry has an empty config map and retains the service protocol map.
- **Precedence:** The first matching spec ID selects an override. A nonempty override configuration map replaces the service protocol map; an empty override map retains it. This is not field-by-field inheritance.
- **Watch for:** The GraphQL mock selects one listener configuration from the first convertible specification config, not a listener per SDL. Tests resolve endpoints per specification. The recipe verifies one local SDL only. This list does not select or load the SDL; definitions does that.
- **Example:** specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: catalog, host: 127.0.0.1, port: 9102&#125;&#125;]

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/graphql).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.graphqlsdl.specs`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.graphqlsdl.specs`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.graphqlsdl.specs`

</details>

<details id="graphqlsdltestrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Run mode</summary>

Test branch discriminator.

- **Value:** exactly "test".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 GraphQL HTTP mock/test consumers in Enterprise 1.28.3. Shared schema acceptance does not establish OSS GraphQL runtime support. A reusable component affects execution only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Declares the inline selected GraphQL run option as test.
- **When omitted:** The selected-service typed model accepts an omitted type because the enclosing service supplies its role. A reusable run-options component needs type.
- **Precedence:** An explicit mode must agree with its selected service role; it does not change the service section.
- **Watch for:** Choose mock under dependencies and test under systemUnderTest. Omission is not an instruction to run both modes.
- **Example:** type: test

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/graphql).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.graphqlsdl.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.graphqlsdl.type`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.graphqlsdl.type`

</details>

## Graphql Sdl Test Run Option Specification Entry

<details id="graphqlsdltestrunoptionspecificationentry-spec">

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/graphql).
