## Key Store Directory Configuration

Certificates configure TLS key material and, for mock listeners, optional incoming mutual TLS.

Select a keystore file or directory. Schema validation rejects both together, although plain binding prefers file. Store/key passwords default to forgotten and aliases default to specmatic plus a caller-specific suffix.

<details id="keystoredirectoryconfiguration-alias">
<summary><code>alias</code> — Key-store alias</summary>

Private-key identity alias in the store.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI mock CLI and contract-test client in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. SOAP/proxy and other consumers need separate execution checks.
- **Effect:** Selects a server key for mock TLS. The core test HTTP client uses the configured alias when present in available keys, otherwise falls back to the first available alias in reviewed source.
- **When omitted:** Default is specmaticmock for mock CertInfo, specmatictest for test conversion; proxy CertInfo uses specmaticproxy.
- **Precedence:** Nested non-null CLI alias overrides config alias in the reviewed certificate merge.
- **Watch for:** The recipe uses one explicit alias per existing file. Missing/wrong alias, multi-key stores, directory generation and CLI alias cases remain unexecuted. Do not assume the test-client fallback applies to the mock server.
- **Example:** Use demo-server for the server store and demo-client for the client store.

**Placement**

- `components.certificates.<name>.keyStore.alias`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.cert.keyStore.alias`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.cert.keyStore.alias`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore.alias`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore.alias`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore.alias`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore.alias`
- `proxies[].proxy.cert.keyStore.alias`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore.alias`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore.alias`

</details>

<details id="keystoredirectoryconfiguration-directory">
<summary><code>directory</code> — Key-store directory</summary>

Directory mode: generate a mock store or load the test client store.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI mock CLI and contract-test client in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. SOAP/proxy and other consumers need separate execution checks.
- **Effect:** Mock CertInfo creates the directory, deletes an existing specmatic.jks and generates a new store. Test HttpsKeyData reads an existing specmatic.jks and fails if absent.
- **When omitted:** The deserializer requires file or directory. Missing both fails; source parser prefers file when both are supplied, while the resolved schema requires the appropriate alternative.
- **Precedence:** Nested non-null CLI store selection can replace configured file/directory mode; source traced, not a blanket precedence rule for outer passwords.
- **Watch for:** Getters canonicalize file/directory paths; entry-point path mapping can precede that. The recipe keeps config and stores in one working directory. Relocated config/source-path layouts remain unexecuted. Directory generation/replacement and directory-based test loading remain unexecuted; do not point a generating mock at a store you need to preserve.
- **Example:** For an existing client store in certs/specmatic.jks, use directory certs; generating mock mode has different effects.

**Placement**

- `components.certificates.<name>.keyStore.directory`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.cert.keyStore.directory`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.cert.keyStore.directory`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore.directory`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore.directory`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore.directory`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore.directory`
- `proxies[].proxy.cert.keyStore.directory`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore.directory`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore.directory`

</details>

<details id="keystoredirectoryconfiguration-password">
<summary><code>password</code> — Key-store password</summary>

Password unlocking the private key, separate from the store password.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI mock CLI and contract-test client in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. SOAP/proxy and other consumers need separate execution checks.
- **Effect:** Passed to the server/client private-key loader; the parent keyStorePassword opens the store.
- **When omitted:** Defaults to forgotten in HttpsConfiguration, independently of the store password.
- **Precedence:** Nested non-null CLI private-key password overrides the configured nested password in the reviewed merge; explicit config outer store password is retained.
- **Watch for:** The source schema description calls this a store password; runtime consumers use it for the private key. Different-password JKS and CLI private-key override cases remain unexecuted.
- **Example:** Use the same public demo password for both fields in these PKCS12 files.

**Placement**

- `components.certificates.<name>.keyStore.password`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.cert.keyStore.password`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.cert.keyStore.password`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore.password`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore.password`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore.password`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore.password`
- `proxies[].proxy.cert.keyStore.password`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.cert.keyStore.password`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.cert.keyStore.password`

</details>

## Logging Configuration

Logging controls level, console/file output and file-name prefix.

logPrefix is a binding alias of logFilePrefix. Incoming non-null logging fields win during merge; this differs from the top-level HTTPS merge behavior.

<details id="loggingconfiguration-json">
<summary><code>json</code> — JSON log output</summary>

Destination settings for structured JSON logs.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core diagnostic logging in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol commands may supply different logging inputs; use their command-specific guidance.
- **Effect:** Selects JSON console and/or file logging independently of plain text.
- **When omitted:** Omitting json selects no JSON printer. With json: &#123;&#125;, JSON console defaults to false and no file is selected, so the empty object adds no JSON output.
- **Precedence:** Core logging setup combines explicit command inputs with the file field by field; supplied command values win. Which inputs are applied depends on the command. Service test/mock settings do not create separate general logging scopes.
- **Watch for:** Adding json does not suppress text console output: set text.console: false if JSON is the only desired console format. CLI options construct a JSON override only when their required staging inputs are present; command registration alone does not establish an override.
- **Example:** Use json: &#123;console: true&#125; with text: &#123;console: false&#125; for a JSON-only configured console.

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.json`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.json`

</details>

<details id="loggingconfiguration-level">
<summary><code>level</code> — Log level</summary>

Minimum log level to emit.

- **Value:** "INFO", "DEBUG".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core diagnostic logging in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol commands may supply different logging inputs; use their command-specific guidance.
- **Effect:** Chooses INFO or DEBUG for the core logger. DEBUG includes additional diagnostic messages.
- **When omitted:** Omission selects INFO.
- **Precedence:** Core logging setup combines explicit command inputs with the file field by field; supplied command values win. Which inputs are applied depends on the command. Service test/mock settings do not create separate general logging scopes. A staged debug=true chooses DEBUG; false does not stage an INFO override.
- **Watch for:** Use the schema values INFO or DEBUG. Model conversion recognizes case-insensitive values and falls back to INFO for an unknown level; that fallback does not make an invalid schema value valid. Other logger modes and protocol consumers require their own review.
- **Example:** Use level: DEBUG when investigating loading or matching; return to INFO when the extra detail is unnecessary.

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.level`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.level`

</details>

<details id="loggingconfiguration-text">
<summary><code>text</code> — Text log output</summary>

Destination settings for human-readable text logs.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core diagnostic logging in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol commands may supply different logging inputs; use their command-specific guidance.
- **Effect:** Selects plain-text console and/or file logging independently of JSON logging.
- **When omitted:** Omitting text retains ordinary console output. With text: &#123;&#125;, console also defaults to true and no file is selected.
- **Precedence:** Core logging setup combines explicit command inputs with the file field by field; supplied command values win. Which inputs are applied depends on the command. Service test/mock settings do not create separate general logging scopes.
- **Watch for:** text.console: false does not disable JSON output. With text console false, no text directory and no JSON destination, the configured logger has no output printer; explicit startup output outside that logger may still occur.
- **Example:** Use text: &#123;console: true, directory: ./logs&#125; to keep text diagnostics on screen and in files.

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.text`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.text`

</details>

## Log Output Configuration

Logging controls level, console/file output and file-name prefix.

logPrefix is a binding alias of logFilePrefix. Incoming non-null logging fields win during merge; this differs from the top-level HTTPS merge behavior.

<details id="logoutputconfiguration-console">
<summary><code>console</code> — Log to console</summary>

Write this log output to the console.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core diagnostic logging in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol commands may supply different logging inputs; use their command-specific guidance.
- **Effect:** Enables or disables the selected format on the console. Set it separately under logging.text and logging.json.
- **When omitted:** The actual printer defaults differ: text console is true; JSON console is false. JSON output must also have a json configuration object.
- **Precedence:** Core logging setup combines explicit command inputs with the file field by field; supplied command values win. Which inputs are applied depends on the command. Service test/mock settings do not create separate general logging scopes. An explicit false is retained, not treated as omission.
- **Watch for:** Disabling console output leaves that format's directory logging enabled. Text and JSON do not share the same console default. Null is not a supported boolean value in the resolved schema.
- **Example:** Set text.console: false and json.console: true to change which format appears on screen.

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.json.console`
- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.text.console`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.json.console`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.text.console`

</details>

<details id="logoutputconfiguration-directory">
<summary><code>directory</code> — Log directory</summary>

Directory where this log output is written.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core diagnostic logging in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol commands may supply different logging inputs; use their command-specific guidance.
- **Effect:** Selects a directory for the chosen format's log files. Text and JSON can use different destinations.
- **When omitted:** Omission creates no file printer for that format; console output follows its separate setting.
- **Precedence:** Core logging setup combines explicit command inputs with the file field by field; supplied command values win. Which inputs are applied depends on the command. Service test/mock settings do not create separate general logging scopes.
- **Watch for:** Ordinary core printer setup resolves a relative directory against the process working directory and creates missing directories/files. It does not automatically rebase on the config directory. Permission/path failures can prevent setup. Empty-string and filesystem-failure outcomes still need execution checks.
- **Example:** Use text.directory: ./logs/text and json.directory: ./logs/json; run from the intended project directory.

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.json.directory`
- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.text.directory`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.json.directory`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.text.directory`

</details>

<details id="logoutputconfiguration-logfileprefix">
<summary><code>logFilePrefix</code> — Log-file prefix</summary>

Prefix used when naming log files.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.
- **Also accepted:** `logPrefix`.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core diagnostic logging in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol commands may supply different logging inputs; use their command-specific guidance.
- **Effect:** Sets the filename prefix independently for text or JSON log files. logPrefix is a binding alias for this same field.
- **When omitted:** Omission selects specmatic. Core filenames combine the prefix, a date component and .log for text or -json.log for JSON.
- **Precedence:** Core logging setup combines explicit command inputs with the file field by field; supplied command values win. Which inputs are applied depends on the command. Service test/mock settings do not create separate general logging scopes.
- **Watch for:** This changes a filename prefix, not the output directory or message content. An empty string is retained instead of using the default. Use a plain filename prefix without path separators. Supplying both alias spellings has not been established by these checks; use one spelling.
- **Example:** Set logFilePrefix: books-test in the selected text or JSON logging output object. logPrefix is an alternative spelling of the same key; supply one spelling.

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.json.logFilePrefix`
- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.json.logPrefix`
- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.text.logFilePrefix`
- `components.settings.<name>.general.logging.text.logPrefix`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.json.logFilePrefix`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.json.logPrefix`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.text.logFilePrefix`
- `specmatic.settings.general.logging.text.logPrefix`

</details>

## Mcp Definition

MCP test settings choose the server, transport, credentials, dictionary and tool selection.

CLI scalar options override config. filterTools and skipTools combine CLI/config entries. The command requires a transport even though its transport-conversion helper has a default. This model has no timeout key.

<details id="mcpdefinition-test">
<summary><code>test</code> — MCP test run options</summary>

Endpoint and transport settings for MCP tests.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** MCP auto testing through specmatic mcp test in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The checked transport is a local JSON streamable-HTTP endpoint; other integrations need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Contains the endpoint, transport, credentials, input data and tool selection for MCP tests.
- **When omitted:** Required if the mcp root section is present. Omitting mcp altogether allows CLI-only configuration, separately source-backed.
- **Precedence:** Scalar CLI choices override matching fields; tool include/exclude lists add to their corresponding config lists.
- **Watch for:** This is test configuration, not an MCP mock server or a component service. Schema validation cannot prove server reachability or tool behavior.
- **Example:** Place baseUrl and transportKind inside mcp.test.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mcp).

**Placement**

- `mcp.test`

</details>

## Mcp Test Run Options

MCP test settings choose the server, transport, credentials, dictionary and tool selection.

CLI scalar options override config. filterTools and skipTools combine CLI/config entries. The command requires a transport even though its transport-conversion helper has a default. This model has no timeout key.

<details id="mcptestrunoptions-baseurl">
<summary><code>baseUrl</code> — MCP server base URL</summary>

MCP server base URL.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** MCP auto testing through specmatic mcp test in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The checked transport is a local JSON streamable-HTTP endpoint; other integrations need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Selects the full HTTP endpoint used for MCP initialization, tool discovery and invocation.
- **When omitted:** Required in a configured mcp.test object. With no effective CLI/config URL, the command throws an error rather than guessing a server.
- **Precedence:** CLI --url wins over config baseUrl, including a configured wrong endpoint.
- **Watch for:** Include the endpoint path required by the server, such as /mcp. The URI schema does not establish support for every URI scheme. Network/TLS failures are execution errors, not successful configuration checks.
- **Example:** The demo uses <code>http://127.0.0.1:19100/mcp</code>.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mcp).

**Placement**

- `mcp.test.baseUrl`

</details>

<details id="mcptestrunoptions-bearertoken">
<summary><code>bearerToken</code> — Bearer token</summary>

Bearer token used to authenticate with the MCP server.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** MCP auto testing through specmatic mcp test in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The checked transport is a local JSON streamable-HTTP endpoint; other integrations need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Sends Authorization: Bearer &lt;value&gt; on the MCP HTTP client requests.
- **When omitted:** Without a CLI/config token, no bearer header is added. The authenticated demo requires its public docs-demo token; unauthenticated operation is source-backed rather than checked here.
- **Precedence:** CLI --bearer-token replaces the config token.
- **Watch for:** Use appropriate secret handling for real credentials. Reports include request/response bodies; transport authentication is not proof that those bodies are safe to share. The demo token is public and has no external privileges.
- **Example:** A wrong demo token yields 401 and zero scenarios; CLI docs-demo recovers.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mcp).

**Placement**

- `mcp.test.bearerToken`

</details>

<details id="mcptestrunoptions-dictionaryfile">
<summary><code>dictionaryFile</code> — Dictionary file</summary>

Path to the dictionary file used by MCP tests.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** MCP auto testing through specmatic mcp test in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The checked transport is a local JSON streamable-HTTP endpoint; other integrations need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Loads generation values for tool arguments. It is input data, not an expected-output fixture.
- **When omitted:** Missing/omitted/nonexistent files use an empty dictionary and generated values in this runner. A nonexistent configured path is not a fail-fast file check.
- **Precedence:** CLI --dictionary-file wins over config. The normal MCP CLI constructs File from the selected path; the recipe separately checks a config in a different directory to establish the working-directory base. Programmatic path mapping needs separate guidance.
- **Watch for:** Supply values matching each tool schema. Arrays are actual array values, not automatically candidate lists for a scalar argument. Malformed/invalid dictionary content and wider path layouts need separate checks.
- **Example:** dictionary.json gives echo.text hello docs; CLI dictionary-cli.json gives hello CLI.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mcp).

**Placement**

- `mcp.test.dictionaryFile`

</details>

<details id="mcptestrunoptions-enableresiliencytests">
<summary><code>enableResiliencyTests</code> — Enable resiliency tests</summary>

Run resiliency tests against the MCP server.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** MCP auto testing through specmatic mcp test in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The checked transport is a local JSON streamable-HTTP endpoint; other integrations need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Expands each tool into generated positive and negative argument scenarios. Negative scenarios expect suitable error handling rather than a successful tool result.
- **When omitted:** False by default in both the model and effective CLI choice. The baseline recipe executes one positive echo scenario.
- **Precedence:** An explicitly supplied CLI --enable-resiliency-tests value wins over the config Boolean, including false.
- **Watch for:** The number of tests depends on the input schema. The released runner may return 0 despite failing scenarios, and some JSON-RPC errors are treated as accepted positive invocations. Inspect individual reports; this is not a guarantee of business correctness.
- **Example:** The demo schema produces one positive plus three negative scenarios; CLI false restores one positive scenario.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mcp).

**Placement**

- `mcp.test.enableResiliencyTests`

</details>

<details id="mcptestrunoptions-filtertools">
<summary><code>filterTools</code> — Included MCP tools</summary>

Tool names to include in MCP tests.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** MCP auto testing through specmatic mcp test in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The checked transport is a local JSON streamable-HTTP endpoint; other integrations need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Includes only fetched tools whose names are in the effective selection.
- **When omitted:** Absent, null or empty config selection means all fetched tools are candidates. CLI names can restrict that default; null/empty omission boundaries are source-backed.
- **Precedence:** Config and CLI --filter-tools names combine as a set. skipTools exclusions then apply to the included tools.
- **Watch for:** Names match exactly. An unknown-only selection or excluding every selected tool produces no scenarios and exit 1; only the exclusion-empty case is executed here. A tool that cannot produce scenarios can also be skipped during generation. The resolved schema rejects explicit null; omit the field or use an array.
- **Example:** Config echo plus CLI broken selects both tools, rather than replacing echo. This mixed-result run returns 0 despite a failure.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mcp).

**Placement**

- `mcp.test.filterTools`

</details>

<details id="mcptestrunoptions-skiptools">
<summary><code>skipTools</code> — Excluded MCP tools</summary>

Tool names to skip during MCP tests.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** MCP auto testing through specmatic mcp test in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The checked transport is a local JSON streamable-HTTP endpoint; other integrations need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Excludes named tools after inclusion filtering.
- **When omitted:** With no exclusions, no selected tool is removed by this list.
- **Precedence:** Config and CLI --skip-tools names combine as a set; exclusion wins when a tool is both included and skipped.
- **Watch for:** An empty final selection fails the run. CLI exclusion does not erase configured exclusions. Exact names are required, not patterns.
- **Example:** Including echo and broken, then skipping broken, tests only echo.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mcp).

**Placement**

- `mcp.test.skipTools`

</details>

<details id="mcptestrunoptions-transportkind">
<summary><code>transportKind</code> — MCP transport</summary>

Canonical MCP transport. Parser tolerance for alternate spellings is not part of the resolved language.

- **Value:** "STREAMABLE_HTTP".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** MCP auto testing through specmatic mcp test in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The checked transport is a local JSON streamable-HTTP endpoint; other integrations need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Selects the MCP test client transport. The released configuration vocabulary contains STREAMABLE_HTTP.
- **When omitted:** The model permits null, but without an effective CLI/config transport the test command fails. Schema acceptance of omission is not a usable default.
- **Precedence:** CLI --transport-kind wins over config. Config enum parsing tolerates unknown/case variations by falling back to STREAMABLE_HTTP, but the resolved schema requires its canonical spelling; this parser tolerance is source-only, not a recommended value.
- **Watch for:** JSON response handling is exercised here. Full SSE/session handling, stdio testing and other clients are not established. Do not confuse specmatic mcp server using stdio with this test transport.
- **Example:** Use transportKind: STREAMABLE_HTTP.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mcp).

**Placement**

- `mcp.test.transportKind`

</details>

## Mock Run Options

<details id="mockrunoptions-asyncapi">
<summary><code>asyncapi</code> — AsyncAPI mock options</summary>

AsyncAPI options for mocking a dependency.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Supplies AsyncAPI execution options for the mock service context.
- **When omitted:** No explicit options for this protocol are supplied by this field. Validation can construct default option objects, but that does not establish that the runner can execute without its required endpoint/broker inputs.
- **Precedence:** The service definition selects specs; the consumer selects this protocol's option branch. A service runOptions reference is converted for the consuming role. Matching per-spec overrides follow that protocol's rules.
- **Watch for:** This does not select a contract file or grant edition support. Different protocol branches may coexist for appropriate specs; do not combine test-only and mock-only fields in one branch. Follow the protocol guide for executable release/edition support.
- **Example:** Nest asyncapi under runOptions for the selected service, and supply the mock variant's fields.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/async-kafka).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.asyncapi`

## Mock Service

Dependency services provide specifications and settings for mocks.

Mock settings use service, then dependency-wide, then global values. Startup/hot-reload/restart accessors use dependency/global settings rather than a single service’s value.

<details id="mockservice-data">
<summary><code>data</code> — Mock data</summary>

Optional examples, dictionary, and adapter data for the mock service.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Supplies external examples, dictionary selection and adapter declarations for this mock dependency.
- **When omitted:** No service-level data is selected. Mock dependency data, CLI/environment inputs and implicit discovery can still contribute through their own consumers.
- **Precedence:** Core mock example directories combine service entries before dependency entries. Dictionary selection prefers the service dictionary over dependency data; core test dictionary selection is separate.
- **Watch for:** An absent data object, an empty examples list and a present invalid path are not equivalent. Shared dependency data can still contribute; adapter execution follows the invoking hook consumer, not schema acceptance alone.
- **Example:** Use data.examples for selected directory groups or refs, and data.dictionary for a dictionary object or ref.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `dependencies.services[].service.data`

</details>

<details id="mockservice-definitions">
<summary><code>definitions</code> — Service definitions</summary>

Specifications and their sources for this service.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Lists the sources and specs for this mock dependency. Each item has a definition wrapper.
- **When omitted:** Required in the resolved service shape. An empty list contributes no specification entries; it does not discover arbitrary files.
- **Precedence:** Inline definitions beside a service reference replace the referenced definitions list. Multiple definitions otherwise contribute their explicitly selected specs.
- **Watch for:** Source directory/repository and spec paths work together. Follow the path-resolution guide; a path-mapping helper alone does not prove a particular command uses config-relative paths.
- **Example:** Use definitions: [&#123;definition: &#123;source: &#123;filesystem: &#123;directory: .&#125;&#125;, specs: [books.yaml]&#125;&#125;].

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions`

</details>

<details id="mockservice-description">
<summary><code>description</code> — Service description</summary>

Optional human-readable description of the service.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Adds a human-readable description to this mock dependency.
- **When omitted:** Optional; no description is supplied. This field does not select a target, spec or mock.
- **Precedence:** An inline description beside a service reference replaces the component description.
- **Watch for:** Treat this as descriptive metadata. It is not a service ID, a spec ID or a guarantee that a runner will display the text.
- **Example:** Use description: Books backend; choose spec IDs separately in the definition spec entries.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `dependencies.services[].service.description`

</details>

<details id="mockservice-runoptions">
<summary><code>runOptions</code> — Mock run options</summary>

Optional mock run options or a reference to reusable options.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Selects protocol-specific execution options for this mock dependency, such as an OpenAPI test target or mock listener.
- **When omitted:** No service-level options are supplied. Core HTTP consumers can use their defaults or CLI inputs; other protocols may require explicit options.
- **Precedence:** A referenced options object is converted for the consuming test/mock role. A matching per-spec ID can select overrides with protocol-specific fallback rules.
- **Watch for:** These options do not select specs: definitions does that. Inline runOptions beside a service reference replaces the whole referenced runOptions field; do not assume recursive merging.
- **Example:** For a test service use runOptions.openapi.type: test and baseUrl; for a dependency use type: mock and port.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions`

</details>

<details id="mockservice-settings">
<summary><code>settings</code> — Mock settings</summary>

Optional mock settings or a reference to them.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Applies test or mock behavior settings to this mock dependency; reusable component settings acquire their role at the use site.
- **When omitted:** No service override is supplied. Test accessors fall back to global test defaults; mock accessors can also inherit dependencies.settings.
- **Precedence:** Resolve any service reference and its shallow overrides first. Then the selected service settings merge field by field with the applicable wider settings.
- **Watch for:** The service object takes role-specific settings directly, such as timeoutInMilliseconds for a test; it does not use the global test/mock wrapper. Runtime precedence still depends on the command and individual field.
- **Example:** Put settings: &#123;timeoutInMilliseconds: 2000&#125; on a tested service, or settings: &#123;strictMode: true&#125; on a mock dependency.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/mock-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `dependencies.services[].service.settings`

</details>

## Mock Service Entry

Dependency services provide specifications and settings for mocks.

Mock settings use service, then dependency-wide, then global values. Startup/hot-reload/restart accessors use dependency/global settings rather than a single service’s value.

<details id="mockserviceentry-service">
<summary><code>service</code> — Mock service</summary>

Inline mock service or reusable service reference.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Supplies one mock dependency inline or resolves a reusable service in mock context.
- **When omitted:** Required for each dependencies.services list item.
- **Precedence:** A component target is converted into the mock service shape. Inline fields beside $ref shallowly replace fields from the target.
- **Watch for:** The surrounding service wrapper is required even when its value is a reference. A component usable for testing still needs compatible mock options here.
- **Example:** Give one list item service: &#123;$ref: "#/components/services/booksBackend"&#125;.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `dependencies.services[].service`

</details>

## Mock Settings

Service settings override dependency-wide settings, which override global mock settings.

Global startup timeout defaults to 20 seconds; generative mocks default to false. Startup/hot-reload/restart getters use dependency/global settings. Other defaults must be checked per runner.

<details id="mocksettings-delayinmilliseconds">
<summary><code>delayInMilliseconds</code> — Response delay</summary>

Delay core HTTP mock responses by this many milliseconds unless the matched expectation supplies its own delay.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core HTTP mock settings in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners and Studio need their own applicability review.
- **Effect:** Adds a delay before the core HTTP response is sent, including ordinary authored-example responses when they have no own delay.
- **When omitted:** Missing configuration falls back to SPECMATIC_STUB_DELAY, environment before JVM property. With no selected value, the response sender does not add this configured delay.
- **Precedence:** For a matching spec, non-null service settings override dependency-wide settings, which override global specmatic.settings.mock, field by field. Explicit false or 0 is retained; omission falls back. Lifecycle consumers use a different scope. The core CLI --delay-in-ms writes a dependency-wide delay; an explicit service delay can still override it. An expectation/example-specific delay supplied to the response sender wins over config.
- **Watch for:** Use a nonnegative millisecond value. Zero requests no additional wait in this path. This is a response delay, not a request timeout or a guaranteed total response duration. Schema integer acceptance alone is not a portable meaning for a negative delay.
- **Example:** A service delay of 600 remains selected when the core CLI stages dependency-wide --delay-in-ms 20. Remove the service value to use the dependency-wide delay.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mock-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.delayInMilliseconds`
- `components.settings.<name>.delayInMilliseconds`
- `components.settings.<name>.mock.delayInMilliseconds`
- `dependencies.services[].service.settings.delayInMilliseconds`
- `dependencies.settings.delayInMilliseconds`
- `specmatic.settings.mock.delayInMilliseconds`

</details>

<details id="mocksettings-generative">
<summary><code>generative</code> — Generative mode</summary>

Controls selected contract-defined invalid-request response generation, including strict-mode fallback behavior.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core HTTP mock settings in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners and Studio need their own applicability review.
- **Effect:** Enables particular contract-defined invalid-request response generation paths. It also affects the strict-mode response chosen when no mock example matches.
- **When omitted:** The V3 accessor defaults to false after dependency/global/service selection. It has no environment/property fallback.
- **Precedence:** For a matching spec, non-null service settings override dependency-wide settings, which override global specmatic.settings.mock, field by field. Explicit false or 0 is retained; omission falls back. Lifecycle consumers use a different scope.
- **Watch for:** This is not the switch for all schema-generated success responses. With strictMode false, a valid request without a matching example can receive a schema-generated success response even when generative is false. With strictMode true and generative true, a matching invalid-request response definition is needed for the contract-defined error response; otherwise strict mode falls back to HTTP 400 text.
- **Example:** generative: false with strictMode: false can still return a generated HTTP 200 response. Set strictMode: true to require a matching example for that request path.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mock-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.generative`
- `components.settings.<name>.generative`
- `components.settings.<name>.mock.generative`
- `dependencies.services[].service.settings.generative`
- `dependencies.settings.generative`
- `specmatic.settings.mock.generative`

</details>

<details id="mocksettings-gracefulrestarttimeoutinmilliseconds">
<summary><code>gracefulRestartTimeoutInMilliseconds</code> — Graceful restart timeout</summary>

Maximum time in milliseconds allowed for a graceful mock restart.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core HTTP mock settings in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners and Studio need their own applicability review.
- **Effect:** Sets the core CLI HTTP server stop grace period, used when restarting or closing the mock. The server receives this duration as its grace period and stop timeout (subject to a separately supplied force timeout).
- **When omitted:** The V3 accessor returns no value when omitted; the core CLI uses 1,000 milliseconds.
- **Precedence:** Dependency-wide dependencies.settings overrides global specmatic.settings.mock field by field. The core lifecycle accessor does not consult individual service settings. CLI --graceful-restart-timeout-in-ms overrides the config value for the core CLI.
- **Watch for:** This is a server shutdown setting, not the delay before a new mock is ready or an HTTP test timeout. Service-level values do not change the core lifecycle accessor. Choose a nonnegative duration; schema integer acceptance does not establish useful semantics for negative values.
- **Example:** dependencies.settings.gracefulRestartTimeoutInMilliseconds: 2000 gives the core CLI server a 2-second stop grace period.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.gracefulRestartTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `components.settings.<name>.gracefulRestartTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `components.settings.<name>.mock.gracefulRestartTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `dependencies.services[].service.settings.gracefulRestartTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `dependencies.settings.gracefulRestartTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `specmatic.settings.mock.gracefulRestartTimeoutInMilliseconds`

</details>

<details id="mocksettings-hotreload">
<summary><code>hotReload</code> — Hot reload</summary>

Reload contracts when their source files change.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core HTTP mock settings in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners and Studio need their own applicability review.
- **Effect:** Enables the core CLI watcher and restart when its watched files change. Use false when you want the mock to stay on its loaded state until manually restarted.
- **When omitted:** The V3 accessor returns no value if omitted; the core CLI defaults to enabled.
- **Precedence:** Dependency-wide dependencies.settings overrides global specmatic.settings.mock field by field. The core lifecycle accessor does not consult individual service settings. CLI --hot-reload enabled/disabled overrides the configured lifecycle choice. The config key itself is Boolean: true or false.
- **Watch for:** The CLI registers contract paths and CLI example directories with its watcher. This does not prove that every referenced config/data file is watched. A service-level hotReload value does not change the lifecycle selection. Programmatic launchers do not automatically gain the CLI watcher.
- **Example:** dependencies.settings.hotReload: false disables the CLI watcher even if global hotReload is true.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.hotReload`
- `components.settings.<name>.hotReload`
- `components.settings.<name>.mock.hotReload`
- `dependencies.services[].service.settings.hotReload`
- `dependencies.settings.hotReload`
- `specmatic.settings.mock.hotReload`

</details>

<details id="mocksettings-lenientmode">
<summary><code>lenientMode</code> — Lenient mode</summary>

Allow lenient validation behavior in the mock.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core HTTP mock settings in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners and Studio need their own applicability review.
- **Effect:** Allows selected core OpenAPI parser/collector failures to be reported while loading proceeds. It does not disable request matching or all validation.
- **When omitted:** The V3 mock accessor has no environment/property fallback. Core spec loading uses false when neither the selected service configuration nor the source entry supplies a mode.
- **Precedence:** For a matching spec, non-null service settings override dependency-wide settings, which override global specmatic.settings.mock, field by field. Explicit false or 0 is retained; omission falls back. Lifecycle consumers use a different scope. A non-null ContractPathData.lenientMode overrides the config value during core spec loading. The CLI --lenient supplies that source-entry mode; omission leaves the config available.
- **Watch for:** Leniency applies only to specific parser recovery paths; unusable contracts or later validation can still fail. Keep the original errors visible. strictMode and lenientMode are independent, and false is not omission.
- **Example:** Use a service lenientMode value for config-driven loading, or CLI --lenient to supply the source-entry parsing mode for that run.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.lenientMode`
- `components.settings.<name>.lenientMode`
- `components.settings.<name>.mock.lenientMode`
- `dependencies.services[].service.settings.lenientMode`
- `dependencies.settings.lenientMode`
- `specmatic.settings.mock.lenientMode`

</details>

<details id="mocksettings-starttimeoutinmilliseconds">
<summary><code>startTimeoutInMilliseconds</code> — Mock start timeout</summary>

Maximum time in milliseconds allowed for the mock to start.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core HTTP mock settings in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners and Studio need their own applicability review.
- **Effect:** Bounds particular mock startup/readiness waits. The core createStub path wraps startup work; the CLI readiness check waits for the HTTP mock and listener connectability.
- **When omitted:** The V3 lifecycle accessor uses 20,000 milliseconds when dependency/global settings omit it.
- **Precedence:** Dependency-wide dependencies.settings overrides global specmatic.settings.mock field by field. The core lifecycle accessor does not consult individual service settings.
- **Watch for:** Placing this key only on a service does not change the core lifecycle accessor. It is not an HTTP request timeout, response delay or guaranteed wall-clock budget for the entire CLI command. CLI readiness can perform more than one wait with this timeout. Use a positive duration.
- **Example:** Put startTimeoutInMilliseconds: 30000 under dependencies.settings to allow the core lifecycle waits a longer limit.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.startTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `components.settings.<name>.mock.startTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `components.settings.<name>.startTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `dependencies.services[].service.settings.startTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `dependencies.settings.startTimeoutInMilliseconds`
- `specmatic.settings.mock.startTimeoutInMilliseconds`

</details>

<details id="mocksettings-strictmode">
<summary><code>strictMode</code> — Strict mode</summary>

Enable strict validation behavior in the mock.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core HTTP mock settings in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate protocol runners and Studio need their own applicability review.
- **Effect:** In core request handling, requires a matching mock expectation instead of ordinary schema-generated success fallback. Also participates in loading, example checks and substitutions.
- **When omitted:** Falls back to SPECMATIC_STUB_STRICT_MODE from the environment, then JVM property. Core CLI startup and response handling use false if no value is supplied.
- **Precedence:** For a matching spec, non-null service settings override dependency-wide settings, which override global specmatic.settings.mock, field by field. Explicit false or 0 is retained; omission falls back. Lifecycle consumers use a different scope. The core CLI --strict stages strictness at dependency scope; an explicit service false can still control request handling. Startup/loading paths that call getStubStrictMode(null) use dependency/global values instead of a service value.
- **Watch for:** Strict mode has several consumers. A CLI strict startup/loading check and a service request policy can therefore differ. A matching example can still return successfully. With no match, generative may select a contract-defined invalid-request response; otherwise strict fallback is HTTP 400 text. It is not lenientMode's inverse.
- **Example:** Dependency strictMode true and service strictMode false allow ordinary generated success responses for that service; reversing those values makes unmatched requests use strict behavior.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mock-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.strictMode`
- `components.settings.<name>.mock.strictMode`
- `components.settings.<name>.strictMode`
- `dependencies.services[].service.settings.strictMode`
- `dependencies.settings.strictMode`
- `specmatic.settings.mock.strictMode`

</details>

## Open Api Mock Run Options

Mock run options choose the listener origin; OpenAPI also supports stateful-mock.

baseUrl takes precedence over host/port. A configured certificate selects HTTPS. OpenAPI logMode/logsDirPath are schema-declared, but no runtime reader was confirmed in the source snapshot.

<details id="openapimockrunoptions-baseurl">
<summary><code>baseUrl</code> — Mock base URL</summary>

Base URL on which the mock service listens.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Supplies the configured mock server origin as a URL.
- **When omitted:** The model derives an origin from port/host when port is present; otherwise it returns no configured origin and the command chooses its fallback.
- **Precedence:** A non-null baseUrl is selected ahead of host and port by this model. A matching per-spec origin can be selected by the calling consumer.
- **Watch for:** This is a listener origin in mock context, not the target implementation URL used for testing. Validate the URL and protocol-specific binding requirements.
- **Example:** Use baseUrl: <code>http://127.0.0.1:9101</code> for a local mock origin.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.baseUrl`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.baseUrl`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.baseUrl`

</details>

<details id="openapimockrunoptions-cert">
<summary><code>cert</code> — Mock certificate</summary>

TLS identity settings or a reference to a named certificate.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI mock CLI and contract-test client in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. SOAP/proxy and other consumers need separate execution checks.
- **Effect:** Mock uses server identity and optional incoming-client requirement.
- **When omitted:** Omission leaves no configured identity; core mock can use CLI wildcard credentials. HTTPS testing without client credentials can connect to a service that does not require them.
- **Precedence:** The selected service run options resolve cert before the endpoint registry is built; per-spec endpoint overrides affect the host/port association but do not create a per-spec cert field. Source-traced certificate merge is field-dependent: explicit config keyStorePassword and mtlsEnabled are retained; nested file/directory, alias and private-key password accept non-null CLI overrides. Do not generalize a CLI-wins rule. The recipe demonstrates that a correct CLI store password does not replace an explicit wrong config store password.
- **Watch for:** Mock incoming mTLS requires a client certificate but uses an insecure trust manager; the independent self-signed client succeeds. Core test HTTPS uses TrustAllStrategy and NoopHostnameVerifier in the reviewed source. These settings do not establish production certificate trust or hostname validation. Multiple identity/endpoint conflicts and other integrations remain unexecuted.
- **Example:** Place server cert in dependencies for mocking, client cert in systemUnderTest for testing; reference #/components/certificates/demoTls where appropriate.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/https).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.cert`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.cert`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.cert`

</details>

<details id="openapimockrunoptions-filter">
<summary><code>filter</code> — Mock filter</summary>

Expression used to select mocked operations.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Supplies the core OpenAPI mock filter for the service containing the selected spec file.
- **When omitted:** The per-file accessor returns no configured filter if no matching OpenAPI service options are found.
- **Precedence:** The accessor reads the matching service OpenAPI filter. Command-staged mock filters can update service options before use.
- **Watch for:** This does not add/remove spec declarations from definitions. Confirm actual mocked operations; do not infer matching from a startup banner.
- **Example:** Configure filter under the dependency service runOptions.openapi and check the intended routes.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/mock-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.filter`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.filter`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.filter`

</details>

<details id="openapimockrunoptions-host">
<summary><code>host</code> — Mock host</summary>

Host on which the mock service listens.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Supplies the bind host used with a configured mock port.
- **When omitted:** With port present and baseUrl absent, the model defaults the host to 0.0.0.0. Host alone does not produce a complete server origin.
- **Precedence:** baseUrl wins over host/port in the model. The selected per-spec origin and actual command staging can affect the final listener.
- **Watch for:** 0.0.0.0 can expose the listener beyond the local machine. Use 127.0.0.1 for a loopback-only example.
- **Example:** Use host: 127.0.0.1 with port: 9101.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.host`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.host`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.host`

</details>

<details id="openapimockrunoptions-logmode">
<summary><code>logMode</code> — Mock log mode</summary>

Select the mock-service events written to logs.

- **Value:** "ALL", "REQUEST_RESPONSE".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Stores the OpenAPI logMode option in the model; a consuming integration must explicitly read it.
- **When omitted:** No model value is supplied. The recorded core V3 virtual-service accessor getVirtualServiceLogMode returns null even when this field is present.
- **Precedence:** No effective core CLI override order is established for this field; do not equate an accepted value with enabled logging.
- **Watch for:** The no-value core accessor does not prove that every Enterprise/Studio consumer ignores the field. That wider consumer review remains open.
- **Example:** Use the documented general logging controls for core diagnostic output.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/how-config-is-used).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.logMode`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.logMode`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.logMode`

</details>

<details id="openapimockrunoptions-logsdirpath">
<summary><code>logsDirPath</code> — Logs directory</summary>

Directory where mock-service logs are written.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Stores the OpenAPI logsDirPath option in the model; a consuming integration must explicitly read it.
- **When omitted:** No model value is supplied. The recorded core V3 virtual-service accessor getVirtualServiceLogsDirPath returns null even when this field is present.
- **Precedence:** No effective core CLI override order is established for this field; do not equate an accepted value with enabled logging.
- **Watch for:** The no-value core accessor does not prove that every Enterprise/Studio consumer ignores the field. That wider consumer review remains open.
- **Example:** Use general.logging.text.directory for the documented core text-log directory.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/how-config-is-used).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.logsDirPath`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.logsDirPath`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.logsDirPath`

</details>

<details id="openapimockrunoptions-port">
<summary><code>port</code> — Mock port</summary>

Port on which the mock service listens.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Supplies the mock listener port when baseUrl is not selected.
- **When omitted:** Without baseUrl and port, the model returns no configured origin; the invoking command supplies its listener default.
- **Precedence:** baseUrl wins over port in the model. Matching per-spec options and command inputs have their own tested precedence.
- **Watch for:** A configured port must be available. This field is not a test-service destination port and does not prove one listener per spec in every runner.
- **Example:** Use port: 9101 with a loopback host for the local mock.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.port`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.port`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.port`

</details>

<details id="openapimockrunoptions-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specification overrides</summary>

Optional per-specification run-option overrides, joined by specification ID.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Lists per-spec OpenAPI option overrides, joined to definition specs by exact spec ID.
- **When omitted:** No per-spec overrides are supplied; applicable service options and consumer fallbacks remain.
- **Precedence:** The first exact matching ID is selected. Individual override fields then use the OpenAPI consumer's own fallback rules; no universal deep merge is implied.
- **Watch for:** These entries do not select additional specification files. An ID mismatch can leave a structurally valid override unused.
- **Example:** Use specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: books-api, port: 9102&#125;&#125;] and the same books-api ID in the definition.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs`

</details>

<details id="openapimockrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Mock mode</summary>

Mock mode discriminator.

- **Value:** "mock", "stateful-mock".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Selects ordinary mock or stateful-mock mode in the matching core mock-mode accessor.
- **When omitted:** Directly typed mock options can omit it; the core mock-mode accessor chooses ordinary mock unless stateful-mock is selected.
- **Precedence:** The explicit value must be mock or stateful-mock in this branch. It is separate from the selected spec IDs and listener address.
- **Watch for:** Schema/model acceptance of stateful-mock does not establish edition, entry-point or stateful-workflow support. The local recipe verifies ordinary mock mode.
- **Example:** Use type: mock for the complete local mock recipe.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.type`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.type`

</details>

## Open Api Run Option Specification

<details id="openapirunoptionspecification-actuatorurl">
<summary><code>actuatorUrl</code> — Actuator URL</summary>

Actuator endpoint URL override for this specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs[].spec.actuatorUrl`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.actuatorUrl`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.actuatorUrl`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.actuatorUrl`

</details>

<details id="openapirunoptionspecification-baseurl">
<summary><code>baseUrl</code> — Service base URL</summary>

Base URL override for this specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 specification declaration and core OpenAPI endpoint selection. Released execution evidence covers mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; test targets and separate protocols retain their own consumer rules.
- **Effect:** Supplies the endpoint for this matching OpenAPI specification override. For a mock this selects where the spec is served; for a test it supplies the target source endpoint.
- **When omitted:** With no baseUrl, this override needs port to construct an endpoint; otherwise endpoint selection falls back to the service protocol options.
- **Precedence:** A non-null override baseUrl wins over host/port in the same override and over the service endpoint. This is endpoint selection, not a recursive merge. In the checked configuration-driven core CLI test, --testBaseURL is staged separately while targets are loaded from the file; matching per-spec endpoints remain selected. Do not infer that the staged service value becomes the file-loaded endpoint for other specs.
- **Watch for:** The resolved schema requires a URI string. An empty string is present to the accessor, not omission; do not use it to request fallback. CLI and downstream endpoint adjustments need their own checks.
- **Example:** Set baseUrl to a loopback HTTP endpoint on port 9102 and port to 9103: the checked mock serves authors on 9102.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs[].spec.baseUrl`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.baseUrl`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.baseUrl`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.baseUrl`

</details>

<details id="openapirunoptionspecification-host">
<summary><code>host</code> — Service host</summary>

Host override for this specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 specification declaration and core OpenAPI endpoint selection. Released execution evidence covers mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; test targets and separate protocols retain their own consumer rules.
- **Effect:** Supplies the host when the matching OpenAPI override constructs an endpoint from its port.
- **When omitted:** With an override port and no host, source selection uses 0.0.0.0 for mocking or localhost for testing; it does not inherit the service host.
- **Precedence:** Override baseUrl bypasses host/port. A host without an override port supplies no endpoint, so source selection falls back to service run options.
- **Watch for:** These are source-selection defaults, not proof of all CLI/bind-address behavior. Empty host is retained by the accessor. Provide host and port together for an explicit endpoint.
- **Example:** Set host: 127.0.0.1 and port: 9102 together in the authors override.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs[].spec.host`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.host`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.host`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.host`

</details>

<details id="openapirunoptionspecification-id">
<summary><code>id</code> — Specification ID</summary>

Specification identifier this override targets.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 specification declaration and core OpenAPI endpoint selection. Released execution evidence covers mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; test targets and separate protocols retain their own consumer rules.
- **Effect:** Selects the already declared specification to which this OpenAPI override applies. The override list does not select additional files.
- **When omitted:** Without an ID matching a declaration, this entry is not selected by the core ID-based endpoint lookup.
- **Precedence:** The first entry whose id exactly equals the declaration ID wins. Case differences do not match; duplicates do not combine.
- **Watch for:** Schema-valid IDs do not prove a successful join. Mismatched and duplicate IDs validate in the checked releases; use a single correctly spelled override per spec.
- **Example:** An authors declaration plus an Authors override uses the service endpoint; correcting it to authors selects port 9102.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs[].spec.id`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.id`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.id`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.id`

</details>

<details id="openapirunoptionspecification-overlayfilepath">
<summary><code>overlayFilePath</code> — Overlay file path</summary>

Path to an overlay applied to this specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs[].spec.overlayFilePath`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.overlayFilePath`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.overlayFilePath`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.overlayFilePath`

</details>

<details id="openapirunoptionspecification-port">
<summary><code>port</code> — Service port</summary>

Port override for this specification.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 specification declaration and core OpenAPI endpoint selection. Released execution evidence covers mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; test targets and separate protocols retain their own consumer rules.
- **Effect:** Constructs a per-spec OpenAPI endpoint together with the override host when no override baseUrl is supplied.
- **When omitted:** No port means the override cannot construct a host/port endpoint. If it also has no baseUrl, the service endpoint is used.
- **Precedence:** Override baseUrl wins over this port. Otherwise the selected override’s port supplies its endpoint instead of the service port.
- **Watch for:** This must be an integer at the resolved-schema boundary. Host is not inherited field by field from the service: omitted override host uses the mock/test accessor default. Port 0 is retained by the accessor; actual transport behavior is separate.
- **Example:** Keep books on service port 9101 and set the authors override host to 127.0.0.1 and port to 9102.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs[].spec.port`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.port`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.port`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.port`

</details>

<details id="openapirunoptionspecification-securityschemes">
<summary><code>securitySchemes</code> — Security schemes</summary>

Credentials keyed by the OpenAPI security-scheme name for a matching spec.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI CLI contract-test credential generation in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, with the separate core mock boundary demonstrated. Other integrations and authored example/dictionary interactions need independent review.
- **Effect:** Associates the matching test spec with credential adapters. Both the spec ID and scheme name must match; metadata does not declare new OpenAPI security requirements.
- **When omitted:** Optional. Without a selected map/entry, scheme-named/global environment/property and generated/dictionary credentials can be used; generated values are not issued credentials.
- **Precedence:** Resolve config templates first. A compatible nonblank configured token wins; otherwise use the exact scheme-named environment variable, then JVM property. Bearer/OAuth/OIDC additionally fall back to SPECMATIC_OAUTH2_TOKEN; Basic to SPECMATIC_BASIC_AUTH_TOKEN. Flags lookup uses environment before JVM property. Dictionary/generated request values can follow when no configured/environment credential exists. Example-level credentials have additional consumers; this is not universal request precedence. An empty scheme-named environment value is present and suppresses the JVM/global fallback; this differs from a blank config token being skipped.
- **Watch for:** The V3 map lookup selects systemUnderTest and matching spec ID, then exact scheme name. Schema-accepted dependency maps do not establish mock token authentication. The checked mock permits an absent bearer header and arbitrary bearer token. Cookie API keys, AND/OR requirements, authorization scopes, refresh/login, external examples/dictionaries, JVM/global fallbacks, null/missing binding cases and wider consumers remain unexecuted.
- **Example:** Definition id auth-demo and run-option id auth-demo select BearerAuth, named exactly as components.securitySchemes.BearerAuth in the contract.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/authentication).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes`

</details>

<details id="openapirunoptionspecificationentry-spec">

Per-specification OpenAPI run-option values.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Wraps one OpenAPI per-specification option object, including its ID and selected override fields.
- **When omitted:** Required within every specs override list item.
- **Precedence:** The contained ID is matched against a definition spec ID; the wrapper is not itself a ref to an indexed definition array.
- **Watch for:** Place id and the override values inside spec. Preserve the source/spec declaration separately.
- **Example:** Use - spec: with id: books-api and the intended override nested below it.
