## Open Api Test Run Options

Test run options choose the service endpoint and protocol-specific behavior.

A per-spec origin overrides the service origin. baseUrl takes precedence over host/port; host alone does not establish an origin. A certificate reference supplies TLS material.

<details id="openapitestrunoptions-actuatorurl">
<summary><code>actuatorUrl</code> — Actuator URL</summary>

URL of the service actuator endpoint used by test support.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.actuatorUrl`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.actuatorUrl`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.actuatorUrl`

</details>

<details id="openapitestrunoptions-baseurl">
<summary><code>baseUrl</code> — Service base URL</summary>

Base URL used when sending test requests.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 OpenAPI system-under-test service options in configuration-driven core CLI HTTP testing, OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Other protocols and direct contract-path/JUnit entry points need their own target-selection review.
- **Effect:** Selects the tested service URL, including its scheme and optional base path. The service endpoint becomes the target for declared specs without their own matching endpoint override.
- **When omitted:** With no baseUrl, a configured port constructs an HTTP endpoint with the selected host or localhost. Without either endpoint form, the CLI/JUnit default-target path tries its base-URL input, explicit CLI host/port, then its config lookup, finally <code>http://localhost:9000</code>.
- **Precedence:** A non-null service baseUrl wins over host/port in that object; a matching per-spec endpoint wins over the service endpoint. In the checked configuration-driven run, file-loaded endpoints stay selected despite a competing testBaseURL environment value or --testBaseURL flag. With no file-loaded endpoint, the fallback reads explicit CLI base URL, then testBaseURL environment, then the same JVM property. CLI staging is separate from reloading specification targets from the file.
- **Watch for:** Do not assume a CLI flag reroutes configured targets. Use a template in the file or omit the file endpoint to let the fallback choose. The runner validates its fallback URL even when specs have configured targets, so an invalid testBaseURL input can fail before requests. Schema URI acceptance does not prove reachability or HTTP transport compatibility; null is not a valid resolved-schema string.
- **Example:** Set baseUrl: <code>http://127.0.0.1:9100</code> with port: 9102: requests use 9100. To vary it by environment, use the complete BOOKS_TARGET URL-template recipe; supplying its target on 9102 changes the endpoint loaded from the file.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/test-targets).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.baseUrl`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.baseUrl`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.baseUrl`

</details>

<details id="openapitestrunoptions-cert">
<summary><code>cert</code> — Test certificate</summary>

TLS identity settings or a reference to a named certificate.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI mock CLI and contract-test client in OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. SOAP/proxy and other consumers need separate execution checks.
- **Effect:** Test uses outgoing client identity when the target requests a client certificate.
- **When omitted:** Omission leaves no configured identity; core mock can use CLI wildcard credentials. HTTPS testing without client credentials can connect to a service that does not require them.
- **Precedence:** The selected service run options resolve cert before the endpoint registry is built; per-spec endpoint overrides affect the host/port association but do not create a per-spec cert field. Source-traced certificate merge is field-dependent: explicit config keyStorePassword and mtlsEnabled are retained; nested file/directory, alias and private-key password accept non-null CLI overrides. Do not generalize a CLI-wins rule. The recipe demonstrates that a correct CLI store password does not replace an explicit wrong config store password.
- **Watch for:** Mock incoming mTLS requires a client certificate but uses an insecure trust manager; the independent self-signed client succeeds. Core test HTTPS uses TrustAllStrategy and NoopHostnameVerifier in the reviewed source. These settings do not establish production certificate trust or hostname validation. Multiple identity/endpoint conflicts and other integrations remain unexecuted.
- **Example:** Place server cert in dependencies for mocking, client cert in systemUnderTest for testing; reference #/components/certificates/demoTls where appropriate.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/https).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.cert`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.cert`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.cert`

</details>

<details id="openapitestrunoptions-filter">
<summary><code>filter</code> — Test filter</summary>

Expression used to select generated tests.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Supplies the core OpenAPI test filter expression.
- **When omitted:** The core accessor falls back to the environment variable named filter, then the Java property named filter.
- **Precedence:** The configured service filter is selected first in this accessor. A command can stage its own filter into that configuration.
- **Watch for:** Filter expressions and filtering by test name are different controls. An empty selection is not evidence that all intended operations passed.
- **Example:** Use the tested test-settings guide to choose an operation filter and verify the selected test count.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/test-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.filter`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.filter`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.filter`

</details>

<details id="openapitestrunoptions-host">
<summary><code>host</code> — Service host</summary>

Host used when sending test requests.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 OpenAPI system-under-test service options in configuration-driven core CLI HTTP testing, OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Other protocols and direct contract-path/JUnit entry points need their own target-selection review.
- **Effect:** Supplies the hostname for the tested service when constructing an HTTP URL from a configured port. It is a request destination, not a mock listener bind address.
- **When omitted:** If port is configured and baseUrl is absent, the service model uses localhost. Host alone, with no port and no baseUrl, supplies no service endpoint; the runner proceeds through its target fallback.
- **Precedence:** A service baseUrl bypasses this field. Without baseUrl, host and port form one service endpoint. A matching per-spec endpoint can replace that whole endpoint; its omitted host does not inherit the service host. CLI/environment fallback settings have separate selection rules.
- **Watch for:** Use a hostname or address without a scheme here; use baseUrl for a full URL. An empty host is a supplied value rather than localhost at model selection and can fail URL/transport checks. The resolved schema rejects explicit null. This host does not configure all protocols or make arbitrary environment host variables universal overrides.
- **Example:** With host: 127.0.0.1 and port: 9102, the service target is <code>http://127.0.0.1:9102</code>. Without host, the constructed target uses localhost.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/test-targets).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.host`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.host`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.host`

</details>

<details id="openapitestrunoptions-port">
<summary><code>port</code> — Service port</summary>

Port used when sending test requests.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 OpenAPI system-under-test service options in configuration-driven core CLI HTTP testing, OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Other protocols and direct contract-path/JUnit entry points need their own target-selection review.
- **Effect:** Supplies the HTTP test target port when the service has no baseUrl. Use it together with host when a simple HTTP endpoint is sufficient.
- **When omitted:** With no port and no baseUrl, the service model produces no endpoint even if host is present. The runner can then use its separate CLI/environment/property fallback, eventually <code>http://localhost:9000</code>. A baseUrl can supply its own explicit or scheme-default port.
- **Precedence:** Service baseUrl wins over this port. Otherwise the port combines with host or localhost to construct an HTTP URL. A matching per-spec endpoint replaces the service endpoint. An explicit --port does not reroute a file-loaded configured service endpoint in the checked CLI recipe.
- **Watch for:** Use a literal integer or a structured template that retains an integer. Schema integer acceptance alone does not guarantee a reachable or valid TCP port. Explicit 0 is a value, not omission or a request to select localhost:9000; unlike mock listening, test destination port 0 is not a useful auto-allocation mechanism.
- **Example:** Omit baseUrl and set host: 127.0.0.1, port: 9102 to send the checked book request to 9102. Prefer baseUrl when selecting HTTPS or a URL base path.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/test-targets).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.port`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.port`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.port`

</details>

<details id="openapitestrunoptions-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specification overrides</summary>

Optional per-specification run-option overrides, joined by specification ID.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Lists per-spec OpenAPI option overrides, joined to definition specs by exact spec ID.
- **When omitted:** No per-spec overrides are supplied; applicable service options and consumer fallbacks remain.
- **Precedence:** The first exact matching ID is selected. Individual override fields then use the OpenAPI consumer's own fallback rules; no universal deep merge is implied.
- **Watch for:** These entries do not select additional specification files. An ID mismatch can leave a structurally valid override unused.
- **Example:** Use specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: books-api, port: 9102&#125;&#125;] and the same books-api ID in the definition.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs`

</details>

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core OpenAPI configuration/model behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. Separate command integrations and stateful features need independent verification.
- **Effect:** Identifies the test variant when explicitly supplied.
- **When omitted:** Optional in directly typed test options: the enclosing service test context already selects OpenApiTestConfig.
- **Precedence:** The only accepted explicit value here is test. A reusable context-dependent OpenAPI component has a separate required discriminator.

## Protobuf Common Run Options

Protobuf options configure gRPC targets/listeners, import paths, compiler version and test timeout.

Test DTO host/port are required and timeout defaults to 5000 ms. The mock service DTO does not require host. A map-based mock parser used by validation instead returns the test DTO; this is a recorded product limitation.

<details id="protobufcommonrunoptions-importpaths">
<summary><code>importPaths</code> — Protobuf import paths</summary>

Directories searched for imported Protobuf definitions.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Supplies directories searched for imported message/enum definitions and compiler inputs.
- **When omitted:** DTO omission uses IMPORT_PATHS from environment before JVM property, or an empty list. The compiler adds its own proto-parent path; the message parser does not thereby gain that path. Explicit [] retains an empty parser list.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching spec override replaces the service protobuf option map. Missing option fields are not inherited; an empty override map retains the service map. Mock appends explicit CLI import paths to its selected list. Test conversion and CLI staging have their own selection, not a universal merge.
- **Watch for:** Relative entries resolve from the main proto directory in the reviewed compiler/parser consumer; ordinary loading does not automatically use the config directory. Nonexistent compiler search paths can be skipped. Nested, remote, environment and CLI path combinations remain open. Enterprise validate --spec-file also uses selected compiler/import options. The checked imported-contract validation uses complete test options; the basic mock-only options hit a separate converter failure.
- **Example:** Use importPaths: ["."] when messages.proto sits beside imported-greeter.proto.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.importPaths`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.importPaths`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.importPaths`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.importPaths`

</details>

<details id="protobufcommonrunoptions-port">
<summary><code>port</code> — gRPC service port</summary>

Port used by the gRPC service.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC CLI mock/test consumers. Shared V3 schema acceptance does not establish OSS gRPC execution.
- **Effect:** Sets the gRPC mock listener port or the tested service port, according to the selected role.
- **When omitted:** Mock without a configured/staged port tries 9000 and may choose a free port when that default is occupied. A convertible gRPC test DTO requires host and port; do not rely on its programmatic fallback of port 0 to make an incomplete file usable.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching per-spec option map replaces the service protobuf option map; missing fields are not inherited. An empty override map retains the service map. The gRPC mock stages an explicit CLI --port ahead of its selected config port. A complete configured test target stays selected despite a competing CLI port.
- **Watch for:** The mock selects one first-convertible config and uses one listener for the selected proto set, rather than one listener per spec. Missing/partial test-target and occupied-default cases are source-traced, not established by the local recipe.
- **Example:** Mock port 9101 moves to 9102 with the checked CLI override; the configured test on 9100 stays on 9100 with CLI port 9102.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.port`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.port`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.port`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.port`

</details>

<details id="protobufcommonrunoptions-protocversion">
<summary><code>protocVersion</code> — protoc version</summary>

Version of protoc used to compile Protobuf definitions.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC CLI mock/test consumers. Shared V3 schema acceptance does not establish OSS gRPC execution.
- **Effect:** Selects the compiler version used to create the descriptor set from the proto contract.
- **When omitted:** With no selected value, the DTO/compiler uses PROTOC_VERSION from the environment before the JVM property, then compiler default 3.11.4.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching per-spec option map replaces the service protobuf option map; missing fields are not inherited. An empty override map retains the service map. An explicit gRPC mock CLI compiler version replaces its selected value; test staging is command-specific and does not universally displace a complete configured compiler value.
- **Watch for:** Compilation can download a platform-specific compiler. The default failed on the checked Apple Silicon machine with Unsupported platform; explicit 3.23.4 worked. This does not prove every platform/version or import layout. Enterprise validate --spec-file also uses selected compiler options without calling the test application; a passing config-structure check does not perform proto compilation.
- **Example:** Use protocVersion: "3.23.4" in the complete mock/test setup; this is unrelated to version: 3 and the Specmatic release.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.protocVersion`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.protocVersion`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.protocVersion`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.protocVersion`

</details>

## Protobuf Mock Run Options

Protobuf options configure gRPC targets/listeners, import paths, compiler version and test timeout.

Test DTO host/port are required and timeout defaults to 5000 ms. The mock service DTO does not require host. A map-based mock parser used by validation instead returns the test DTO; this is a recorded product limitation.

<details id="protobufmockrunoptions-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specification overrides</summary>

Optional per-specification Protobuf overrides.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Lists protobuf run-option overrides keyed by declared specification IDs. It changes options, not the list of contracts to load.
- **When omitted:** Without this list or without a matching ID, the service protobuf options remain selected.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching spec override replaces the service protobuf option map. Missing option fields are not inherited; an empty override map retains the service map. The first matching ID is selected by the core run-option lookup.
- **Watch for:** Declare specs in definitions; do not use this list to add contract paths. Duplicate/mismatched IDs, empty maps and multiple-spec behavior require separate checks.
- **Example:** Give the declared proto ID greeting and add specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: greeting, port: 9102, protocVersion: "3.23.4"&#125;&#125;]; a test override also supplies host.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.specs`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.specs`

</details>

<details id="protobufmockrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Mock mode</summary>

Mock branch discriminator.

- **Value:** exactly "mock".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Declares mock protobuf run options for the corresponding service role.
- **When omitted:** The inline role-specific model permits omission and retains its role; reusable component run options require an explicit discriminator.
- **Precedence:** A tested service requires test options; a dependency uses mock options. Type does not turn a dependency declaration into a system under test.
- **Watch for:** Use the explicit role in a copyable starter. Omitted-mode runtime behavior and every ref form are not established by the local recipe. In Enterprise 1.28.3 validate --spec-file, basic mock options are converted through GrpcMockConfig.from(Map), which returns a GrpcTestConfig and fails on missing host. The schema accepts extra properties, but host is not a declared top-level mock option or a mock listener selector. Use a separate complete test-role validation config rather than relying on the erroneous converter.
- **Example:** Use type: mock under that service's runOptions.protobuf.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.type`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.type`

</details>

## Protobuf Mock Run Option Specification Entry

<details id="protobufmockrunoptionspecificationentry-spec">
<summary><code>spec</code> — Protobuf mock run-option override</summary>

Per-specification Protobuf mock run-option values.

- **Value:** object + see variant constraints.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Wraps one ID-keyed override object inside the protobuf specs list.
- **When omitted:** Each authored list entry must include the spec wrapper; omit the entire list when no override is needed.
- **Precedence:** The wrapped id is matched to the declared spec before its options are selected. A nonempty matching spec override replaces the service protobuf option map. Missing option fields are not inherited; an empty override map retains the service map.
- **Watch for:** This is an override object, not a path string or another definition wrapper. Schema validity alone does not prove an ID matched.
- **Example:** Use a list item with spec: &#123;id: greeting, port: 9102, protocVersion: "3.23.4"&#125;; test also includes host.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs[].spec`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec`

</details>

## Protobuf Run Option Specification Common

Protobuf options configure gRPC targets/listeners, import paths, compiler version and test timeout.

Test DTO host/port are required and timeout defaults to 5000 ms. The mock service DTO does not require host. A map-based mock parser used by validation instead returns the test DTO; this is a recorded product limitation.

<details id="protobufrunoptionspecificationcommon-host">
<summary><code>host</code> — Service host</summary>

Host override for this specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Selects the test target host in a matching protobuf override. The gRPC mock DTO has no host field.
- **When omitted:** An empty override retains service options. A nonempty test map lacking host is not a complete convertible test DTO; it does not inherit the outer host.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching spec override replaces the service protobuf option map. Missing option fields are not inherited; an empty override map retains the service map. A convertible configured test target precedes staged host fallback.
- **Watch for:** The mock ignores this shared host value and its server is created by port; it does not select a loopback listener. Partial targets and explicit host differences remain execution-pending.
- **Example:** Repeat host: 127.0.0.1 with port: 9102 and compiler 3.23.4 in the complete test override.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs[].spec.host`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.host`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.host`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.host`

</details>

<details id="protobufrunoptionspecificationcommon-id">
<summary><code>id</code> — Specification ID</summary>

Specification identifier this override targets.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Matches a protobuf override to the ID on the declared contract.
- **When omitted:** An unnamed declaration cannot select a named override through its filename; unmatched overrides leave service options selected.
- **Precedence:** Core lookup compares the declared ID directly and selects its first matching override. A nonempty matching spec override replaces the service protobuf option map. Missing option fields are not inherited; an empty override map retains the service map.
- **Watch for:** Matching greeting is checked. Case/duplicate/partial/null outcomes need their own execution checks; this is not an RPC method name.
- **Example:** Repeat id: greeting in the declared spec object and override object.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs[].spec.id`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.id`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.id`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.id`

</details>

<details id="protobufrunoptionspecificationcommon-importpaths">
<summary><code>importPaths</code> — Protobuf import paths</summary>

Directories searched for imported Protobuf definitions.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Selects import search directories in a matching protobuf spec override.
- **When omitted:** An empty override retains service options. A nonempty map without importPaths uses DTO/environment/property defaults rather than inheriting the outer list.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching spec override replaces the service protobuf option map. Missing option fields are not inherited; an empty override map retains the service map.
- **Watch for:** Relative paths use the main proto directory in the reviewed consumer. A successful compile alone does not prove imported message parsing succeeded. Per-spec import replacement is source-backed; the checked import recipe varies the service-level list.
- **Example:** When overriding an imported contract, repeat importPaths: ["."] and its compiler/endpoint fields.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs[].spec.importPaths`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.importPaths`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.importPaths`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.importPaths`

</details>

<details id="protobufrunoptionspecificationcommon-port">
<summary><code>port</code> — Service port</summary>

Port override for this specification.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC CLI mock/test consumers. Shared V3 schema acceptance does not establish OSS gRPC execution.
- **Effect:** Changes the selected gRPC port for a matching declared spec ID.
- **When omitted:** When the matching override map has no runtime values, the service map remains selected. Once the override has values, an omitted port does not inherit the service port; mock can fall back, while a test DTO may be unconvertible.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching per-spec option map replaces the service protobuf option map; missing fields are not inherited. An empty override map retains the service map. An explicit mock CLI port is staged afterward; a test uses its convertible selected per-spec target.
- **Watch for:** Multiple proto specs still share the mock's one selected listener config. This field does not prove independent per-spec mock listeners. Partial override outcomes remain execution-pending.
- **Example:** Give declaration and override ID greeting, port 9102 and compiler 3.23.4; test also repeats host 127.0.0.1.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs[].spec.port`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.port`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.port`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.port`

</details>

<details id="protobufrunoptionspecificationcommon-protocversion">
<summary><code>protocVersion</code> — protoc version</summary>

Version of protoc used to compile Protobuf definitions.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC CLI mock/test consumers. Shared V3 schema acceptance does not establish OSS gRPC execution.
- **Effect:** Selects the compiler version in a matching spec override.
- **When omitted:** An empty override retains service options. In a nonempty map, an omitted compiler value does not inherit the service compiler; DTO/compiler environment/property/default selection can then apply.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching per-spec option map replaces the service protobuf option map; missing fields are not inherited. An empty override map retains the service map.
- **Watch for:** Repeat a compiler version that supports your platform when adding a nonempty override. An ID names the declared spec; it is not a proto filename or RPC method.
- **Example:** The complete greeting override repeats protocVersion: "3.23.4" with its target port.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs[].spec.protocVersion`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.protocVersion`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.protocVersion`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.protocVersion`

</details>

<details id="protobufrunoptionspecificationcommon-requesttimeout">
<summary><code>requestTimeout</code> — gRPC request timeout</summary>

Maximum time in milliseconds to wait for a gRPC request.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Sets the test call deadline in milliseconds in a matching protobuf override. It is not a mock response-delay setting.
- **When omitted:** An empty override retains service options. A nonempty map without this field returns to the test DTO/settings/client 5000 ms default, not the outer deadline.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching spec override replaces the service protobuf option map. Missing option fields are not inherited; an empty override map retains the service map.
- **Watch for:** The mock DTO ignores this field. Zero/negative deadline behavior and per-spec timeout effects are not established by the service-level recovery recipe.
- **Example:** Repeat requestTimeout: 3000 with host, port and protocVersion in a complete test override.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs[].spec.requestTimeout`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.requestTimeout`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.specs[].spec.requestTimeout`

</details>

## Protobuf Test Run Options

Protobuf options configure gRPC targets/listeners, import paths, compiler version and test timeout.

Test DTO host/port are required and timeout defaults to 5000 ms. The mock service DTO does not require host. A map-based mock parser used by validation instead returns the test DTO; this is a recorded product limitation.

<details id="protobuftestrunoptions-host">
<summary><code>host</code> — gRPC service host</summary>

Host used by the gRPC test client.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC CLI mock/test consumers. Shared V3 schema acceptance does not establish OSS gRPC execution.
- **Effect:** Selects the gRPC implementation host for contract tests.
- **When omitted:** The test DTO requires host with port for successful conversion. GrpcTestSettings has a localhost fallback, but the scenario loader requires a convertible full config; always supply the complete endpoint.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching per-spec option map replaces the service protobuf option map; missing fields are not inherited. An empty override map retains the service map. A complete configured endpoint is selected before staged CLI host/port.
- **Watch for:** This client uses plaintext gRPC, not an HTTP baseUrl. No TLS settings are exposed in these protobuf options. Missing/partial targets and every integration fallback need separate execution checks. Enterprise validate --spec-file needs loadable test options including host/port, but it compiles/checks the proto and examples without connecting to that host.
- **Example:** Use host: 127.0.0.1 with port: 9100 for the independent local demo.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.host`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.host`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.host`

</details>

<details id="protobuftestrunoptions-requesttimeout">
<summary><code>requestTimeout</code> — gRPC request timeout</summary>

Maximum time in milliseconds to wait for a gRPC request.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC CLI mock/test consumers. Shared V3 schema acceptance does not establish OSS gRPC execution.
- **Effect:** Sets the gRPC call deadline in milliseconds. Use it for a slow but correct unary response.
- **When omitted:** The DTO/settings/client fall back to 5000 milliseconds. This is a request deadline, not a whole-suite or compiler timeout.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching per-spec option map replaces the service protobuf option map; missing fields are not inherited. An empty override map retains the service map. A non-null selected DTO deadline precedes GrpcTestSettings fallback; general core HTTP timeout fields do not directly replace this protocol-specific value.
- **Watch for:** Zero/negative deadlines are not a portable way to disable timeout; the gRPC deadline API receives the value. The checked failure/recovery uses a service-level deadline for one plaintext unary call; other consumers and per-spec deadline effects remain separate.
- **Example:** Use requestTimeout: 10000 beside the test host/port when the application needs a longer request deadline.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.requestTimeout`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.requestTimeout`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.requestTimeout`

</details>

<details id="protobuftestrunoptions-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specification overrides</summary>

Optional per-specification Protobuf overrides.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Lists protobuf run-option overrides keyed by declared specification IDs. It changes options, not the list of contracts to load.
- **When omitted:** Without this list or without a matching ID, the service protobuf options remain selected.
- **Precedence:** A nonempty matching spec override replaces the service protobuf option map. Missing option fields are not inherited; an empty override map retains the service map. The first matching ID is selected by the core run-option lookup.
- **Watch for:** Declare specs in definitions; do not use this list to add contract paths. Duplicate/mismatched IDs, empty maps and multiple-spec behavior require separate checks.
- **Example:** Give the declared proto ID greeting and add specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: greeting, port: 9102, protocVersion: "3.23.4"&#125;&#125;]; a test override also supplies host.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.specs`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.specs`

</details>

<details id="protobuftestrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Run mode</summary>

Test branch discriminator.

- **Value:** exactly "test".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Enterprise 1.28.3 gRPC V3 configuration consumers. Source-backed rules cover the stated use sites; checked recipes establish only the listed local unary effects.
- **Effect:** Declares test protobuf run options for the corresponding service role.
- **When omitted:** The inline role-specific model permits omission and retains its role; reusable component run options require an explicit discriminator.
- **Precedence:** A tested service requires test options; a dependency uses mock options. Type does not turn a dependency declaration into a system under test.
- **Watch for:** Use the explicit role in a copyable starter. Omitted-mode runtime behavior and every ref form are not established by the local recipe.
- **Example:** Use type: test under that service's runOptions.protobuf.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.protobuf.type`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.protobuf.type`

</details>

## Protobuf Test Run Option Specification Entry

<details id="protobuftestrunoptionspecificationentry-spec">
<summary><code>spec</code> — Protobuf test run-option override</summary>

Per-specification Protobuf test run-option values.

- **Value:** object.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Effect:** Wraps one ID-keyed override object inside the protobuf specs list.
- **When omitted:** Each authored list entry must include the spec wrapper; omit the entire list when no override is needed.
- **Precedence:** The wrapped id is matched to the declared spec before its options are selected. A nonempty matching spec override replaces the service protobuf option map. Missing option fields are not inherited; an empty override map retains the service map.
- **Watch for:** This is an override object, not a path string or another definition wrapper. Schema validity alone does not prove an ID matched.
- **Example:** Use a list item with spec: &#123;id: greeting, port: 9102, protocVersion: "3.23.4"&#125;; test also includes host.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/grpc).

- `components.runOptions.<name>.protobuf.specs[].spec`

## Proxy Definition

Proxy configuration chooses an upstream target, listener, optional mocks and recording behavior.

The common V3 proxy getter uses only the first proxy even though the schema accepts a list. Proxy adapter and certificate consumers differ from service use sites.

<details id="proxydefinition-adapters">
<summary><code>adapters</code> — Proxy adapters</summary>

Adapter hooks applied while proxying requests and responses.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `proxies[].proxy.adapters`

</details>

<details id="proxydefinition-baseurl">
<summary><code>baseUrl</code> — Proxy base URL</summary>

Base URL exposed by the local proxy.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core CLI HTTP proxy in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The recipe checks local forwarding/recording; TLS, interception extensions and other entry points are separately scoped.
- **Effect:** Selects the listening host/port for the proxy, not its forwarding destination.
- **When omitted:** The common config falls back to 0.0.0.0:9000 when no listener URL or legacy host/port is available. The recipe explicitly chooses loopback; default binding is source-backed.
- **Precedence:** CLI --host and --port override their respective components. CLI port 0 or -1 is treated as absent by this command and falls back to configured/default port.
- **Watch for:** The URL scheme alone does not configure TLS; cert/keystore controls are separate. Omitted binding is not loopback-only. Invalid URLs and other edge values need separate runtime checks.
- **Example:** Config listens at <code>http://127.0.0.1:19111</code>; CLI --port 19112 moves the listener.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/proxy).

**Placement**

- `proxies[].proxy.baseUrl`

</details>

<details id="proxydefinition-cert">
<summary><code>cert</code> — Proxy certificate</summary>

TLS certificate settings or a reference to a named certificate.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `proxies[].proxy.cert`

</details>

<details id="proxydefinition-mock">
<summary><code>mock</code> — Mock specifications</summary>

Specification identifiers or paths to mock while proxying.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `proxies[].proxy.mock`

</details>

<details id="proxydefinition-recordingsdirectory">
<summary><code>recordingsDirectory</code> — Recordings directory</summary>

Directory where proxy recordings are stored.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core CLI HTTP proxy in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The recipe checks local forwarding/recording; TLS, interception extensions and other entry points are separately scoped.
- **Effect:** Selects the output directory for inferred OpenAPI contracts and recorded JSON examples.
- **When omitted:** The common getter defaults to the current canonical directory. Source path mapping can change an input before this getter; a relocated config needs its own entry-point path check.
- **Precedence:** The positional CLI output directory wins over this config field. ProxyCommand canonicalizes the selected File before constructing the proxy.
- **Watch for:** Use a new output directory. CLI target plus existing nonempty output can be rejected by the command guard, while configured-target paths follow a different guard path. Generated files may be rewritten/extended; no arbitrary user-file preservation is claimed.
- **Example:** Configured recordings produces proxy_generated.yaml and examples; positional cli-recordings moves those files.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/proxy).

**Placement**

- `proxies[].proxy.recordingsDirectory`

</details>

<details id="proxydefinition-target">
<summary><code>target</code> — Upstream target</summary>

Required upstream target for the proxy.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core CLI HTTP proxy in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The recipe checks local forwarding/recording; TLS, interception extensions and other entry points are separately scoped.
- **Effect:** Selects the upstream base URL used for ordinary relative-path forwarding.
- **When omitted:** Required in a proxy definition. With no selected config target and no nonblank CLI target, the command errors rather than choosing an upstream.
- **Precedence:** A nonblank CLI --target wins over the selected configured target. Absolute HTTP request URLs have a separate forwarding path and are not established by this recipe.
- **Watch for:** This recipe checks a pathless local HTTP base URL and GET forwarding. CONNECT is unsupported in the reviewed Proxy runtime; TLS tunneling and target URL path/query variations are not established.
- **Example:** CLI target 19110 overrides configured nonexistent target 19113.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/proxy).

**Placement**

- `proxies[].proxy.target`

</details>

<details id="proxydefinition-timeoutinmilliseconds">
<summary><code>timeoutInMilliseconds</code> — Proxy timeout</summary>

Maximum upstream request duration in milliseconds.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core CLI HTTP proxy in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The recipe checks local forwarding/recording; TLS, interception extensions and other entry points are separately scoped.
- **Effect:** Sets the HTTP client response timeout for upstream forwarding, in milliseconds.
- **When omitted:** The common config uses 6000 ms when omitted. This is a forwarding timeout, not a lifetime for the proxy server.
- **Precedence:** CLI --timeout-in-ms wins over the selected config value.
- **Watch for:** A forwarding timeout is returned as HTTP 500 by the reviewed proxy error path. Zero/negative/null edge values and other transports need separate checks.
- **Example:** The 200 ms demo endpoint times out with config 20 ms; CLI 1000 ms succeeds.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/proxy).

**Placement**

- `proxies[].proxy.timeoutInMilliseconds`

</details>

## Proxy Entry

Proxy configuration chooses an upstream target, listener, optional mocks and recording behavior.

The common V3 proxy getter uses only the first proxy even though the schema accepts a list. Proxy adapter and certificate consumers differ from service use sites.

<details id="proxyentry-proxy">
<summary><code>proxy</code> — Proxy definition</summary>

Target, listener, adapter, certificate, and recording settings for the proxy.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core CLI HTTP proxy in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The recipe checks local forwarding/recording; TLS, interception extensions and other entry points are separately scoped.
- **Effect:** Wraps one proxy definition in the root proxies list.
- **When omitted:** Required by the wrapper schema. Omitting the whole list allows CLI-only setup, separately source-backed.
- **Precedence:** The first wrapped definition is selected by the released core CLI; the wrapper does not introduce another merge layer.
- **Watch for:** A proxy is not placed under systemUnderTest.service.runOptions. Config structure checks do not connect to its target.
- **Example:** Use proxies: [&#123;proxy: &#123;target: ..., baseUrl: ...&#125;&#125;] as a placement fragment.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/proxy).

**Placement**

- `proxies[].proxy`

</details>

## Proxy Settings

Proxy configuration chooses an upstream target, listener, optional mocks and recording behavior.

The common V3 proxy getter uses only the first proxy even though the schema accepts a list. Proxy adapter and certificate consumers differ from service use sites.

<details id="proxysettings-ignoreheaders">
<summary><code>ignoreHeaders</code> — Ignored headers</summary>

Header names excluded from proxy processing.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core CLI HTTP proxy in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The recipe checks local forwarding/recording; TLS, interception extensions and other entry points are separately scoped.
- **Effect:** Requests removal of named response headers from recorded examples. In the checked releases the tested X-Trace header remains; this field must not be relied on for redaction.
- **When omitted:** With no list, no configured response-header removal transformation is added.
- **Precedence:** This global proxy list creates recording transformations; it does not remove the original response header returned to the caller or request headers sent upstream.
- **Watch for:** Released example retains exact X-Trace: demo-trace even with ignoreHeaders: [X-Trace]. In reviewed source RemoveValue returns null and ExampleTransformer.apply returns the unchanged accumulator for a null transformation result. Use recordRequests false when recording is inappropriate, and separately account for logs. No runtime fix is included.
- **Example:** Both caller response and saved JSON still contain the demo trace header.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/proxy).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.proxy.ignoreHeaders`
- `specmatic.settings.proxy.ignoreHeaders`

</details>

<details id="proxysettings-recordrequests">
<summary><code>recordRequests</code> — Record requests</summary>

Record requests handled by the proxy.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core CLI HTTP proxy in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The recipe checks local forwarding/recording; TLS, interception extensions and other entry points are separately scoped.
- **Effect:** Controls whether forwarded interactions are added to the proxy recording collection. It does not stop forwarding.
- **When omitted:** Omitted/null model setting falls back to true in Proxy. The resolved Boolean schema rejects explicit null; omission is the supported default form.
- **Precedence:** The global specmatic.settings.proxy setting is read when the Proxy is created. This core command has no recordRequests CLI switch.
- **Watch for:** False disables recorded contract/example generation for these interactions, not console traffic logs. Dump is asynchronous; wait for completion before treating file absence as evidence.
- **Example:** False still returns the exact upstream response; completed dump reports no stubs and writes no contract/examples.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/proxy).

**Placement**

- `components.settings.<name>.proxy.recordRequests`
- `specmatic.settings.proxy.recordRequests`

</details>

## Reference

A reference identifies reusable configuration. Local component refs use #/components/&lt;collection&gt;/&lt;name&gt;.

Sibling properties replace matching properties on the referenced object with a shallow merge. Replacing a nested settings object does not preserve its omitted child fields. Internal component resolution does not support array indices or JSON Pointer escape decoding.

<details id="reference-ref">
<summary><code>$ref</code> — Reference target</summary>

Component, internal pointer, relative, or supported external reference target.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 RefOrValue use sites, local component map lookup and typed shallow sibling conversion. Released examples cover source/service references and nested service-settings replacement in core OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. External document forms and protocol consumers need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Selects a reusable value that is converted to the type required at the reference use site. A destination existing in components is not enough: it must fit that expected value. Declaring a component does not select it for a test or mock.
- **When omitted:** Use the applicable complete inline value when no reference is needed. The reference object form requires a string $ref; there is no implicit destination. Missing or null local destinations fail resolution.
- **Precedence:** Local references traverse literal slash-separated map keys under components, without pointer escape decoding or array indexes. Sibling fields shallowly replace matching top-level target properties before typed conversion; nested objects are not recursively merged. Siblings on a non-object target cannot be applied.
- **Watch for:** Choose a target with the required use-site shape. A source cannot stand in for a service requiring definitions. Relative external source files failed in both checked releases, for JSON and YAML inputs; schema acceptance does not make them runnable. The browser checks bounded local shapes without fetching resources or establishing runtime compatibility, chains or cycles.
- **Example:** Select the books service from dependencies, and its local source from the definition. Adding only settings.delayInMilliseconds beside the service reference replaces that service settings object and drops its authored strictMode, changing the checked unmatched response from HTTP 400 to generated HTTP 200.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/component-references).

**Placement**

- `components.examples.mockExamples[].$ref`
- `components.examples.testExamples[].$ref`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.cert.$ref`
- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.cert.$ref`
- `components.services.<name>.data.adapters.$ref`
- `components.services.<name>.data.dictionary.$ref`

## Reports Definition

Governance provides coverage criteria and a report output directory.

Report formats are schema-declared, but no runtime format-selection reader was confirmed. Do not rely on formats alone to select output. Coverage discovery settings belong to OpenAPI test run options.

## Root

V3 uses version, systemUnderTest, dependencies, proxies, mcp, specmatic and components. A file can configure only the operation you need.

Inline services are supported. Named components are reusable declarations; declaring a component does not automatically select it for a run.

<details id="root-components">
<summary><code>components</code> — Reusable components</summary>

Named reusable sources, services, run options, data, certificates, adapters, and settings.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Holds reusable sources, services, run options, settings, examples, dictionaries, adapters and certificates.
- **When omitted:** Inline configurations remain supported. A reference to an absent component cannot resolve.
- **Precedence:** Use an explicit supported reference at the consuming field. Declaring a component alone does not select a contract or run a service.
- **Watch for:** Component names are chosen by you. The target must have the shape expected by its use site; an existing pointer alone does not prove compatibility.
- **Example:** Declare components.sources.localContracts and reference it from a definition source.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `components`

</details>

<details id="root-dependencies">
<summary><code>dependencies</code> — Dependencies</summary>

Mocked dependency services and their optional data/settings.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Selects dependency services used by configured mock runs, with shared mock settings and data.
- **When omitted:** No dependency services are selected through this section. Test-only files can omit it.
- **Precedence:** Selection comes from dependencies.services, independently of systemUnderTest. Shared dependency settings/data contribute through their own consumers.
- **Watch for:** The section does not by itself run a mock or cause every test integration to start one. Follow the combined test/mock recipe for the actual startup sequence.
- **Example:** List a backend under dependencies.services with its service wrapper and mock run options.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `dependencies`

</details>

<details id="root-mcp">
<summary><code>mcp</code> — MCP configuration</summary>

Model Context Protocol test endpoint and transport settings.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** MCP auto testing through specmatic mcp test in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The checked transport is a local JSON streamable-HTTP endpoint; other integrations need separate verification.
- **Effect:** Configures MCP auto tests independently of service specification definitions. It does not register Specmatic as a coding-agent MCP server.
- **When omitted:** Optional root section. The command can obtain its endpoint and transport through CLI arguments without this block, but needs both effective values. That CLI-only omission case is source-backed, not executed by this recipe.
- **Precedence:** The MCP test command consumes this root section; HTTP service runOptions are not an alternative placement for these settings.
- **Watch for:** The test invokes real tools. In these releases, exit 0 means at least one scenario passed, not that every scenario passed. Use the report gate when all tests must succeed.
- **Example:** A complete file can contain only version: 3 and mcp.test for this task.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/mcp).

**Placement**

- `mcp`

</details>

<details id="root-proxies">
<summary><code>proxies</code> — Proxy definitions</summary>

Proxy definitions. Multiple proxies are currently valid; target selection is runtime behavior.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core CLI HTTP proxy in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. The recipe checks local forwarding/recording; TLS, interception extensions and other entry points are separately scoped.
- **Effect:** Supplies proxy configurations. The released core CLI consumes only the first entry, not one listener per list item.
- **When omitted:** With no configured first proxy, CLI values can supply the target/listener and the command uses common defaults. This omission case is source-backed, not executed by this recipe.
- **Precedence:** getProxyConfig selects firstOrNull; CLI scalar options then override that selected configuration. Later entries are not used by this CLI accessor.
- **Watch for:** Do not infer concurrent listeners from the list schema. Separate configs/processes are needed for separate selected setups. Other entry points need separate review.
- **Example:** Two entries declaring 19111 and 19112 start only 19111.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/proxy).

**Placement**

- `proxies`

</details>

<details id="root-specmatic">
<summary><code>specmatic</code> — Specmatic settings</summary>

Specmatic-owned license, governance, and global settings.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Groups global settings, reporting/governance and license configuration.
- **When omitted:** The accessor uses its individual defaults or non-file inputs. No governance or license file is selected by this section when absent.
- **Precedence:** Put runtime defaults under specmatic.settings; put reporting under specmatic.governance and the license path under specmatic.license.
- **Watch for:** This is not a service definition, and global settings are not a substitute for selecting specs. Each command decides which of these fields it consumes.
- **Example:** Use specmatic.settings.test for shared test defaults and service.settings for a selected service override.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `specmatic`

</details>

<details id="root-systemundertest">
<summary><code>systemUnderTest</code> — System under test</summary>

The service under test, supplied inline or by a supported reference.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Selects one service whose specifications supply configured contract-test inputs. The service can contain multiple definitions and specs.
- **When omitted:** No service is selected for testing through this section. Mock-only files can omit it. A CLI or integration may also accept explicit inputs.
- **Precedence:** Only the nested service is selected; merely declaring a component service does not add a tested service.
- **Watch for:** Declaring this section does not start an implementation. The selected test command still needs a reachable target.
- **Example:** Use systemUnderTest.service inline, or give service a $ref to #/components/services/booksGateway.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `systemUnderTest`

</details>

<details id="root-version">
<summary><code>version</code> — Configuration version</summary>

Literal V3 configuration marker; it is not template-able at the resolved-config boundary.

- **Value:** exactly 3.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Selects the configuration file format. Use the literal integer 3 for this reference.
- **When omitted:** Required for a V3 file; authoritative config validation requires a literal supported version, not an assumed V3 default.
- **Precedence:** This selects the file structure, not the installed Specmatic release or edition.
- **Watch for:** Do not template this marker or use a product release such as 2.55.3. Older formats belong in the migration guide.
- **Example:** Start the complete file with version: 3.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `version`

</details>
