## Security Scheme Configuration V3

<details id="securityschemeconfigurationv3-token">
<summary><code>token</code> — Security token</summary>

Raw credential value; prefixes and API-key locations are supplied from the contract scheme.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI CLI contract-test credential generation in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, with the separate core mock boundary demonstrated. Other integrations and authored example/dictionary interactions need independent review.
- **Effect:** Bearer/OAuth/OIDC add Bearer; Basic adds Basic to validated Base64 username:password; API keys use the declared header/query name. Config tokens do not perform token acquisition or refreshing in this setup.
- **When omitted:** The model requires token. A blank configured token is ignored during resolution and falls back to scheme-named/global lookup. Without a usable credential, generated/dictionary values may be used; a valid schema does not ensure successful authentication.
- **Precedence:** Resolve config templates first. A compatible nonblank configured token wins; otherwise use the exact scheme-named environment variable, then JVM property. Bearer/OAuth/OIDC additionally fall back to SPECMATIC_OAUTH2_TOKEN; Basic to SPECMATIC_BASIC_AUTH_TOKEN. Flags lookup uses environment before JVM property. Dictionary/generated request values can follow when no configured/environment credential exists. Example-level credentials have additional consumers; this is not universal request precedence. An empty scheme-named environment value is present and suppresses the JVM/global fallback; this differs from a blank config token being skipped.
- **Watch for:** The V3 map lookup selects systemUnderTest and matching spec ID, then exact scheme name. Schema-accepted dependency maps do not establish mock token authentication. The checked mock permits an absent bearer header and arbitrary bearer token. Cookie API keys, AND/OR requirements, authorization scopes, refresh/login, external examples/dictionaries, JVM/global fallbacks, null/missing binding cases and wider consumers remain unexecuted.
- **Example:** Use demo-bearer without Bearer; Base64 docs:demo is ZG9jczpkZW1v without Basic. A template &#123;API_TOKEN:&#125; can supply a real token without storing a secret default.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/authentication).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes.<name>.token`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes.<name>.token`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes.<name>.token`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes.<name>.token`

</details>

<details id="securityschemeconfigurationv3-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Security scheme type</summary>

Credential adapter kind; use the kind compatible with the contract security scheme.

- **Value:** "oauth2", "openIdConnect", "basicAuth", "bearer", "apiKey".
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI CLI contract-test credential generation in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, with the separate core mock boundary demonstrated. Other integrations and authored example/dictionary interactions need independent review.
- **Effect:** Converts credentials into oauth2, openIdConnect, bearer, basicAuth or apiKey adapters. Bearer/OAuth/OIDC share the OAuth-token interface; Basic and API-key consumers require their respective adapters. A mismatched valid enum can make the configured token unavailable to the consumer.
- **When omitted:** The authored model requires a non-null type and uses exact case-sensitive enum values; it does not infer the kind from token content. Missing/null values need separate released binding checks.
- **Precedence:** Resolve config templates first. A compatible nonblank configured token wins; otherwise use the exact scheme-named environment variable, then JVM property. Bearer/OAuth/OIDC additionally fall back to SPECMATIC_OAUTH2_TOKEN; Basic to SPECMATIC_BASIC_AUTH_TOKEN. Flags lookup uses environment before JVM property. Dictionary/generated request values can follow when no configured/environment credential exists. Example-level credentials have additional consumers; this is not universal request precedence. An empty scheme-named environment value is present and suppresses the JVM/global fallback; this differs from a blank config token being skipped.
- **Watch for:** The V3 map lookup selects systemUnderTest and matching spec ID, then exact scheme name. Schema-accepted dependency maps do not establish mock token authentication. The checked mock permits an absent bearer header and arbitrary bearer token. Cookie API keys, AND/OR requirements, authorization scopes, refresh/login, external examples/dictionaries, JVM/global fallbacks, null/missing binding cases and wider consumers remain unexecuted.
- **Example:** OpenAPI type http with scheme bearer uses config type bearer; OpenAPI http basic uses basicAuth. API key placement/name comes from the contract, not this config.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/authentication).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes.<name>.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes.<name>.type`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes.<name>.type`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.openapi.specs[].spec.securitySchemes.<name>.type`

</details>

## Specification Definition

A definition binds a source to specification paths. Named spec IDs connect declarations to per-spec run-option overrides.

Specs can be strings or wrapped spec objects. IDs are matching identifiers, not a global CLI alias registry. The first matching ID override is selected.

<details id="specificationdefinition-spec">
<summary><code>spec</code> — Specification override</summary>

Inline specification path and optional per-specification overrides.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 specification declaration and core OpenAPI endpoint selection. Released execution evidence covers mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; test targets and separate protocols retain their own consumer rules.
- **Effect:** Wraps the object form of a selected specification, allowing its path and optional id/urlPathPrefix. A plain string path is the simpler alternative.
- **When omitted:** A string entry needs no spec wrapper. For the object variant, the wrapper and its path are required by the resolved schema.
- **Precedence:** The wrapped id participates in exact matching to the service’s protocol overrides; the wrapper itself does not merge run options.
- **Watch for:** Keep the shape - spec: &#123;id: authors, path: authors.yaml&#125;. Placing id/path directly in the list item is not this supported object variant. urlPathPrefix has separate consumer effects not demonstrated by the port recipes.
- **Example:** Use the wrapped form to identify authors.yaml as authors while retaining its source-relative path.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec`

</details>

## Specification Object

A definition binds a source to specification paths. Named spec IDs connect declarations to per-spec run-option overrides.

Specs can be strings or wrapped spec objects. IDs are matching identifiers, not a global CLI alias registry. The first matching ID override is selected.

<details id="specificationobject-id">
<summary><code>id</code> — Specification ID</summary>

Optional specification identifier used by per-spec and run-option joins.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 specification declaration and core OpenAPI endpoint selection. Released execution evidence covers mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; test targets and separate protocols retain their own consumer rules.
- **Effect:** Identifies this selected specification for matching protocol run-option overrides. It does not register a command-line alias.
- **When omitted:** Omitting id leaves this spec without an ID-based join; a string-path declaration likewise has no ID.
- **Precedence:** Exact, case-sensitive equality selects the first matching override in that service’s protocol list. No match leaves the protocol run options in effect.
- **Watch for:** IDs need not match filenames. A mismatched or duplicate override can pass config validation while being bypassed. Do not assume IDs are globally unique across all service lists.
- **Example:** Declare id: authors beside path: authors.yaml, then use id: authors under runOptions.openapi.specs.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec.id`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec.id`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec.id`

</details>

<details id="specificationobject-path">
<summary><code>path</code> — Specification path</summary>

Resolved path or source-relative location of the specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 specification declaration and core OpenAPI endpoint selection. Released execution evidence covers mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; test targets and separate protocols retain their own consumer rules.
- **Effect:** Selects the specification file in the wrapped spec form, using the associated definition’s source.
- **When omitted:** Path is required in the resolved object variant; id is not a replacement for a filename.
- **Precedence:** The path chooses the file; id separately joins run-option overrides. The source resolves the declared path before source-entry creation and validation.
- **Watch for:** This local recipe executes beside the config. Do not infer Git/web or all consumer path bases from it; see the files-and-paths guide. Model mapPaths helpers are not proof that every entry point invokes them.
- **Example:** Set path: authors.yaml and id: authors; the two strings have distinct jobs.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec.path`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec.path`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec.path`

</details>

<details id="specificationobject-urlpathprefix">
<summary><code>urlPathPrefix</code> — URL path prefix</summary>

Optional URL path prefix applied to this specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 selected specification source URLs and core OpenAPI CLI test request routing in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Mock path rewriting and separate protocols need their own execution evidence.
- **Effect:** Appends a path prefix to this specification’s selected endpoint without editing its contract paths. Use it when the implementation mounts the API beneath a path such as /v2.
- **When omitted:** Missing, empty and slash-only prefixes add no path. The accessor trims leading/trailing slashes before deciding whether a prefix is present.
- **Precedence:** The prefix is applied after selection of the spec override or service endpoint. With a nonempty prefix but no source endpoint, the accessor falls back to SPECMATIC_BASE_URL or DEFAULT_BASE_URL before appending it. This source fallback is separate from later CLI runner target selection.
- **Watch for:** The resolved schema requires a string when supplied. A prefix already present in baseUrl is not deduplicated; do not author the same prefix twice. This field modifies source endpoint paths, not a portable promise that a mock rewrites all contract routes. Whitespace or internal slashes are not normalized by the trim-of-slashes accessor.
- **Example:** An authors target on port 9102, contract path /authors/1, and urlPathPrefix: /v2/ produces the checked test request /v2/authors/1. Books remains unchanged.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/test-spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec.urlPathPrefix`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec.urlPathPrefix`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.specs[].spec.urlPathPrefix`

</details>

Global settings or a reference to a reusable settings component.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Supplies the global concrete settings object, inline or by reference.
- **When omitted:** Uses an empty concrete settings object in the core accessor; individual settings then apply their own defaults and non-file inputs.
- **Precedence:** The global object groups general, test, mock, proxy and backwardCompatibility. Applicable service or dependency settings can override role-specific fields.
- **Watch for:** Do not put direct service-only fields at this level. A referenced target must convert to concrete settings, not merely exist.
- **Example:** Use specmatic.settings.test.timeoutInMilliseconds: 2000, or select #/components/settings/common.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

## Success Criteria Definition

Governance provides coverage criteria and a report output directory.

Report formats are schema-declared, but no runtime format-selection reader was confirmed. Do not rely on formats alone to select output. Coverage discovery settings belong to OpenAPI test run options.

<details id="successcriteriadefinition-enforce">
<summary><code>enforce</code> — Enforce success criteria</summary>

Whether failing the configured thresholds should fail the operation.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `specmatic.governance.successCriteria.enforce`

</details>

<details id="successcriteriadefinition-maxmissedoperationsinspec">
<summary><code>maxMissedOperationsInSpec</code> — Maximum missed operations</summary>

Maximum number of missed operations allowed in a specification.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `specmatic.governance.successCriteria.maxMissedOperationsInSpec`

</details>

<details id="successcriteriadefinition-mincoveragepercentage">
<summary><code>minCoveragePercentage</code> — Minimum coverage percentage</summary>

Minimum coverage percentage required for governance success.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Placement**

- `specmatic.governance.successCriteria.minCoveragePercentage`

</details>

## System Under Test Service Definition

The system under test supplies the specifications and execution settings for testing a real service.

Service settings override global test settings field by field. Run options select protocol targets; data supplies examples and dictionaries.

<details id="systemundertestservicedefinition-service">
<summary><code>service</code> — Test service</summary>

Inline test service or reusable service reference.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Supplies the tested service inline or resolves a reusable service in test context.
- **When omitted:** Required when systemUnderTest is present. Omit the outer section for a mock-only configuration.
- **Precedence:** A referenced contextual service is converted into the test service shape. Inline fields beside a service $ref shallowly replace fields from the target.
- **Watch for:** An inline settings object beside a service reference replaces that whole referenced settings field before global defaults are applied; it is not a recursive merge.
- **Example:** Use service: &#123;$ref: "#/components/services/booksGateway"&#125; under systemUnderTest.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `systemUnderTest.service`

</details>

## Test Run Options

<details id="testrunoptions-asyncapi">
<summary><code>asyncapi</code> — AsyncAPI test options</summary>

AsyncAPI options for testing the system under test.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Recorded V3 core configuration accessor behavior for OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3. These source-backed rules do not certify every protocol runner or integration.
- **Effect:** Supplies AsyncAPI execution options for the test service context.
- **When omitted:** No explicit options for this protocol are supplied by this field. Validation can construct default option objects, but that does not establish that the runner can execute without its required endpoint/broker inputs.
- **Precedence:** The service definition selects specs; the consumer selects this protocol's option branch. A service runOptions reference is converted for the consuming role. Matching per-spec overrides follow that protocol's rules.
- **Watch for:** This does not select a contract file or grant edition support. Different protocol branches may coexist for appropriate specs; do not combine test-only and mock-only fields in one branch. Follow the protocol guide for executable release/edition support.
- **Example:** Nest asyncapi under runOptions for the selected service, and supply the test variant's fields.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/async-kafka-test).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.asyncapi`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.asyncapi`

## Test Service

The system under test supplies the specifications and execution settings for testing a real service.

Service settings override global test settings field by field. Run options select protocol targets; data supplies examples and dictionaries.

<details id="testservice-data">
<summary><code>data</code> — Test data</summary>

Optional examples, dictionary, and adapter data for the test service.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Supplies selected external examples and a dictionary for the tested service. The schema also permits adapters; their execution must be established separately.
- **When omitted:** No service-level example directories or dictionary are supplied. CLI/environment example inputs and implicit discovery can still contribute through their own consumers.
- **Precedence:** The core test example accessor adds EXAMPLE_DIRECTORIES after selected service directories. The test dictionary accessor chooses the selected service dictionary before SPECMATIC_STUB_DICTIONARY. Reference-site data overrides replace the whole referenced service data field.
- **Watch for:** This is test-service data, not shared dependency data. Declaring an adapters object here does not establish that the core test runner invokes its hooks. A missing directory and an omitted data object are not interchangeable.
- **Example:** Use data.examples for selected directory groups or refs, and data.dictionary for a dictionary object or ref.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/data-selection).

**Placement**

- `systemUnderTest.service.data`

</details>

<details id="testservice-definitions">
<summary><code>definitions</code> — Service definitions</summary>

Specifications and their sources for this service.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Lists the sources and specs for this tested service. Each item has a definition wrapper.
- **When omitted:** Required in the resolved service shape. An empty list contributes no specification entries; it does not discover arbitrary files.
- **Precedence:** Inline definitions beside a service reference replace the referenced definitions list. Multiple definitions otherwise contribute their explicitly selected specs.
- **Watch for:** Source directory/repository and spec paths work together. Follow the path-resolution guide; a path-mapping helper alone does not prove a particular command uses config-relative paths.
- **Example:** Use definitions: [&#123;definition: &#123;source: &#123;filesystem: &#123;directory: .&#125;&#125;, specs: [books.yaml]&#125;&#125;].

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions`

</details>

<details id="testservice-description">
<summary><code>description</code> — Service description</summary>

Optional human-readable description of the service.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Adds a human-readable description to this tested service.
- **When omitted:** Optional; no description is supplied. This field does not select a target, spec or mock.
- **Precedence:** An inline description beside a service reference replaces the component description.
- **Watch for:** Treat this as descriptive metadata. It is not a service ID, a spec ID or a guarantee that a runner will display the text.
- **Example:** Use description: Books backend; choose spec IDs separately in the definition spec entries.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/local-openapi).

**Placement**

- `systemUnderTest.service.description`

</details>

<details id="testservice-runoptions">
<summary><code>runOptions</code> — Test run options</summary>

Optional test run options or a reference to reusable options.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Selects protocol-specific execution options for this tested service, such as an OpenAPI test target or mock listener.
- **When omitted:** No service-level options are supplied. Core HTTP consumers can use their defaults or CLI inputs; other protocols may require explicit options.
- **Precedence:** A referenced options object is converted for the consuming test/mock role. A matching per-spec ID can select overrides with protocol-specific fallback rules.
- **Watch for:** These options do not select specs: definitions does that. Inline runOptions beside a service reference replaces the whole referenced runOptions field; do not assume recursive merging.
- **Example:** For a test service use runOptions.openapi.type: test and baseUrl; for a dependency use type: mock and port.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/spec-overrides).

**Placement**

- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions`

</details>

<details id="testservice-settings">
<summary><code>settings</code> — Test settings</summary>

Optional test settings or a reference to them.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 structure and core configuration accessors for the recorded OSS 2.55.3 / Enterprise 1.28.3 baseline. Separate protocol runners and integrations need their own setup checks.
- **Effect:** Applies test or mock behavior settings to this tested service; reusable component settings acquire their role at the use site.
- **When omitted:** No service override is supplied. Test accessors fall back to global test defaults; mock accessors can also inherit dependencies.settings.
- **Precedence:** Resolve any service reference and its shallow overrides first. Then the selected service settings merge field by field with the applicable wider settings.
- **Watch for:** The service object takes role-specific settings directly, such as timeoutInMilliseconds for a test; it does not use the global test/mock wrapper. Runtime precedence still depends on the command and individual field.
- **Example:** Put settings: &#123;timeoutInMilliseconds: 2000&#125; on a tested service, or settings: &#123;strictMode: true&#125; on a mock dependency.

[See the complete configuration guide](/references/configuration/test-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `systemUnderTest.service.settings`

</details>

## Test Settings

Service test settings override specmatic.settings.test field by field.

Do not assume every CLI flag replaces config: HTTP reporting combines staged/configured filters with AND. schemaResiliencyTests also accepts resiliencyTests as a binding alias.

<details id="testsettings-junitreportdir">
<summary><code>junitReportDir</code> — JUnit report directory</summary>

Directory where JUnit reports are written.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** JUnit XML reports from the core CLI test command. Other report formats and direct JUnit launchers have separate output configuration.
- **Effect:** Chooses the directory for the core CLI JUnit XML report. Use it when CI needs a predictable location for the test-results artifact.
- **When omitted:** The V3 accessor returns no configured report directory. Without the CLI flag either, the core command does not request this XML report; governance/report settings are separate.
- **Precedence:** The core CLI --junitReportDir value wins over the service setting, which wins over the global specmatic.settings.test setting. This accessor has no environment/property fallback.
- **Watch for:** The ordinary core CLI uses a relative report directory from the process working directory, including when the value comes from a nested config file. Path-rebasing tools can transform this field through mapPaths; ordinary config loading does not perform that rebasing. Keep the output directory writable. This key does not select HTML, CTRF or every report type.
- **Example:** junitReportDir: ./build/test-results writes TEST-junit-jupiter.xml under that configured directory when running through the core CLI.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/test-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.junitReportDir`
- `components.settings.<name>.junitReportDir`
- `components.settings.<name>.test.junitReportDir`
- `specmatic.settings.test.junitReportDir`
- `systemUnderTest.service.settings.junitReportDir`

</details>

<details id="testsettings-lenientmode">
<summary><code>lenientMode</code> — Lenient mode</summary>

Allow lenient validation behavior during tests.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI parsing and example loading. CLI and JUnit entry points differ; do not transfer this setting to other protocol parsers.
- **Effect:** Allows selected parser/collector failures to be reported while loading continues. It does not make every invalid contract usable or disable response checks during test execution.
- **When omitted:** The accessor falls back to SPECMATIC_TEST_LENIENT_MODE from the environment, then the JVM property. Core parsing uses false for an absent value. One external-example failure gate specifically checks for explicit false, so an unresolved null is not identical to false in every path.
- **Precedence:** A non-null tested-service value overrides the global specmatic.settings.test value field by field. Missing fields continue to inherit; explicit false is a value, not an instruction to fall back. Without staged modes, config wins over SPECMATIC_TEST_LENIENT_MODE. The core CLI stages false when --lenient is omitted, overriding a true config value; use --lenient to enable it for that core CLI run. A JUnit invocation without a staged mode can use the config value.
- **Watch for:** Leniency only applies to specific recovery paths. Partial failures may already be logged and returned regardless of this flag, and later example validation can still fail. Keep the original error visible and fix the contract where possible.
- **Example:** For core CLI parsing leniency, use --lenient even if the file contains lenientMode: true. A direct JUnit configuration without a mode override reads the configured value.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.lenientMode`
- `components.settings.<name>.lenientMode`
- `components.settings.<name>.test.lenientMode`
- `specmatic.settings.test.lenientMode`
- `systemUnderTest.service.settings.lenientMode`

</details>

<details id="testsettings-maxtestcount">
<summary><code>maxTestCount</code> — Maximum test count</summary>

Maximum number of tests to execute.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core JUnit contract-test execution. This note does not assert a suite-wide limit for separate Enterprise protocol runners.
- **Effect:** Runs at most the selected number of otherwise executable scenarios. Subsequent executable scenarios are marked skipped with MAX_TEST_COUNT_EXCEEDED. Scenarios already classified as non-executable do not consume this count.
- **When omitted:** A missing configuration value falls back to MAX_TEST_COUNT from the environment, then the JVM property. If all are absent, the JUnit runner leaves the scenario sequence uncapped.
- **Precedence:** A non-null tested-service value wins over the global specmatic.settings.test value, then MAX_TEST_COUNT from the environment, then the JVM property. Other test settings continue to inherit independently.
- **Watch for:** At this runner's execution-selection step, 0 or a negative value skips every executable scenario; it does not mean unlimited. Use omission for no cap, and a positive integer for a useful cap. Invalid numeric environment/property text fails conversion.
- **Example:** With maxTestCount: 1, the first executable scenario runs and later executable scenarios are skipped. Increasing maxTestRequestCombinations does not override this execution limit.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/test-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.maxTestCount`
- `components.settings.<name>.maxTestCount`
- `components.settings.<name>.test.maxTestCount`
- `specmatic.settings.test.maxTestCount`
- `systemUnderTest.service.settings.maxTestCount`

</details>

<details id="testsettings-maxtestrequestcombinations">
<summary><code>maxTestRequestCombinations</code> — Maximum request combinations</summary>

Maximum request combinations generated for a test.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core schema-based request generation used by HTTP contract testing. Protocol DTOs and other runners need their own applicability review.
- **Effect:** Caps the candidate combinations produced by the core request-pattern combination generator. Use it to bound the expansion of requests with multiple candidate values. This is not a cap on the total number of contract tests.
- **When omitted:** The configuration accessor falls back to MAX_TEST_REQUEST_COMBINATIONS, first from the environment then the JVM property. If all are absent, core generation uses Int.MAX_VALUE (2,147,483,647), effectively no smaller configured cap.
- **Precedence:** A non-null service value wins over the global specmatic.settings.test value; the selected config value wins over MAX_TEST_REQUEST_COMBINATIONS from the environment and JVM property. Settings merge field by field, so overriding this field does not discard other global test settings.
- **Watch for:** Choose a positive integer. The resolved schema's integer type alone does not establish a usable limit: CombinationSpec rejects values below 1 when generation reaches it. Invalid numeric environment/property text throws during numeric conversion; it is not silently replaced with the default.
- **Example:** Use 20 to limit combination expansion for requests with many candidate values. Several operations or examples can still produce more than 20 tests overall; use maxTestCount for an execution cap.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.maxTestRequestCombinations`
- `components.settings.<name>.maxTestRequestCombinations`
- `components.settings.<name>.test.maxTestRequestCombinations`
- `specmatic.settings.test.maxTestRequestCombinations`
- `systemUnderTest.service.settings.maxTestRequestCombinations`

</details>

<details id="testsettings-parallelism">
<summary><code>parallelism</code> — Test parallelism</summary>

Parallel execution setting for generated tests.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core CLI scheduling of JUnit contract tests. Merely reading a config in a custom JUnit integration does not apply the CLI scheduler setup.
- **Effect:** Selects automatic or fixed JUnit parallel execution in the core CLI. Use auto for the dynamic strategy, or a quoted positive integer for a fixed worker count.
- **When omitted:** Falls back to SPECMATIC_TEST_PARALLELISM from the environment, then the JVM property. With no value, the core CLI leaves JUnit parallel-execution properties unchanged; external JUnit configuration may still affect scheduling.
- **Precedence:** Service configuration overrides global configuration, then the environment and JVM fallback. The selected value is applied to JUnit execution properties by the core CLI before execution.
- **Watch for:** This is a string field: use "4", not an unquoted YAML integer. The CLI accepts auto or parses an integer; positive values are needed for useful fixed parallelism. Do not use an empty string, zero or a negative value to request serial execution. It controls test execution, not source fetching or all protocol workers.
- **Example:** parallelism: "4" enables the fixed strategy with four workers. Omit the key and its fallback inputs when you want the CLI to leave JUnit scheduling configuration alone.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.parallelism`
- `components.settings.<name>.parallelism`
- `components.settings.<name>.test.parallelism`
- `specmatic.settings.test.parallelism`
- `systemUnderTest.service.settings.parallelism`

</details>

<details id="testsettings-schemaresiliencytests">
<summary><code>schemaResiliencyTests</code> — Schema resiliency tests</summary>

Select all, positive-only, or no schema resiliency tests.

- **Value:** "all", "none", "positiveOnly".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.
- **Also accepted:** `resiliencyTests`.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core schema-based positive/negative contract-test generation. MockSettings has a separate similarly named key; this note covers TestSettings.
- **Effect:** Selects all positive and negative schema-derived tests, positiveOnly, or none. Use it to expand beyond authored example tests. none turns off this generation mode; authored tests can still run.
- **When omitted:** The accessor derives a suite from SPECMATIC_GENERATIVE_TESTS and ONLY_POSITIVE, each read from the environment before the JVM property. SPECMATIC_GENERATIVE_TESTS=true selects all; otherwise ONLY_POSITIVE=true selects positiveOnly; otherwise the suite is none.
- **Precedence:** An explicit staged JUnit generative mode can enable/disable resiliency ahead of config. Otherwise a non-null service value overrides the global test value, then flag-derived selection. An explicit none suppresses those flag fallbacks.
- **Watch for:** resiliencyTests is an accepted alias under this V3 TestSettings object. Prefer schemaResiliencyTests and supply only one spelling. More generated requests can require a dictionary, matching authentication and application handling of invalid inputs; selecting all is not proof those prerequisites are met.
- **Example:** schemaResiliencyTests: positiveOnly generates positive schema variations. Switch to all when you also want invalid-input scenarios; choose none for authored example tests without that expansion.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.resiliencyTests`
- `components.services.<name>.settings.schemaResiliencyTests`
- `components.settings.<name>.resiliencyTests`
- `components.settings.<name>.schemaResiliencyTests`
- `components.settings.<name>.test.resiliencyTests`
- `components.settings.<name>.test.schemaResiliencyTests`
- `specmatic.settings.test.resiliencyTests`
- `specmatic.settings.test.schemaResiliencyTests`
- `systemUnderTest.service.settings.resiliencyTests`
- `systemUnderTest.service.settings.schemaResiliencyTests`

</details>

<details id="testsettings-strictmode">
<summary><code>strictMode</code> — Strict mode</summary>

Enable strict validation behavior during tests.

- **Value:** boolean.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core OpenAPI contract loading, dictionary selection and test generation. Separate protocol runners require their own review.
- **Effect:** Requires usable example data in strict-sensitive paths. For example, a named response example without a matching request example fails when the operation can have a named request example; dictionary values present at a JSON path must match its schema.
- **When omitted:** The accessor falls back to SPECMATIC_TEST_STRICT_MODE from the environment, then the JVM property. Core contract loading uses false if none supplies a value.
- **Precedence:** A non-null tested-service value overrides the global specmatic.settings.test value field by field. Missing fields continue to inherit; explicit false is a value, not an instruction to fall back. An explicitly supplied core CLI --strict or staged JUnit mode is applied to the service ahead of config. Otherwise config wins over the environment/property fallback.
- **Watch for:** Strictness is not a universal response-validation toggle. It is used by several example, substitution and dictionary paths. strictMode and lenientMode are independent settings; neither silently negates the other.
- **Example:** If the contract has a named response example but lacks the corresponding request example for a parameterized operation, strictMode: true can fail loading rather than generating missing request data. Supply the matching request example to correct the contract.

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.strictMode`
- `components.settings.<name>.strictMode`
- `components.settings.<name>.test.strictMode`
- `specmatic.settings.test.strictMode`
- `systemUnderTest.service.settings.strictMode`

</details>

<details id="testsettings-timeoutinmilliseconds">
<summary><code>timeoutInMilliseconds</code> — Test timeout</summary>

Maximum request duration in milliseconds.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** Core HTTP contract testing through the CLI/JUnit runner in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Other protocol-specific timeout fields have separate behavior.
- **Effect:** Sets the HTTP test request timeout in milliseconds. Use it when a correct API response takes longer than the normal request timeout; it does not set a timeout for the whole test suite.
- **When omitted:** When neither configuration nor SPECMATIC_TEST_TIMEOUT supplies a value, the core JUnit contract-test runner uses 6,000 milliseconds. This is a runner default, not a default inserted into the YAML.
- **Precedence:** An explicit core CLI --timeout-in-ms wins over --timeout (seconds). That CLI value is applied to the tested service. Otherwise a non-null service setting wins over the global specmatic.settings.test value, then SPECMATIC_TEST_TIMEOUT from the environment, then the JVM property of the same name, then the runner default.
- **Watch for:** Omission and explicit null fall back during model merging; the resolved schema rejects null for this integer field. A quoted number or empty string is not an integer. Do not use 0 as a portable way to disable timeouts: transport behavior is separate from this setting's fallback rules.
- **Example:** Set the global value to 10000 and a service value to 2500: that service uses 2500 ms. A core CLI --timeout-in-ms 8000 then selects 8000 ms for that run.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/test-settings-guide).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.settings.timeoutInMilliseconds`
- `components.settings.<name>.test.timeoutInMilliseconds`
- `components.settings.<name>.timeoutInMilliseconds`
- `specmatic.settings.test.timeoutInMilliseconds`
- `systemUnderTest.service.settings.timeoutInMilliseconds`

</details>

## Web Source Options

A web source downloads specifications from a nonblank base URL.

The source has a local cache. Structural browser checks cannot establish remote availability or validate downloaded content.

<details id="websourceoptions-url">
<summary><code>url</code> — Source URL</summary>

Remote specification source URL.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Sets the HTTP(S) specification-download base. The checked resolver joins the base and each relative spec path with a slash; it does not set an API target.
- **When omitted:** Required for a selected web source. Omission or a blank value is invalid; no environment fallback is implicit in this field. Use an authored template if variation is needed.
- **Precedence:** The base keeps its path even when a spec starts with a slash. One trailing slash on the base and one leading slash on the spec are removed by the join. Absolute spec URIs are rejected.
- **Watch for:** The loader writes under the config directory’s .specmatic/web and downloads during loading. The local-path calculation uses host/base path/spec path, omitting port/query; it is not a unique origin key or immutable/offline cache guarantee. These examples do not verify authenticated hosts, redirects or every query/fragment form.
- **Example:** Base <code>http://127.0.0.1:9200/contracts</code> and spec books.yaml fetch /contracts/books.yaml. The checked /books.yaml spec still fetches that same base path.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/contract-management).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.web.url`
- `components.sources.<name>.web.url`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.web.url`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.web.url`

</details>

## Web Source Schema

A web source downloads specifications from a nonblank base URL.

The source has a local cache. Structural browser checks cannot establish remote availability or validate downloaded content.

<details id="websourceschema-web">
<summary><code>web</code> — Web source options</summary>

Remote URL settings for this web source.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Required in this object variant; alternative branches can have different requirements.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 source selection and the traced core CLI consumers. The linked executed recipes cover OpenAPI mocks in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3; separate protocol/integration behavior and private transports require their own verification.
- **Effect:** Chooses downloading specs from a web base URL. The selected specs are appended to its url, and the downloaded files feed the operation.
- **When omitted:** Web is optional only as a provider choice. When selected, its url must be present and nonblank; there is no default specification host.
- **Precedence:** Mutually exclusive with filesystem and git. This source URL is independent of the tested API URL or mock listener.
- **Watch for:** The V3 web provider uses ResolvedWebSource, not the legacy direct-URL downloader. Its append and cache-path rules must not be inferred from generic URI resolution.
- **Example:** Use web: &#123;url: <code>http://127.0.0.1:9200/contracts</code>&#125; with specs: [books.yaml] to load the demonstrated spec.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/contract-management).

**Placement**

- `components.services.<name>.definitions[].definition.source.web`
- `components.sources.<name>.web`
- `dependencies.services[].service.definitions[].definition.source.web`
- `systemUnderTest.service.definitions[].definition.source.web`

</details>

## Wsdl Mock Run Options

Mock run options choose the listener origin; OpenAPI also supports stateful-mock.

baseUrl takes precedence over host/port. A configured certificate selects HTTPS. OpenAPI logMode/logsDirPath are schema-declared, but no runtime reader was confirmed in the source snapshot.

<details id="wsdlmockrunoptions-baseurl">
<summary><code>baseUrl</code> — Mock base URL</summary>

Base URL on which the mock service listens.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Sets the complete mock listener origin.
- **When omitted:** When absent, host/port supplies an origin only if port is present; otherwise this model supplies no origin.
- **Precedence:** A matching per-spec origin wins over this service origin. Within this object, non-null baseUrl wins over host and port.
- **Watch for:** In OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, relative local WSDL paths can miss the mock endpoint lookup and use the CLI listener instead. The linked recipe supplies an absolute physical source directory and verifies configured routing. Use a complete URL. Omission does not make an empty string a valid URL.
- **Example:** baseUrl: <code>http://127.0.0.1:9101</code>, with competing port: 9102, still selects 9101.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.baseUrl`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.baseUrl`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.baseUrl`

</details>

<details id="wsdlmockrunoptions-cert">
<summary><code>cert</code> — Mock certificate</summary>

TLS certificate settings or a reference to a named certificate.

- **Value:** object.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Supplies inline or referenced HTTPS key-store/mTLS configuration for the selected WSDL service.
- **When omitted:** No cert means no WSDL-specific certificate registry entry. The host/port model uses http without cert and https when cert is present.
- **Precedence:** The service certificate is registered against each selected specification origin, preferring its matching per-spec origin. An explicit baseUrl determines its own scheme.
- **Watch for:** Certificate resolution and registration are source-backed; SOAP TLS/mTLS execution remains pending. The cert path-mapping helper does not prove every entry point invokes it. In OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, relative local WSDL paths can miss the mock endpoint lookup and use the CLI listener instead. The linked recipe supplies an absolute physical source directory and verifies configured routing.
- **Example:** cert: &#123;$ref: "#/components/certificates/soapCertificate"&#125; refers to an authored certificate component; this is a placement fragment, not a verified TLS recipe.

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.cert`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.cert`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.cert`

</details>

<details id="wsdlmockrunoptions-host">
<summary><code>host</code> — Mock host</summary>

Host on which the mock service listens.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Sets the hostname or bind address used with port.
- **When omitted:** The model uses 0.0.0.0 when port is supplied and host is omitted. Host alone supplies no origin if both baseUrl and port are absent.
- **Precedence:** baseUrl bypasses host/port. A matching per-spec origin replaces the service origin.
- **Watch for:** In OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, relative local WSDL paths can miss the mock endpoint lookup and use the CLI listener instead. The linked recipe supplies an absolute physical source directory and verifies configured routing.
- **Example:** host: 127.0.0.1 with port: 9101 selects a local listener.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.host`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.host`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.host`

</details>

<details id="wsdlmockrunoptions-port">
<summary><code>port</code> — Mock port</summary>

Port on which the mock service listens.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Sets the listener port when using host/port.
- **When omitted:** No port value is inserted by the model. Without port or baseUrl, it supplies no configured origin; any later listener/test fallback belongs to the runner.
- **Precedence:** baseUrl wins over port. A matching per-spec origin wins over the service host/port.
- **Watch for:** In OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, relative local WSDL paths can miss the mock endpoint lookup and use the CLI listener instead. The linked recipe supplies an absolute physical source directory and verifies configured routing. The integer schema does not establish that every integer is a usable TCP port.
- **Example:** port: 9101

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.port`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.port`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.port`

</details>

<details id="wsdlmockrunoptions-specs">
<summary><code>specs</code> — Specification overrides</summary>

Optional WSDL per-specification overrides.

- **Value:** array.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Lists endpoint overrides for individual specifications identified by their authored spec IDs.
- **When omitted:** No list means no per-spec endpoint override. An empty list has no matching entries.
- **Precedence:** The first entry with an exactly matching ID is selected. A usable origin from that entry wins over the service origin; an entry without an origin falls back.
- **Watch for:** Each entry wraps its values in spec. This list does not load WSDL files; definitions selects contracts. WSDL overrides do not provide the OpenAPI overlay fields.
- **Example:** specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: greeting, baseUrl: <code>http://127.0.0.1:9102</code>&#125;&#125;]

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.specs`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.specs`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.specs`

</details>

<details id="wsdlmockrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Mock mode</summary>

Mock branch discriminator when this object is directly typed.

- **Value:** exactly "mock".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Declares this inline WSDL run option as mock.
- **When omitted:** The selected-service model accepts an omitted type because the enclosing service role selects mock; reusable run-options components need their discriminator.
- **Precedence:** An explicit type must agree with the service role; it does not change a tested service into a dependency or the reverse.
- **Watch for:** Only the appropriate enum is accepted for this typed model. Use the matching service section and mode.
- **Example:** type: mock

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.type`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.type`

</details>

## Wsdl Run Option Specification

<details id="wsdlrunoptionspecification-baseurl">
<summary><code>baseUrl</code> — Service base URL</summary>

Base URL override for this specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Sets the complete endpoint for the matching WSDL specification.
- **When omitted:** Without baseUrl, the override needs port to supply an origin. Otherwise the service origin remains selected.
- **Precedence:** Within the matching entry, baseUrl wins over host/port. Its complete origin wins over the service origin.
- **Watch for:** This is an origin override, not field-by-field inheritance. In OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, relative local WSDL paths can miss the mock endpoint lookup and use the CLI listener instead. The linked recipe supplies an absolute physical source directory and verifies configured routing.
- **Example:** baseUrl: <code>http://127.0.0.1:9102</code>

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.specs[].spec.baseUrl`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.baseUrl`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.baseUrl`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.baseUrl`

</details>

<details id="wsdlrunoptionspecification-host">
<summary><code>host</code> — Service host</summary>

Host override for this specification.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Sets the hostname/bind address of a matching host/port override.
- **When omitted:** With an override port, omitted host uses localhost for testing and 0.0.0.0 for mocking; it does not inherit the service host. Without an override port/baseUrl, host alone supplies no override origin.
- **Precedence:** Override baseUrl wins. Otherwise the override constructs a new origin from its host, caller default and port.
- **Watch for:** The WSDL per-spec host/port helper constructs an http origin. This note does not establish a per-spec HTTPS recipe. In OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, relative local WSDL paths can miss the mock endpoint lookup and use the CLI listener instead. The linked recipe supplies an absolute physical source directory and verifies configured routing.
- **Example:** spec: &#123;id: greeting, host: 127.0.0.1, port: 9102&#125;

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.specs[].spec.host`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.host`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.host`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.host`

</details>

<details id="wsdlrunoptionspecification-id">
<summary><code>id</code> — Specification ID</summary>

Specification identifier this override targets.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Matches this override to the ID of a loaded specification.
- **When omitted:** No ID means this entry cannot match a non-null specification ID. Define an explicit ID at both the contract declaration and override.
- **Precedence:** Matching is exact and the first matching entry wins; the override does not infer its target from a file path.
- **Watch for:** Duplicate IDs make the first-match behavior significant. The recipe verifies a matching ID; duplicate and missing-ID outcomes here are source-backed.
- **Example:** Use id: greeting in definitions[].definition.specs[].spec and runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.specs[].spec.id`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.id`
- `dependencies.services[].service.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.id`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.id`

</details>

<details id="wsdlrunoptionspecification-port">
<summary><code>port</code> — Service port</summary>

Port override for this specification.

- **Value:** integer.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Sets the port of a matching host/port override.
- **When omitted:** Without override port or baseUrl, no override origin is supplied; the service endpoint remains eligible. The service port is not copied into a partial override.
- **Precedence:** Override baseUrl wins over this port. A usable override origin wins over the service origin.
- **Watch for:** Choose a usable TCP port. Source-backed host/port rules are distinct from the released per-spec baseUrl execution check. In OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3, relative local WSDL paths can miss the mock endpoint lookup and use the CLI listener instead. The linked recipe supplies an absolute physical source directory and verifies configured routing.
- **Example:** spec: &#123;id: greeting, host: 127.0.0.1, port: 9102&#125;

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.specs[].spec.port`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.port`

- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.specs[].spec.port`

Per-specification WSDL run-option values.

## Wsdl Test Run Options

Test run options choose the service endpoint and protocol-specific behavior.

A per-spec origin overrides the service origin. baseUrl takes precedence over host/port; host alone does not establish an origin. A certificate reference supplies TLS material.

<details id="wsdltestrunoptions-baseurl">
<summary><code>baseUrl</code> — Service base URL</summary>

Base URL used when sending test requests.

- **Value:** string.
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Sets the complete origin of the implementation to test.
- **When omitted:** When absent, host/port supplies an origin only if port is present; otherwise this model supplies no origin.
- **Precedence:** A matching per-spec origin wins over this service origin. Within this object, non-null baseUrl wins over host and port.
- **Watch for:** This endpoint selects the running implementation; Specmatic test does not start it. CLI/environment fallback when no configured endpoint exists is a separate runner decision. Use a complete URL. Omission does not make an empty string a valid URL.
- **Example:** baseUrl: <code>http://127.0.0.1:9100</code>, with competing port: 9102, still selects 9100.

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.baseUrl`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.baseUrl`

- **When omitted:** No list means no per-spec endpoint override. An empty list has no matching entries.
- **Precedence:** The first entry with an exactly matching ID is selected. A usable origin from that entry wins over the service origin; an entry without an origin falls back.
- **Watch for:** Each entry wraps its values in spec. This list does not load WSDL files; definitions selects contracts. WSDL overrides do not provide the OpenAPI overlay fields.
- **Example:** specs: [&#123;spec: &#123;id: greeting, baseUrl: <code>http://127.0.0.1:9102</code>&#125;&#125;]

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.specs`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.specs`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.specs`

</details>

<details id="wsdltestrunoptions-type">
<summary><code>type</code> — Run mode</summary>

Test branch discriminator when this object is directly typed.

- **Value:** exactly "test".
- **Requirement:** Not directly required by this property’s parent; enclosing alternatives may impose conditions.

**Runtime behavior**

- **Applies to:** V3 local SOAP 1.1 configuration in OSS 2.55.3 and Enterprise 1.28.3. Reusable components affect a run only when selected by a service.
- **Effect:** Declares this inline WSDL run option as test.
- **When omitted:** The selected-service model accepts an omitted type because the enclosing service role selects test; reusable run-options components need their discriminator.
- **Precedence:** An explicit type must agree with the service role; it does not change a tested service into a dependency or the reverse.
- **Watch for:** Only the appropriate enum is accepted for this typed model. Use the matching service section and mode.
- **Example:** type: test

[Try the complete tested example](/references/configuration/wsdl).

**Placement**

- `components.runOptions.<name>.wsdl.type`
- `components.services.<name>.runOptions.wsdl.type`
- `systemUnderTest.service.runOptions.wsdl.type`

</details>
